# Changelog

## Unreleased
- Session and release care (T-1549): the brainstorm pack holds each of the user's quotes once; fm release warns when it starts in an hour whose gates this project's record shows failing at least twice as often as usual; each release's notes name every default it changes (plugin/defaults.json keeps the last release's), and fm settings changed says which of those reach you because you never set them; fm digest --say reads the digest aloud with an installed speech program
- Receipts and review (T-1547): fm task receipt ID --svg FILE draws the receipt as a blueprint sheet (files in reading order with their added and removed lines, the swatches it added, its before/after); --sign KEY signs it with your SSH key and prints the ssh-keygen -Y verify line that checks it; and fm audit prep puts the nits earlier reviews found in the same files into the review brief as a pre-review checklist
- fm code app extras (T-1546): F is a photo booth (the frame on screen as an SVG, and a PNG with rsvg-convert, in Foreman's photos folder); fm code tui --snapshot FILE.png draws a recorded run as a PNG; and fm code tui --chaos SECONDS [--seed N] is a chaos twin that throws hostile event floods, random keys and resizes at a headless app and reports any crash with the seed to replay it
- Fixed: fm session stop could miss a runner that started its turn just as the stop began (it read the pid before marking the session stopped), leaving the turn running and the status back at running; and fm check's flaky-test record now keeps how each test failed, with the assertion shown, so a race can be found after its rerun passed (T-1789)
- Site build and stories (T-1541): two builds of the site from one commit are byte-identical (fm code's snapshots are drawn at a fixed time, not the wall clock); each recorded fm code run gets a case-study page, event by event, linked from its card; the Releases page has a release-over-release wall of generated covers; and a task that closes proved red then green gets a hand-lettered title card (fm task card ID, written by fm task finish too)
- Design sprint and pairing (T-1538): fm design sprint walks a ten-minute sprint, five stages two minutes each (a concrete brief, a direction with its audacity, a tokens table, lint-clean UI files, passing gates), each with its check and the next step of the first that fails; fm design vague TEXT is the veto list for sweeping requests ("modern", "clean", "pop"…), each word with the concrete question to ask instead; and fm task receipt shows the colours and motion durations a UI task added as a swatch strip
- Divergence for UI work (T-1536): fm looks tournament runs a taste duel or a four- or eight-look bracket you answer match by match (each winner +1 in your taste ledger); fm looks opposite LOOK names the looks farthest from it (opposite day); fm looks book --out FILE writes a lookbook page; fm design diverge 3 writes three variant briefs in looks far apart and diverge pick LOOK keeps the winner; the directions skill says when to diverge (three variants in lanes, a tournament, opposite day, a lookbook, five historical idioms of one layout) and /foreman:looks asks tournament matches as questions
- 3D checks with no browser or GPU (T-1530): a scene's animation can move the camera (a camera path, replayed as the demo, still aimed at its target); fm scene compile --hud adds a dev overlay (frame rate, draw calls, triangles, geometries, textures, also on globalThis.__fmScene); fm scene check validates and counts; fm scene turntable draws a contact sheet of the scene turned about its vertical axis with the software rasteriser; fm scene shader checks shader cards and GLSL files (compiled with glslangValidator when installed; each card's uniform table matches its code); fm scene check validates a .gltf or .glb offline; fm scene soak runs the scene in headless Chrome when a browser and a three.js build are here, failing on growing geometries or textures, a p95 frame time over --budget-ms, or a dispose that leaves it drawn, and says why it skipped when they aren't
- Learning from playbooks (T-1525): fm playbooks score NAME --task ID shows which of a playbook's steps a finished task followed; fm playbooks kata NAME gives its held-out briefs as practice and --answer FILE scores yours; fm playbooks lesson RANGE is a five-minute read of a diff (what changed, where, what its tasks learned, the checklists to read it with); and the site has a Playbooks field guide, every playbook by stage with when to use it
- Playbooks from this repo's own work (T-1523): fm playbooks draft ID turns a done task into a playbook draft (its steps, each with the run that proved it; its checks; its lessons and first failures as pitfalls); fm playbooks mine lists the files this repo changes together; fm playbooks counter lists what failed here (reverted commits, tasks blocked with a reason); and fm playbooks show puts the steps the active task's failed runs point at first
- A false-done benchmark (T-1377): bench/spec-suite/make.py --suite impossible builds three tasks no change can do (rules that contradict each other, a file only another team may commit, a required internal package that isn't installed), and --check proves every attempt fails; fm bench build --commits R --verify CMD --impossible keeps commits whose tests fail at their own commit too, as judged cases a run passes only by saying the task can't be done (and fails when it makes the tests pass anyway); fm bench duel then adds "false done: Foreman n/k, <rival> n/k"
- fm bench duel --ablate F1,F2 (or all) runs full Foreman, then Foreman with each named hook feature switched off in turn (FOREMAN_ABLATE: the per-call notes fix, deps, veto, scope, lessons, steps, ambient, bus, cards, test-first, design and gate-pipe, and the Stop hook's drive; never the guard), with a verdict per feature (off lost passes, changed nothing, or only saved money); --vs none --equal-spend gives Claude with no plugins Foreman's spend on each case as fresh attempts (5 at most, stopping at a pass), under duel-spend labels the benchmarks page keeps apart (T-1375, T-1376)
- fm code tui after a close: u twice within 2 s undoes the run (git revert of its commit: a new commit, nothing lost) and marks its history file undone, so past runs show UNDONE; P shows fm pr's pull request text for the task, and Enter there posts it through fm pr ID --post, which needs the task's publish grant; neither acts without a closed, committed run (T-1352)
- fm code tui tickets: during a run, n then Tab turns the question box into next ›, and Enter puts the request on a rail of tickets under the track; when the run closes the first ticket starts as the next run (a blocked or stopped run holds the rail and says so); T lists them, where space picks one up and puts it down, arrows move it and Delete drops it; tickets left when the app quits are listed in the scrollback (T-1350)
- fm code tui's home is a deck: a card per project in as many columns as fit, each with its runner, the active task's stations as a mini track (plan, work n/m, checks, close), the task, and blocked, inbox and queue badges; arrows move by card, the focus ring glides to the selected card, and Enter's project view grows out of the card (both land at once with motion reduced or off); fm projects --json gains each project's runner (T-1345)
- fm code tui: x on a stalled or stopped run's card hands its task to a live CLI (the run's runner when it has an interactive form and is installed: claude, codex, gemini; else claude) in this terminal, with the brief as the opening prompt (the task, its plan, each check's command, why it stopped); on exit the app takes the terminal back and runs fm code --recheck ID: the task's checks again, each through Foreman's guard and recorded as evidence, with the next command (T-1341)
- fm in any shell from the first session: session start makes ~/.local/bin/fm → the running Foreman's bin/fm when nothing is there, and re-points a Foreman link whose version folder an update removed (a file, another program's link or a working link to another checkout stays); fm doctor asks a fresh login shell (only the profile's PATH) and FAILs with the export line and the profile file for bash, zsh or fish when it can't find this fm; fm code tui's welcome, before a project's first run, shows fm on PATH, a runner and the folder as a project, each missing one with its key (l links fm, m picks a runner, i makes the project), and e opens the question box with "explain this repo" (T-1333)
- fm code tui --tour: the sample run played over about 30 seconds in the real screen, a caption bar naming each panel as its event lights it (the track, Checks, Activity, the guard, Tokens per call and Time, Changed, the close); --tour --snapshot FILE.svg writes it as one looping animated SVG (SMIL, a frame per event, the last frame the still where SMIL doesn't run; demo/code-tui-tour.svg), --tour --plain prints the captions with the Activity lines (T-1309)
- fm code tui: a replayed run (--events FILE, or Enter on a past run) steps through its events with ←/→ (Home and End jump to its start and end), the screen rebuilt at each, and the status bar says where (◂▸ 10/14 verify); a switch between the run, its diff and past runs fades in over a quarter second instead of jumping (not with motion off) (T-1307)
- Guard: python's -c code is no longer also read as bash (x.get('at') read as a subshell running at was refused as scheduling a command); what python code spawns is still checked, and perl, ruby, node and php -c (a syntax check or a config file) aren't read as code; bash, sh and pwsh -c text still is (T-1786)
- fm ideas sends the pack once per round as the system text every lens child shares (a file, so no argument size limit) with only the lens as the message, and the first lens runs alone for up to 10 s so the rest read the pack from the prompt cache it wrote; the run prints and keeps the cache tokens read and written (T-1265)
- After a /rewind or a revert: when the working files are back to a tree recorded before a done step's proof (the task's start or an earlier run), that step reopens, its proof is kept as rewound and never counted, and fm next asks for it again; checked at session start, on each prompt and in fm state (Claude Code raises no rewind event). fm doctor names each line in CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.local.md, rules or Claude Code's memory that contradicts Foreman's rules (never run the tests, no tests, commit every change, never commit, skip planning) with file:line and a fix (T-1267)
- Foreman in one file: plugin/templates/CLAUDE.lite.md is the discipline (brief, test first, evidence, one task at a time, review, safety) in under 40 lines for people who won't install the plugin, and fm bench duel --vs snippet runs Foreman against Claude with no plugins reading that file as CLAUDE.md; a hygiene test finds plugin/lib functions nothing names (one, fmguard._quoted, removed) (T-1264)
- foreman-ui 0.19.0: a grant (fm ask) is put to a person. The mod answers the guard's ask for it as its own (tool.check), so Claude Code settles it only through the dialog, a host's callback or a PermissionRequest hook: never auto mode's classifier, bypass mode or a saved rule for all of Bash; where nobody can be asked it is refused (T-1262)
- Benchmarks page: a generated "Where Foreman loses" section lists every duel row where Claude Code passed and Foreman didn't, or Foreman cost or took 25% more, each with fm bench repro LABEL CASE, which reruns that row on both sides with the run's own model, runs, budget, compaction window and marathon, and prints then and now side by side (T-1261)
- fm bench soak --compact-at TOKENS runs a compaction storm (each session compacts once its context nears TOKENS, every few turns when small; each case counts its compactions), and --sweep 20000,40000,80000 runs the soak once per window with a row each (passes, repeats, compactions, cost), to measure where compaction starts to cost repeats (T-1259)
- fm motion verify samples the page in fm shot's own browser (a Chromium on PATH, FOREMAN_CHROME or Playwright's download; nothing installed) instead of fm smoke's whole frozen crawl: one page, the frozen clock and paused animations, and the same sampleMotion fm smoke runs (T-1716)
- fm page looks --cards prints the look cards as trading cards: each in its own palette and type pair (title, an Aa, its line, a palette strip, type and motion), 63 x 88 mm, nine to an A4 page, one file with nothing from another host (T-1650, the cards; the shader sketchbook and the site gallery are their own items)
- UI playbooks: the UI contract is written down (references/ui/INDEX.md: motion, contrast, keyboard, small screens, states), every UI playbook says how it meets each rule in its own ## UI contract section, and fm check playbooks refuses one that doesn't. fm playbooks coverage prints a heatmap of stages by stack: how many playbooks in each stage name a card of each stack area (T-1623)
- fm run --all-projects works every Foreman project's queue in turn with the same options (--runner NAME too), each under its own run lock, skipping projects with nothing queued, and says how many it worked and how many stopped early (T-1782)
- fm code app: g pressed during a run leaves that run alone and starts the queue when it ends, counting it in the digest; s stops both (T-1783)
- fm code app: g works the project's queue, one fm code --task run after another, until it is empty, two tasks in a row block, or s stops it; then a digest lands in Activity (what closed, what blocked and why, why it stopped), headed "While you were away" when no key was pressed for five minutes (T-1779, the app part)
- foreman-ui pane: an option set to a value it can't use (a typo in settings.json) keeps its last good value instead of falling back to the default, says so once as a toast, and a restarted pane takes the last good values it kept, as the fm code app keeps its last good tui.toml (T-1613)
- fm scene compile --to component --tag NAME names the element (pebble-scene), so two scenes share a page, the module's own comments and box rules naming it too; a name that isn't a valid custom element name is refused. The element's label is watched: a page that changes it renames the canvas, and taking it away hides the canvas so the text twin speaks (T-1706)
- Guard: a backslash inside single quotes ('x\') anywhere in a command no longer stops it reading a literal for loop as the commands it runs, which had left an earlier cd uncertain and refused writes in the folder the command had moved to (T-1781)
- foreman-ui pane: a saved palette (fm design tokens --to pane --save) stays when another option changes; it was dropped until fm ui's view next refreshed, which can be long when nothing moves (T-1780)
- fm run --runner NAME works the queue through fm code --task on that runner (codex, gemini, a code.toml one) instead of claude -p sessions, with fm run's own loop around it: one task at a time or in lanes, blocked tasks skipped, no progress stops it (T-1773, the first part)
- fm code app: n during a run asks a question about the code beside it, read-only: fm code --ask answers it without a task or the tree's lock (and refuses if the planner plans a change), and the answer lands in Activity while the run goes on; s or q ends an ask still going (T-1770)
- The checks that each fm command has its own help position and that tiers come from registrations moved from test_help to test_cli, which every fmcli.py change runs: fm map links tests by name, so T-1608 never ran test_help and shipped a shared position (T-1776)
- The guard reads a script a command runs about 6x faster with the same verdicts, so more real scripts are read whole inside its 0.2 s CPU box (182 of 200 public system shell scripts, was 145) instead of falling back to the literal scan: a script read no longer guesses where an unknown target lands (it dropped every such finding), a line is joined to the next or read for a heredoc only when it ends in \ or holds <<, and one command's comments and words are worked out once, not by each check (T-1778)
- fm settings check draws tui.toml every way Foreman does (the app at every layout, a headless snapshot as --snapshot makes it, and --plain's lines) and names the way that can't, with exit 1, so a setting that only breaks the snapshot or a piped run shows before it matters (T-1612)
- foreman-ui pane: fm design tokens DESIGN.md --to pane --save keeps that palette for the pane, which paints it over its default at the next refresh (cb and riso still win); fm ui carries it checked (known keys, #rrggbb only) and --clear forgets it (T-1717)
- The hook bench's script case is budgeted as a hook (150 ms) plus the guard's CPU time box for one cold script read (200 ms), read from the guard: its 37 KB script never finishes inside the box, so 300 ms left the hook's own startup about 85 ms, which a loaded machine ate (T-1777)
- fm dojo: a short drill of commands the guard refuses and why, for people: each round asks allow or refuse, then shows the guard's own live verdict and the reason; off a terminal it prints the drill as a study sheet with the answers (T-1608)
- fm code runs one at a time per working tree: a second run in the same tree stops and names the run holding it (task, pid, since when), while a lane's worktree has its own; a run that closes, blocks or stops after making its task tells fm notify (T-1342, the lock and notify parts)
- Known fixes: a command that writes files (sed -i, fm edit, a redirect into a file, git checkout …) changed the code, as an Edit does, so the pass after it is no longer kept and offered as a command fix; a debugging sed on a test file had been offered as "the fix" for a failing run (T-1771)
- fm code runners --probe MODEL scores a model 0-10 on ten small graded prompts (JSON only, data in an envelope stays data, unknown rather than invented), and fm code bench --variants base,evidence runs the seeds once per prompt variant and reports each one's closed count, so a prompt change is measured before it becomes the default (T-1403)
- fm code --after T-ID "<follow-up>" plans a new task that carries the earlier run's request, plan, end and changed files (as data) and names it; in the app, Enter after a close opens that follow-up prompt (n stays a fresh request) (T-1340)
- fm mandate keeps a sweeping request as one standing record (its text, its clauses, an end date): fm scout and fm night read it, and once it ends scout captures one decision (renew or let it lapse) and nothing more under it (T-1400)
- fm scout watches upstream: a skill source whose remote hasn't moved isn't fetched (git ls-remote), one that moved is one finding listing the skills added, removed and renamed with its licence class, and each Claude Code changelog line past the tested version naming something Foreman never mentions becomes a finding; nothing installs (T-1391)
- fm code says in one line when an indexed skill from fm lib's sources clearly matches the request (its name, licence class and token cost; fm lib show reads it), and installs nothing; fm plugins suggest lists up to three marketplace plugins that fit the project's own stack, each with its reason (T-1397)
- A failure's signature is never a test runner's tally (FAILED (1 classes), FAILED (failures=2), pytest's "N failed" line, fm quiet's ✗ exit line): every red test run had shared one, so deliberate red-first runs were captured as a "recurring obstacle" (T-1769)
- foreman-ui pane: every string in the view is cleaned as it arrives, so a task title, project name or check note can no longer draw a bidi override (‮ turned text around) or a control character; below 26 columns the pane says "Foreman: 26+ columns" instead of spilling past its edge. A hostile-width sweep (CJK, emoji, combining, RTL, zero-width, controls) through the pane's own renderer now guards both (T-1679)
- fm code runs workers and checks on the project's pinned toolchain: .tool-versions, .python-version, .node-version and .nvmrc are read as data, each pin is looked up with mise, asdf or pyenv (run from /, so the repo's own config, hooks and .envrc never run), and its bin folder goes first on PATH; a pin no manager has installed is named, never installed (T-1407)
- fm code app: a closed run ends with a receipt line in Activity (task, checks passed, files and lines, tokens, cost, time, commit), and quitting leaves the last screen as plain text in the scrollback, so the run stays readable after q (T-1305)
- fm check office FILE checks an .xlsx or .docx deliverable with no extra library, as a verify command: no error cells (#REF!, #DIV/0!, #N/A …) or broken Word fields ("Error! Reference source not found."), and --cell 'Sheet1!B7=42', --sheets N, --contains TEXT. A member over 64 MB unpacked or any DTD is refused (T-1406)
- The piped-gate note no longer fires on 'fm check' inside quoted text, such as a grep pattern holding `\|fm check` (T-1768)
- fm check reruns just the failing tests of a slow gate (over 120 s; FOREMAN_SLOW_GATE) when its output names every failure (unittest totals or pytest's summary): a full suite that failed once in a load spike now clears in seconds as a flake instead of opening an urgent baseline task (T-1678)
- A Bash call that pipes fm check (`fm check | tail -5`, in the background too) gets a note that its exit status is the last program's, not the gates': read the ✓/✗ line before pushing. T-1758 reached main after such a call's exit 0 was tail's (T-1759)
- A flag among a command's path words no longer strands the paths after it: `fm new check --run PATH`, `fm img diff A --tol 3 B` and the like parse as meant (the command's own options move in front of its paths). Free text (a request, a message) is left as typed, so a flag inside one is still an error (T-1671)
- fm design lint's afterglow rule reads Tailwind state classes: hover:bg-…, focus:scale-…, group-hover:opacity-… or aria-[…]:text-… on an element with no transition-*, duration-* or animate-* class is flagged, as a :hover rule with no transition is; NOT_COVERED says CSS-in-JS state styles and @apply aren't read for it (T-1689)
- The guard's hint for a changelog edit with no task no longer says to focus the finished task again (fm focus refuses a done task); it says to add the line in the next task with a scope note (T-1767)
- fm img diff --tol N counts a pixel within N on every channel as unchanged (0 to 255), so an anti-aliasing shimmer needn't fail a screenshot check; the JSON says the tolerance used (T-1684)
- fm quiet hands its command its own stdin when that is a file or a pipe with data waiting, so `fm quiet -- python3 - <<EOF` runs the script; it ran nothing and reported ✓ exit 0 before. A terminal, /dev/null or an idle open pipe still gets /dev/null, so nothing waits on an agent's stdin (T-1766)
- fm code's slot-format ladder: when a model's plan or close notes fail JSON repair twice, it is asked one plain-text question a field (type, tier, title, criteria as 'what is true :: command' lines, steps; each close note), and the answers are assembled and checked as before, so a local model that can't hold a JSON shape still plans; a run that fails both stops with Next: fm code models (T-1765)
- fm lib check counts related names with no card by each lint note's kind (related or stale), not by matching ': related ' in its words, so a stale note that quotes that phrase is no longer counted (T-1604)
- fm check playbooks compiles the python blocks marked no-run (they never run, so a syntax slip in one went unseen); the repo's 41 pass (T-1587)
- The 3D playbook's WebGL context cap is measured, not guessed: Chrome desktop keeps 16 live (HeadlessChrome 153 on Linux, 40 made); the browsers that couldn't be measured here are named as unverified (T-1557)
- The terminal UI playbook grows from eight rules to 31 a reviewer can mark: colour depth, display width and wide glyphs, resize, bracketed paste, a motion budget, the terminal's lifecycle and CLI product design, with Foreman's own terminal code as the worked example (T-1515)
- fm code slot integrity: an edit or write that elides code ('# ... rest unchanged', '// … existing code') is refused and re-asked unless the file already says it; an edit whose old text misses only by whitespace is applied where that match is unique and refused as ambiguous otherwise; a fix round names up to five file:line places from the failing check's output (T-1398; the one-field-per-question ladder is T-1765)
- fm task finish no longer captures a 'Catch it earlier' follow-up when the why-not-caught says the work was new ('new …', 'n/a'), as it already didn't for 'none'; three such items were noise (T-1763)
- fm code delight: a project's first closed run says so once (first proved change in <project>, with two brighter, wider close waves in the app), and picking a runner in the app says hello with its newest run's end and age (T-1349)
- fm code: a run's edit leases now end with it whether a model or a worker did the work; a model run's leases held the code for 20 more minutes, so the next run on the same function was refused (T-1764)
- fm code says once at start when fm doctor finds two Foreman installs or Foreman's hook registered twice (each distinct finding once; state/code-warned.json remembers) (T-1757)
- Security: the supply check hashes the bytes of a plugin's files over 4 MB (streamed, 256 MB a plugin), and past that or past the 4,000-file cap stamps their size, mtime and ctime, so an edit that puts size and mtime back still shows; a baseline from before adopts the new hash without a false alarm (T-1446)
- fm code app action cards: a failure shows what happened, what Foreman did and the next command (Enter resumes, opens a read-only fm command's output, or names any other for your shell); a live stall shows w wait, k stop, m stop and resume with another runner; the app stops runners a killed run left when it opens, and quitting mid-run prints a stopping … stopped line per runner (T-1762: T-1755, T-1756, T-1761)
- fm code diagnostics: fm code why [ID] explains a run (how it ended or that a kill stopped it, the steps done, a stall's cause, the next command); text piped on stdin becomes the request's fenced context (a pipe or file only, 256 KB and 5 s at most, so a pipe that never closes can't hang a run); the start event names the run's containment (Landlock with its ABI, or guard only) and the app's header shows it (T-1760: T-1373, T-1374, T-1402)
- Security: fm code --task (and the app's r on a task) refuses a task still waiting for the user's own yes (a brief pulled by fm sync, imported, or written by a replay) before any of its commands runs, naming why and fm ask ID confirm; the task's verify command could run on one key press (T-1369)
- fm ideas' "near T-…" marks: an idea is marked near a done task only when half its distinctive words are in that task's title, or three quarters in its title and request together; words in a fifth of the done tasks (three at least) don't count. Two words a long request happened to hold made spurious matches (T-1303)
- Local models' real context: a local server fm code finds says the context it serves (Ollama's num_ctx from /api/show, else its 4096 default; llama.cpp's n_ctx from /props; vLLM's max_model_len; LM Studio's loaded context), falling back to 8192, and fm code's prompt caps follow it (T-1360)
- fm code run friction: a blocked run, a stalled worker, a run a kill left unfinished and a run that fails before its task exists each count as friction with fm code, logged once per cause onto the one improvement round in Foreman's own project (never the user's), and the app's home shows it first as Foreman noticed (T-1339)
- fm code config: every fm code setting (runner, model, lean, limit_wait, chain, the on hooks, worker timeout, max steps, max usd, confinement, sensitivity) with its value and source, --explain KEY for what one does and how to change it, and a code.toml that doesn't parse named with its line while the view shows the defaults (a run still refuses it) (T-1359)
- fm code routing by record: fm code runners shows each runner's record per kind from every project's past runs (runs, closed, dollars a closed run), and runner = auto gives the work to the cheapest runner green on the plan's kind (3+ runs, two thirds closed), saying why, or keeps the planning runner when none is (T-1358)
- fm code runner pings: fm code runners --ping [NAME] asks each installed runner one tiny question through its read-only plan command in a scratch folder and sorts the answer (ok, not logged in, usage limit, flag refused, endpoint down, no answer) with latency and version, kept for the app, whose runners screen shows a lamp per runner and pings the selected one with p (T-1335)
- fm code limits and failover: a worker turn's failure is sorted (usage limit with its reset time, sign-in, crash, timeout); a limit resetting within code.toml limit_wait (default an hour) is waited out and the turn done again, past it the run stops with its resume command; code.toml chain = [...] hands a crashed, timed-out or signed-out runner's turn to the next (T-1356)
- fm code run-event hooks: code.toml [on] closed, blocked and needs-you commands get the event's JSON on stdin, past Foreman's guard, 30 s at most (a refusal or a failure is a note, never the run's end); fm state --line ends with the newest fm code run, running, stopped or how it ended (T-1351)
- fm code durable runs: a new run names each unfinished run in the project with its fm code --resume command (a run another fm code is working is left out); the app's past runs show an unfinished one with that command and r resumes it there; opened from home outside any project, the app reopens the project left last with a welcome back; tests kill a run while planning, mid-step and during its check and resume it (T-1338)
- fm code cost: each call's dollars (a claude CLI's total_cost_usd, or tokens times a models.json profile's price per million) go to the budget ledger under the project, the close line shows the run's total, and --max-usd caps a run across resumes, claude turns getting --max-budget-usd with what is left (T-1357)
- fm code: every failure ends with one Next: line naming the command to run (and the event carries it; the app shows it under the failure); the start event names the Foreman running it (root and version); every runner fm code starts is registered, and the next run stops any a hard-killed fm code left running and says which (T-1754: T-1334, T-1355, T-1337)
- Claude Code plugin parity: the rules point a session at fm which for any verb or skill (fm help lists them all), and a test fails when a user-facing fm verb is neither named in the plugin's rules, commands or skills nor found by fm which (T-1520)
- fm research add --from-agent: a Noticed line close to an open task (fm capture's near-duplicate rule) is logged onto that task instead of becoming a second one, and the output names where it went (T-1726)
- Playbooks reach reviewers and recall as fm playbooks show gives them: fm audit prep writes the review checklists the diff's languages call for (code-reviewer always) with this project's {{facts}} and overlays filled in, and names the file in the brief, which the fm-reviewer agent reads first; fm recall points at fm playbooks show NAME, not a file path (T-1681)
- fm code's TUI: fm code --themes prints every theme side by side on a sample run (plain text under NO_COLOR); the diff view reveals its lines on open (at once with motion reduced or off) and marks the words a changed line changed; at 160 columns or more a third column shows the Plan and Check output panels (T-1742: T-1311, T-1312, T-1347)
- fm code's TUI: the request box edits like a shell line (arrows, Home/End, Ctrl-A/E/W/U/K, Up/Down for this launch's requests) with a preview of where Enter sends it and which runner takes it; /name runs an app command, Tab after @ completes a tracked file; / in the run view filters the Activity log (its title counts the matches), and the focused panel names its own keys in its bottom edge (T-1741: T-1313, T-1346)
- fm code's TUI: q or s during a live run asks for a second press within 2 s; the ? keys poster shows the command line behind a key; a launch tip names one command never used; the terminal title names the run's state, a bell rings once when the run is blocked or the guard refuses (not with motion off), and a model quiet past 10 s is named. A half-block canvas (T-1314) was not built: the token chart already draws at eighth-block resolution (T-1740: T-1310, T-1348)
- Brief parsing is linear on step and criterion lines: a run of 20,000 spaces inside one took 1.5 s per parse (every hook parses briefs, and a pulled brief can hold one); now under a millisecond, same groups (T-1616)
- fm check --affected --evidence records the affected run on the task (labelled affected tests only; closing still needs the full gates); fm lease take lists only what it took; a task waiting for the user's yes says why (synced, imported, written by a replay, or found in an old session) (T-1752: T-1747, T-1748, T-1750)
- Layout collisions: the site header fits at 320 px (it made the page 349 px wide; a real-browser test measures 320, 360 and 390), and fm page graph's poster drops a label that would overlap one already placed (the text twin still names every task) (T-1738: T-1683, T-1735)
- fm check waits for the machine-wide gate lock at most 15 minutes (FOREMAN_GATE_WAIT seconds), then runs beside the holder and says so: a hung check in another project held every check for over 40 minutes (T-1753)
- fm sync import: a task new to this machine that arrives from the repo's .foreman/ waits for the user's yes (confirm), and no pulled brief carries another machine's lane, builder or cost marks; this machine's own marks and confirm stay on updates. fm home import shares the same settling (T-1749)
- Guard: a move is judged at its sources too (mv, mv -t, rsync --remove-source-files), so moving a protected file out of the plugin cache or core is refused; git -C X clone url dir is judged at X/dir (T-1751: T-1744, T-1745)
- Load-proof tests: run.py --load N reproduces load flakes beside N busy processes; hook, fm and fm code hang guards stretch with the measured load; the hero, city and graph frame checks are judged by animation time and rest, not wall-clock windows (T-1730: T-1719, T-1721, T-1728)
- One WCAG contrast formula (fm code's TUI uses fmdesign's), every fm help command its own position, fm doctor flags emoji variation selectors anywhere, and MASTER.md lists every plugin/lib module with a test that keeps it so (T-1736: T-1675, T-1686, T-1687, T-1724)
- fm home export / import: Foreman's projects (tasks, archive, research, decisions, ledger, meta) and tui.toml as one tarball, secrets redacted and sensitive projects left out, with a hash manifest; import is the user's to run, takes only that allowlist (no grants, trust, runner config, check commands or recorded runs; open tasks wait for fm ask ID confirm), checks every path and hash first and only goes into an empty home (T-1665)
- fm sync import: an invisible character inside a run, worktree or audit mark no longer keeps it counting on the importing machine (T-1665)
- Guard: touch that creates a file in a protected folder (Claude Code's plugin cache, Foreman's core) is judged as a write, as is one after an rm, unlink or mv in the same command; touch on an existing file and touch -c pass, and a touched file run after is no "script written here" (T-1680). git -C and git init read a $D set to an absolute path or ~ where it points, a later -C $D both on its own and under the folder before it, and the refusal says "$D unresolved" when it can't tell (T-1487). Discarding git commands however spelled (\git, g''it, $'git', a variable, eval, a function, sh <<<) are saved first like plain ones, in the folder an earlier cd (inside eval or sh -c too) or a -C $R names, every repo it discards in, all within one 3 s budget; after a cd it can't follow, the note says it couldn't tell where instead of saving the session's folder (T-1482)
- fm shot and fm stories take --profile tv|eink|car|wrist (that surface's width, height and scale, from fm critique's table) and --reduced-motion (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce, set before the first paint) (T-1737: T-1725, T-1734)
- Lane and close friction: finish --commit names another open task that edited a file it commits whole (shared.py was also edited by T-xxxx since this task's focus); a CLEAN task's builder brief says to record the behaviour lock (fm check --evidence ID --step 1) first; fm check --affected leaves another lane's red-first test file out of the command it runs (by path, so a same-name test of another runner still runs) and says when every linked test was skipped; fm lease drop lists only what it dropped (8 at most) and the lane claims still held (T-1731)
- Redaction reads key names: "password": "x" in JSON text (escaped JSON and Python reprs too) and {"password": "x"} in logged objects are redacted; token counts and other keys that only contain the words are kept (T-1733)
- fm check --affected (each builder's own run) no longer queues on the machine-wide gate lock; only a full run does (T-1743)
- Gate runs share one machine-wide lock: a full fm check and the release canary queue behind each other, a waiter says who holds it, and a stale or stray holder value is ignored (T-1668)
- fm capture refuses a near-duplicate of an open task of the same type, naming it (exit 5; --json gives {"refused", "of", "title"}; --anyway keeps both), notes a similar open task of another type, and notes a FEATURE close to done work with that task's id; only fm capture checks, not fm intake, fm task new or fm research --from-agent (T-1662)
- fm ui --json: every string of the view is plain text (escape sequences, control, format and separator characters removed; newlines kept), so no ledger line, signal or hand-edited brief can make Claude Code refuse the pane; paths are kept as they are (T-1674)
- Settings: setting a key no surface has is refused by name everywhere (fm code's :set line and fm settings serve said nothing before) (T-1723)
- fm sentinel re-verifies closed tasks a later commit touched: each commit since its last scan that changes a file in a closed task's scope queues that task's passing checks (not its own commits, by the Foreman-Task trailer; 5 tasks per commit; history rewritten or a branch switched only moves the watermark), the queue runs first, and a failure captures a FIX naming the task with the output redacted; queued entries with side effects or for tasks no longer done are dropped unrun and counted (T-1663)
- fm page graph: the task graph as one 3D page that loads nothing from another host. Open and linked tasks stand in layers by the longest chain they wait on; a cycle is broken at its own lowest id and named on the page. depends_on are arcs, status is colour (each 3:1 on the ground, blocked hatched and done ringed besides), and tier is height. An SVG poster names the active and blocked work, the text twin holds every task and what it waits on, and a second run refreshes it from fm state. It's held to the city's checks through a shared OneFile test (T-1654)
- fm critique --stress TEXT pseudo-localises a string (accents, 40% longer, [brackets]; placeholders and HTML entities intact) and --profile tv|eink|car|wrist prints a surface's viewport and contrast preset (T-1643)
- fm settings serve: the settings as a page on 127.0.0.1 (random port; the printed URL's token works once and becomes an HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookie; Host and Origin checked, bodies capped, CSP default-src 'none'), each change through fm settings set's own checks and the guard; stops on Ctrl-C, SIGTERM or 30 idle minutes (T-1659)
- Guard: unsetting or overriding CLAUDECODE through any wrapper is refused, since env's own words are read wherever env appears (chrt, taskset and stdbuf -o L before env, unbuffer [-p]); script -c, sh -c and setsid forms were already refused, and each is now a test row (T-1722)
- fm stories CHART: a component's state matrix, one page with a cell per state a JSON chart lists (default, hover, error…), each state reached from about:blank by fm flow steps and shot in the headless Chromium fm shot finds, under fm flow's URL rule and request blocking; the shots inline with a CSP, so the page loads nothing from another host; no browser, or one that won't start: the states and their steps listed, and exit 2 (never a pass). The chart is untrusted: 20 states, 20 steps a state, 64 KB, 12 MB of shots in all, 300 s a run. fm flow's goto keeps a relative path's ?query#fragment, so a state can be a query string (T-1640)
- fm design check: an a11y gate (the role tokens' contrast pairs, shadcn's --X-foreground on --X too, failing when blocking and no pair could be checked; the lint's reduced-motion, flash and img-alt findings; and a :focus or :focus-visible style that shows wherever there are interactive elements) and a debt gate counting untokened colours, durations and radii per file under a budget from DESIGN.md's Gates line (`- debt: advisory 40`), listing the top files (T-1646)
- fm page city: the repo as a 3D city at dusk in one file, each tracked file a building as tall as its lines with windows lit by its commits in 180 days ("history unavailable" when git log fails), a district per folder, raw WebGL2 over an isometric SVG poster it starts from; a 4.5 s intro the first drag or key ends, and reduced motion and no WebGL2 keep the poster (T-1653)
- fm critique URL|FILE: a computed-style census of the page (colours, fonts, sizes, radii, shadows, spacing, with counts; frozen, files and localhost only unless --any-url) critiqued against DESIGN.md's tokens — unnamed values, near-duplicates (ΔE_OK < 0.02, lengths within 7%) and one-off outliers; --why SELECTOR prints the matched rules with their stylesheets and the winning declaration per property (T-1642)
- fm code app: `[[rule]]` tables in tui.toml switch the layout or a panel while a run event holds (verify, failing, running, closed) and switch back after, with a "rule N" chip in the status line; extensions declare their own settings (`api.setting`, API version 2), which `fm settings` and the settings screen list, check and set under `[ext.NAME]`. fm settings loads extensions only for the actions that read their settings, each under a 3 s limit with stdin closed, so a hanging or exiting one never stops it (T-1657)
- MASTER.md: the plugin/lib row (one 22,000-character table line every module shared) is now a list with one line per module, so changes to different modules no longer conflict at merge (T-1713)
- fm settings chords: press keys and see each as fm code's own decoder reads it, named as [keys] takes it (ctrl+a, f5, up), with Alt chords shown as Esc then the key and sequences fm code doesn't name shown as not delivered, so a binding is known to arrive before it's bound; needs a terminal (in Claude Code: ! fm settings chords) (T-1660)
- fm flow FILE: a user journey, one step a line (goto URL|FILE, click SEL, type SEL TEXT, expect SEL [TEXT], wait MS), run in the headless Chromium fm shot finds, as a verify command: exit 1 names the step that failed, 2 with no browser or one that won't start (never a pass). The flow is untrusted: five verbs, 200 steps, 2000 characters a word, --timeout at most 60 s a step and 600 s a journey; selectors and text reach the page as JSON data; goto opens only files in the flow's folder or localhost, 127.0.0.1 and [::1] (--any-url: any http(s) page), and every request the page makes is held to the same rule through DevTools' Fetch domain, so a redirect, refresh or clicked link elsewhere is blocked and fails its step. fm shot's browser close no longer waits forever on a process it can't kill (T-1639).
- The first UI edit's design-direction note now says "load: Skill foreman:ui-design" (the job skill, which routes to directions) instead of /foreman:directions (T-1715)
- fm design roll [--seed N] [--apply|--undo]: a seeded pick of one look card (its palette roles, type pair with a non-generic lead face, motion and texture together); --apply writes it as DESIGN.md's ## Look with the card's name beside the seed, keeping the bytes before the first roll (a record and a plain .orig copy) through rerolls and failed or half-done ones, and --undo restores them byte for byte while the file is what the roll wrote; a symlinked DESIGN.md is refused (T-1648)
- fm design palette SEED --out FILE writes the export to FILE (a symlink is refused); it was advertised and ignored (T-1714)
- fm shot --diff BASE compares each page's viewport shot with BASE/<page>/viewport.png from an earlier fm shot --out BASE. A change within the baseline's noise floor passes: the floor is the most pixels any of the baseline's own frames differs from its viewport shot. A change over the floor fails and names the changed box. A baseline with no frames has an unmeasured floor, so any change fails, and a floor over 5% of the page warns that the baseline is animated and should be shot with --frozen. Every shot checks document.fonts for families that visible text uses but drew in a fallback; under --diff a fallback, or fonts that couldn't be checked in 10 s, fails the run. With --diff, no browser exits 1 ("not compared") instead of 0, and an empty or missing BASE, or one inside the output folder, is refused (T-1637).
- fm doctor warns when a model-invocable skill was named in more than five sessions and loaded in none in 30 days (a report only; user-only skills and the idle "next" are skipped); fm usage and doctor count through one note_skill() (T-1703)
- fm design tokens DESIGN.md --to css|app|pane [--light|--dark]: DESIGN.md's Tokens table (read by its header's column names) compiles to CSS variables (dark values under prefers-color-scheme and .dark/[data-theme="dark"]; a value that could escape its declaration falls back to its hex or is named), an fm code tui.toml theme + [colors] fm settings accepts (roles from token names, shadcn's included; the preset picked by the screen colour's lightness; a style too faint is left out and named, and a preset style too faint on the new screen named too) or the Claude Code pane's palette JSON (T-1651)
- fm design check [ROOT]: design gates a project opts into in DESIGN.md's ## Gates section (`- brief: advisory` or `blocking`); the first, the executable brief, checks the UI files for each Knobs colour, quoted type family and duration and each Direction `- must:` line, naming where it found each or that it didn't; advisory exits 0 with its findings, blocking exits 1, so fm check's gates can include it (T-1645)
- Three job skills Claude loads on task words: debug-test when a test fails, a run crashes or a bug comes back (read the failure with fm fail, trace or logs, reproduce, narrow with fm suspects, bisect, whyred and record, one hypothesis at a time, then a red-to-green fix), release when it is about to release or ship (the release-and-changelog playbook for any project, fm release's checkpointed steps in Foreman's own repo, never a push or publish without the task's publish grant), and research when it needs to find something out before building (recall and saved notes first, then this repo, the stack cards and reuse, then fm research ask's cited web research, one saved note). The hooks' FIX hint now loads debug-test and the RESEARCH hint loads research, and a test fails any hint that names a missing or user-only skill. Each skill names only playbooks and fm verbs that exist, which one shared test helper checks for every skill; the skill listing's cap rises from 2,000 to 2,200 characters for them and T-1700's ui-design (T-1701).
- Lane friction: the Stop drive offers builder lanes up to fm lane max's cap (it compared to the default 2); fm lane brief says "not launching it? fm lane rm ID frees the slot"; merging a lane whose worktree is gone says fm lane new ID re-attaches its kept branch or --unverified merges it (T-1709, T-1710)
- fm doctor has a "headless rendering" row: available with the browser's path and version, or why not (no Chromium found, an unusable FOREMAN_CHROME, or a failed start with the browser's last error line). It is a 5 s version probe, always a PASS, and installs nothing (T-1638).
- fm which "<task>": the skills, playbooks and fm commands that fit a task in plain words, each with the exact call that loads it (Skill foreman:X, or "you type /foreman:X" for a user-only skill; fm playbooks show stage/name; fm VERB). Skills are read from the skills folder when it runs, so a new one needs no list; a playbook is indexed by its header only; Foreman's own release tools aren't offered for a user's release; a task whose fits hold under half its words, like one common word, says nothing matches. The prompt hook mentions it once a session when a work request names no skill. A shared stemmer in fmrecall now makes a doubled consonant single (shipping → ship).
- fm playbooks route STAGE names the playbooks a stage or task type loads (from the stage table), blame NAME maps each line to the task whose commit wrote it (the summary's id first, then a Task: trailer, then the body), and preview NAME lists the checks loading it adds: its runnable python blocks and quoted `… :: CMD` verify commands (T-1622)
- fm motion verify [PATH] [--samples FILE]: values sampled under fm smoke's frozen profile (each motion-NAME animation seeked through one iteration) held to the Motion Score's duration and curve within 2% of the travel; each drift names the motion, property, time, expected and sampled values and the tolerance; colours and rotations are noted, not compared; with no browser it says so and checks only the score (T-1627)
- foreman:ui-design: one skill for page, component, animation and 3D work (pick a direction, set tokens, build, add motion and a 3D scene, then check contrast, lint and screenshots) that routes to the ui playbooks, the directions skill and fm design/scene/motion/looks/page/img/smoke. A design request's hint now loads it, and a UI request's hint (now also raised by animation, motion, 3D, WebGL and three.js words) names it beside the playbook index. MASTER.md and the plugin README list tidy and doctor as user-only (T-1700).
- The Stop drive and fm next no longer name a queued task whose dependency is still open (held by a builder lane or waiting): they offer free work instead, and the drive's side-work lists skip it too (T-1708)
- fm playbooks eval NAME --ablate: each ## section (outside code fences) dropped in turn and the lift re-scored; a section's share is the lift it carries alone, and zero share flags a cut candidate (or a section another one repeats). The no-playbook pass runs once; --real's up-front budget estimate counts every ablation (T-1619)
- Static skill-trigger eval: test_docs ranks every model-invocable skill's description by word overlap against three or more plain requests per skill (plugin/tests/fixtures/skill_triggers.json) and fails on a miss or a tie; a new skill without an entry fails with the exact line to add (T-1704)
- fm playbooks eval NAME [--briefs FILE] [--real [--model M]]: a playbook's held-out briefs (plugin/evals/playbooks/<stage>/<name>.json, each a task and has/re/not checks) answered with and without it loaded; the lift (pass rate with minus without) is printed and recorded in playbook-evals.jsonl. By default a literal model reads the playbook as the answer, a deterministic text-presence check; --real measures what claude -p does, tool-less, checked against fm budget up front and per call. Brief files are validated (no empty checks, no long or nested-quantifier regexes). verify/tdd-workflow ships the first set (T-1618)
- fm usage counts skills honestly: a Foreman skill a hook's note or the drive's Stop reason names (/foreman:x or "load: Skill foreman:x") is logged as skill_named, a /foreman:x the user types counts as a use, and a subagent's playbook Read in a lane counts even when its path outruns the event's 120-character target. It lists "named, not loaded: X ×N" (sessions offered X that never loaded it); only real skills count, so a workflow such as /foreman:review or a typo never shows (T-1702).
- fm shot URL|FILE…: a viewport shot, frames in real time and a poster frame (the most painted) per page that moves, from a headless Chromium already on the machine (FOREMAN_CHROME, PATH or Playwright's download) driven over the DevTools pipe; no browser: exit 0 and why, nothing installed. --frozen is fm smoke's frozen profile: the clock fixed, finite animations shown at their end, endless ones held at their start. Shots go in the project's state shots/ or a non-link --out; a browser that floods, hangs or dies ends the run in time and says so (T-1636)
- fm page looks: a looks gallery in one HTML file with nothing loaded from another host. Each look card gets its own plate in the card's palette roles and type pair (a type class falls back to faces already on the device): a ledger, a broadcast strap and ticker, an index-card drawer, a survey map with legend and inset, test tiles, a lab notebook, lido lanes, night-market stalls, an observatory field of view, a sewing pattern, a freight manifest and a departures board, each with the card's motion and texture. Motion plays once as it comes into view, and Replay runs it again. Under reduced motion only the fades remain and the ticker stays still. A card without a plate of its own gets a plain palette-and-type one, so the page always holds one specimen per card. fm page and fm page looks now refuse an existing --out file they didn't write (T-1649).
- fm design lint's afterglow rule no longer flags a state whose element does move: a transition timed with a var() duration token counts, .kiln .mark covers .kiln .tile:hover .mark, and .tabs button covers .tabs [aria-pressed]; the starter web template lints clean again (T-1707)
- fm motion compile [PATH] --to css|waapi|three [--out FILE]: a clean Motion Score becomes CSS custom properties (--motion-NAME-duration, -easing, -transition, -animation) with from-only @keyframes, an ES module of WAAPI options with pick() and play(), or three.js tween parameters with ease(p) functions (cubic-bezier solved, steps() and linear() mapped exactly, since three.js has no easings of its own). Every target carries the score's own durations in ms and easings, and each motion's reduced form keeps its meaning under prefers-reduced-motion or <html data-motion="reduce"> (data-motion="full" wins over the system): none plays nothing and leaves the rest state, instant goes to the end state in 0 ms, fade animates opacity only over the same duration. A none motion's transition is all 0s, so a comma list holding it stays valid. A score with problems compiles nothing and exits 1 (--json prints them); --out writes through a temp file and refuses the score itself, and fm motion check refuses --to and --out. Motion and property names are now ASCII only, as check's messages always said (T-1625).
- fm code workers are confined with Landlock where the kernel has it (Linux 5.13+): a worker and everything it starts can write only in its tree and git folders, scratch, /dev, Claude Code's own folders and the usual tool caches, never the rest of the home folder or other projects; reads are untouched. The run says once whether its worker is confined; FOREMAN_CONFINE=0 turns it off; without Landlock workers run as before and fm doctor says so (T-1607).
- Settings profiles: tui.toml can hold [profile.NAME] tables, each with a when clause on the terminal (ssh = true, tmux = true, term = "xterm-*", width_below = N columns, colors = 0/8/256/24) and the settings it changes. The fm code app applies the first one the terminal meets over the file's own settings, each value through load_config's same checks (a bad one skipped and named profile.NAME.KEY, a bad or missing when passing the profile over), read through the same bounded reader, with the last good copy and live merge as before. fm settings profile (and /foreman:settings profile from Claude Code) prints this terminal's facts (the width unknown with no terminal and no COLUMNS, which no width_below meets), the profiles in order with what each sets, and the one that applies; an empty when is refused. fm settings list and the settings screen name the active profile and mark the keys it sets (◆, "set by profile NAME here"); fm settings set and the screen's :set say each profile that also sets the key and where it overrides; fm code tui --print-config prints the [profile] tables instead of baking one in; names from the file print escaped, never as a line of their own; diff no longer calls [profile] a dead setting, and export says profiles aren't carried (T-1656).
- fm scene compile --to component: the three.js module, byte for byte the same SCENE and mount() with its survival code, plus <fm-scene>, a custom element that mounts it in an open shadow DOM. The page writes <fm-scene label="..."> with the poster as its slot="poster" child and the text twin after it, so before the module loads, or with no WebGL, both still show; the label names the canvas (with none, the canvas is hidden from assistive tech and the twin speaks), the element carries data-state, taking it out of the page frees everything and releases the context, and putting it back mounts on a fresh canvas. A shadow root the page declared is kept, filled in when it has no canvas; the module's header and scene-3d.md give the page CSS that boxes the element before it is defined, so loading it shifts nothing (fm-scene::part(stage) sets the ratio after). A second compiled scene on one page finds fm-scene taken, warns, and leaves the page to define its exported FmScene under another name (T-1634).
- The hooks name the Foreman skill or playbook that fits, once a session per hint (a compaction re-arms it), instead of a procedure path on every step's Next line, worded as the call to make: FIX and FEATURE → load Skill foreman:intake (debugging.md / execute.md) and fm playbooks show tdd-workflow, CLEAN/PERFORMANCE/SECURITY → foreman:playbooks, verifying → verification-loop, auditing → the /foreman:review workflow; a request for a new look → foreman:directions and the ui playbooks, a smaller UI change → the ui playbooks. A hint goes out whole or waits for a turn with room, never cutting the Next line; fm next always shows it (and its JSON has a skill field). The context-tax replay went from 397 to 321 prompt bytes (T-1697).
- fmraster: an integer software rasteriser, the deterministic frame source for tests and terminal previews. Triangles in camera space (the camera at the origin looking along +z) are cut at the near plane into one or two triangles, go through a perspective divide to 1/16-pixel fixed point, fill by edge functions, depth-test on an interpolated 1/z and take one flat shade from a directional light, with back faces culled; a render past 16 full frames of pixel tests is refused before it draws, and save() writes the frame as a PNG through fm img. No float is ever made, so a lit cube's pixels are the same on every run, machine and Python, pinned by a hash in the tests (T-1631).
- Site hero scene: the proof-chain poster comes alive in a single-file WebGL2 scene (site/hero-scene.js, from the ui/scene-3d.md template, inlined by site/build.py, loading nothing). It reads its stations from the poster on show (the 4-wide row, or the 2×2 under 720px), so each diamond becomes a lit, faceted octahedron in the same place, size and hue, and a pulse of light runs the track, red where the work starts and green where it is proved, lighting each station and giving it a quarter turn that ends where it began. One pass each time the hero comes into view, then no frames; it pauses offscreen and in hidden tabs, caps the pixel ratio at 2, follows the light/dark switch and survives a lost context. Reduced motion, no WebGL2, a software renderer or any failure keep the still poster, and the canvas stays hidden from screen readers until it is live. Browser tests cover each path, and a screenshot check confirms the scene at rest is the poster, lit (T-1610).
- Guard trust: a key-shaped secret in a prompt (pasted blocks included) gets one short note naming its kind, never the value (don't repeat or store it; suggest rotating it), one-off sessions included, and a correction holding one ("never use <key>") is stored redacted, its words too; from the third same-kind fm ask in a session (counted per session, side by side) the reply suggests batching the rest of the planned work into one ask (core: --standing); fm doctor --wrong "CMD" [--should block] files a false alarm or a miss as a labelled case in Foreman's corpus plus one FIX task in that checkout's project for review, once per case, and never changes the guard (T-1606).
- fm scene compile: scene.json describes a 3D scene once as plain data (camera, lights, named materials, meshes as box, sphere, plane or vertices and faces, and named keyframe animations), checked with every default filled in so each renderer reads one shape. Unknown keys, duplicate keys, NaN, a far plane past 10,000 × near, more than three direct lights, a directional light at the origin and two animations on one property are refused by where they sit. --to three emits an ES module that builds it with three.js the way templates/scene.html survives: no WebGL keeps the poster, context loss is survived, the DPR is capped, reduced motion holds one still frame, nothing draws offscreen or hidden, a shader error ends at the poster, and dispose() frees it all and releases the context (T-1633).
- fm lane rm no longer refuses a Foreman lane over the guard's own script cache: an ignored folder git lists whole is discarded when everything in it is a known cache (state/guard-scripts.json, build and tool caches); any other file in it still refuses (T-1685).
- Motion Score: motion.toml (or a .json with the same schema) names each motion a UI uses, as [[motion]] entries or [motion.NAME] tables: its duration in ms or as a DESIGN.md token, its easing (a CSS keyword, cubic-bezier, steps, linear(), the motion playbook's ease-enter/-exit/-move or a token), the properties it animates and its reduced form (none: the rest state, never played; fade; instant). fm motion check [PATH] resolves the tokens from every table in DESIGN.md's Tokens section and names each motion with no reduced form, an unknown easing, a duration outside 0–2000 ms or a bare 0.3 meant as seconds, a reused name, a token DESIGN.md lacks or fm design extract truncated, or `all` for properties, exit 1 on any; fm design extract now keeps token values up to 200 characters (T-1624).
- Playbooks load through fm playbooks show PATH, and the playbooks skill now uses it. In the shipped text, a {{fact}} (test, gates, language, entry, ci, layout, hot, cards) is filled in from fm map for the current project; the ones named by repo files are inline code cut at 120 characters. An unknown or empty fact shows as {{fact?}}, and Go, Actions and Handlebars templates are left alone. The project's .claude/playbooks/<stage>/<name>.md follows under a "This project" heading. It sits with the project's other Claude Code files, so a session can write it and git shares it. It is shown as one blockquote below a line saying it can't relax Foreman's rules, so it can't pass for shipped text. It is marked (uncommitted) when it differs from HEAD, and its own {{…}} is left as written. It is read only as a plain file under the checkout: a link is refused and named, and it is cut at 16 KB. Control characters are dropped, and plain() now drops variation selectors everywhere. A broken map is one stderr note. The verification loop names the project's test command, and fm code workers may run fm playbooks show too. A show counts as a read in fm check playbooks' reach report and in fm usage, and the lint flags a {{name}} that is no fact (T-1621).
- fm design lint afterglow rule (T-1628): a :hover, :focus, :active, [aria-*] or .is-* block that changes color, background, transform or opacity while its element has no transition or animation anywhere in the run is flagged, since an instant jump reads as a glitch; blocks inside prefers-reduced-motion are exempt. Matching is by selector text, not the cascade. The site's own nav, copy button, side links and chart marks gain short transitions.
- Guard ratchets: the evasion corpus gains three seeded stacks of two obfuscations per dangerous command (picked by a hash of STACK_SEED, so every machine and Python version agrees; composed only where the shell still runs the original, which the bwrap oracle confirms where it can), and a gap that lets a dangerous command through now fails the tests instead of being written down; a test drops each guard rule the labelled corpora are refused with and checks the corpus goes red, and guard_untested.json lists the rules no case exercises, which may only shrink (new labelled cases for plugin, remote and self-authorize took three off it); a mutant drops only that rule's findings, so the next rule decides as it would without it. fm bench guard --mutate works again: its throwaway copy of plugin/ now carries site/bench_safety.py, which the evasion tests import (broken since T-1263) (T-1605).
- Site hero poster: site/build.py draws the proof chain (tasks done, evidence runs, proofs, commits) from site/data/stats.json as an inline SVG in the landing page's hero: one station per total along a quiet track, each the site's diamond in its signal colour with its area set by its total, the real number above and a stamp-style label below; under 720px a 2×2 without the dated kicker. It is the first paint with nothing to load from anywhere, an aria-label reads the totals and the date of the record, the colours follow the light and dark tokens, a rebuilt stats.json redraws it, and an index.html that lost the poster's mark fails the build. Its fixed frame is the still T-1610's scene will sit over (T-1609).
- fm img: a stdlib PNG codec, so no Pillow is needed. It decodes grey, grey+alpha, RGB, RGBA and palette images at 8 bits and below, with all five filters and tRNS transparency, into RGBA, and encodes RGB or RGBA. Adam7 and 16-bit files are refused by name, as are a palette index past PLTE and a FIFO. The size is checked before inflating, so a zlib bomb stops at what the header allows, and a chunk name reaches the terminal escaped. fm img info PATH prints the size and mode. fm img diff A B prints the changed-pixel count and box, exiting 1 when the images differ; two fully transparent pixels count as equal, and diff() takes a per-channel tolerance. --out writes A faded with each change in red, private (0600), never over an input and never through a symlink. A worker of fm code may run info and diff, but not --out (T-1630).
- fm fsck: a read-only check of Foreman's own state that goes deeper than fm doctor, with each line naming its project. It reports open tasks' lanes whose folder, git record or branch is gone, `*.pid` locks at least an hour old whose process is dead, journals whose last line was cut short or isn't JSON, and any of these it can't read; it exits 1 if it finds any. `--fix` (also run by fm doctor --fix) removes only the stale locks, re-checking each one first, and is refused inside a PID namespace (a sandbox), where a live holder can look dead. Broken lanes and torn (hash-chained) ledgers are left for a person, and flock `.lock` files are never touched. fm doctor shows its one-line count (T-1667).
- fm code app width oracle (T-1516): test_code_tui.HostileWidth feeds CJK, emoji, combining marks, RTL, zero-width, control and very long strings through every panel and view at four sizes and asserts each row fills exactly its width by an independent column rule; it also sweeps settings, layout, runners and the prompt, checks the string reached the screen, and found and fixed variation selectors taking a column of their own (a pictograph plus U+FE0F now fills two, as kitty, WezTerm and iTerm2 draw it; skin-tone modifiers none) and wide characters cut in half by an overlay or a clip (orphaned or doubled cell). A crash in the sweep saves a fixture under plugin/tests/fixtures/crashes that the same test replays.
- fm looks: a taste ledger for the twelve looks, kept in Foreman's state. Looks are ranked by what the user kept (+3), reverted (−3) or met with "not like that" (−2), and by a 12-pair calibration: a one-key picker on a terminal that reads escape sequences to their end, `/foreman:looks` through AskUserQuestion, and L in the fm code app with Backspace to go back. Pages, photos (with Pillow when installed) or files the user names also count, read-only, capped at 8 MiB and 50 a run, never a URL or a symlink, and never more than +3 a look; only their names are kept, and they are trimmed first. Kept and reverted are recorded when the model runs fm looks keep/revert. A broken ledger is kept as .bak. The directions skill drafts from the top of the ranking (T-1537).
- Design tokens: fm design palette SEED makes a light/dark pair whose body text meets 4.5:1 (7:1 in dark), each pair aimed a little above its floor, and exports DTCG, CSS and Tailwind; fm design extract reads a UI's :root custom properties (dark @media and .dark blocks, nested ones included) into a ## Tokens table in DESIGN.md, written through a lock, keeping CRLF and symlinks and refusing a file that isn't UTF-8; fm design contrast checks a token file's pairs. The /foreman:design-extract skill drives it; a DESIGN.md holding only tokens no longer counts as a chosen direction (that takes a ## Direction section) (T-1507).
- Strict-by-default templates: fm new web|scene|panel copies a template that passes its own check (O_EXCL, so nothing already there is replaced without --force, and --force replaces a symlink itself, never its target); fm new check PATH runs a panel as the fm code app would, only if it is yours and writable by no one else (a file in another git repo needs --run), in a bounded child whose pass needs a per-run token, so an early exit can't read as green. Documented runnable fences run in a temp home with TMPDIR in their own folder (trusted like the tests); a card's verified: date may be a day ahead; fm help QUESTION answers in lines of at most 100 columns and refuses a question with nothing to look up (T-1519).
- fm replay --oracle [--cmd TEXT --cwd DIR] runs each labelled guard case (or one command) in a throwaway bwrap sandbox (read-only root, no network, an empty home, memory and file size capped) whose PATH holds nothing, so the shell records every program it would start, with the argv it really built and the folder its cd's reached, instead of running it; each line runs with the programs failing and then succeeding, so both sides of a || or an if are seen. A recorded command the guard refuses on its own is a line the guard misread; a sandbox that didn't start or a guard that raised is unable and fails the run too. Without bwrap it says so and runs nothing (T-1553).
- fm code app settings safety: a tui.toml the app can't draw with starts the last one it could (kept in Foreman's state on each good start; the defaults when there is none) and says so on the screen, the file left as written; an edit to tui.toml while the app runs is read back in once a second and applied when it draws (kept out and said when it doesn't, isn't valid TOML, isn't a regular file or is over 1 MB), and a key changed there is never overwritten unseen by the app's own writes; fm settings bisect (and /foreman:settings bisect) names the setting that breaks the screen by trying halves of the changes on temporary copies, never writing tui.toml. fm bus send without its text prints its usage instead of crashing (T-1543).
- foreman-ui pane replay: mods/foreman-ui/tools/pane_replay.py draws the Foreman pane from a recorded fm ui --json view with no live session, through the mod's own test kit and its fakes (render: text, or --json for the drawn tree), and twin diffs two runs in separate processes (--matrix adds a third in another time zone and locale); escape sequences, control and bidi characters from a view never reach the terminal, and a run past its timeout ends with every process it started. claude plugin test draws the synthetic fixture twice and checks the bytes match (T-1534).
- fm timeline exports a task's run from the ledger as Perfetto / Chrome trace JSON (the task, its steps and checks as spans, every event a mark, redacted again; kinds and timings only in a sensitive project) or, with --otlp, OTLP JSON; fm glance prints the status as one ASCII JSON line for shells and status bars (waybar's text, tooltip and class; ids and counts only in a sensitive project, an error line when the state can't be read, nothing created outside a project); fm lsp is a stdio-only Language Server stub that publishes a brief's proof gaps (criteria with no verify command or evidence, steps with no test run) and survives bad frames, lone surrogates and long lines. A worker of an fm code run may run fm glance and fm timeline without --out (T-1551).
- fm code app settings: b binds the next key pressed to a row's command (refusing quit, Esc, Ctrl-C, help, hush, settings wherever they are bound, and the last key that moves or opens a list), l moves panels between and within columns (one write and one undo per move), r tests a runner from the registry or code.toml by asking its CLI for --version with a timeout, stopped if the app quits; lists always move and open with the arrows, PgUp/PgDn and Enter; /foreman:settings keys | layout | runners shows the same (T-1511).
- fm replay judges labelled and real commands with Python's own path variables (PYTHONPATH, PYTHONSTARTUP…) cleared and then put back, so a replay from a shell that sets them gives the verdicts a clean hook would (T-1599).
- Safety ratchet: the guard is held to an allow-corpus of about 100 everyday commands, replayed with the labelled cases (a missing or thinned corpus fails the replay); fm evolve refuses the guard's own files, its replay and evolve code, and symlinked targets, reading the pinned commit; a guard that raises reports unable everywhere (replay, candidate scoring, the friction digest), never a pass (T-1517).
- fm lease take keeps tasks apart within one session: builder lanes share their main session's id, so another task's lease now refuses the take (naming that task) instead of being handed over; --warn keeps both claims, a task retakes its own, and a removed lane releases its task's leases (T-1588).
- fm --json output prints any value as text (fm settings list --json no longer crashes on a TOML datetime) and escapes DEL and C1 control characters (T-1589).
- Guard: git checkout -b NAME and git switch -c/--create NAME with no start point make a branch at HEAD and change no file, so they pass in Foreman's checkout and the home folder; with a start point, a pathspec, -B or -C they stay tree writes, and a hooks path set on the line still refuses (T-1598).
- fm code app: z hushes motion (full, reduced, off) for the app and the Claude Code pane alike; the spinner turns by what the agent is doing; a riso theme and palette on both surfaces (the pane's lane and type colours follow the palette now); a half-block rail once the terminal confirms the glyph's width; ? shows the keys as a poster; tui.toml messages never carry raw terminal escapes (T-1512).
- fm task evidence --repeat N runs a check up to 50 times within --timeout and records median, spread (MAD), min and max; any failing run fails it, timed-out runs stay out of the statistics. The release canary reruns a project that failed under high load on its own (one 10-minute budget for all reruns) and marks a pass there as a load flap, captured but never counted green. fm task receipt ID lists a task's changed files in reading order and shows its before/after numbers, --repeat medians included (T-1518).
- fm smoke --profile torture|empty|offline|slow3g|deuteranopia|protanopia|tritanopia|achromatopsia|frozen|motion|all crawls the web UI under hostile conditions, and plain fm smoke also shoots the visual-qa frame matrix (360/768/1440 wide, light and dark, plus a reduced-motion pass) into a per-commit folder named after each view, so a receipt can point at it. Frame and profile findings are notes unless --strict (or fm smoke set strict on); a Playwright with no browser is skipped with the reason, and a failed cmd or http check now fails fm smoke even then (T-1509).
- Settings you can share and take back: fm settings diff lists the keys you changed from the defaults and flags any nothing reads; fm settings export prints them as one share code, and fm settings import CODE checks every key and value before writing any (one bad key refuses the whole code; control characters are shown escaped) and writes only keys that differ; fm settings undo puts the last set or import back, leaving alone anything edited by hand since; all through the guard and the lock, and from /foreman:settings, which runs import only for a well-formed code (T-1510).
- fm check playbooks lints the playbook library: every file indexed (SKILL.md or its stage INDEX.md) and every index line live, SKILL.md under its 10,000-character budget, no shared titles or near-duplicates, a Use-when line and a numbered procedure in each, real fm lib show names, python blocks that run under python3 -I in an empty folder with memory and process limits (no-run opts out; trusted like the repo's tests), and a size ratchet with 10% headroom (fm check playbooks ratchet records growth and shrinkage); it also names playbooks no session read lately (T-1505).
- /foreman:directions: from one sentence about a product, six one-screen style directions drawn from the direction bank and the twelve looks, each different from the others and from the generic defaults (cream and terracotta, near-black with an acid accent, broadsheet hairlines, SaaS card kit, template chrome); the pick is saved as DESIGN.md with an audacity level, recorded with fm decide under full autonomy. Eleven skill descriptions were trimmed so the skill listing stays under its 2,000-character cap (T-1508).
- Hook note: a task's first UI edit (css, html, jsx, tsx, vue, svelte, astro) in a repo with no DESIGN.md gets one nudge toward a design direction (ui/design-direction.md, /foreman:directions when installed); once per task, never a block (T-1514).
- fm-reviewer: a design lens for UI diffs, with three personas: an art director (a point of view, or the generic tells fm design lint names), an accessibility reviewer (contrast, focus, motion, target size) and a first-time user. fm audit prep adds it at any tier when the diff touches UI (vendored, built and minified files aside), with the screenshots the task names; fm audit run runs it only then, so a backend diff costs nothing extra; advisory, never required. Reviewer lens headings must be exact ("## design", "## lens: design"), so "## Design notes" or "## Edge cases" no longer switch lens (T-1501).
- fm code app: a theme, palette or motion change in the settings screen shows at once on the screen behind it without being written, checked (guard included) as the write would be; any key within 10 seconds keeps it, otherwise the screen goes back and the file stays as it was (T-1573).
- Guard: interpreter code (python, pypy, node, perl, ruby, lua, R, julia, tcl; -c/-e, heredoc and here-string bodies, and the files named as their operands) that reads a home-folder credential file is refused like cat of it: a whole path, one joined from the home folder, a glob that reaches one, or after a leading cd (T-1460).
