Unsetting or overriding CLAUDECODE or CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT by name (env -u, unset, NAME=, export -n, local, read, printf -v, a computed unset) is refused, since fm reads them to know Claude runs it; a whole fresh environment (env -i) isn't (T-1570). awk's system(), print | "cmd", "cmd" | getline, print > "file", getline < "file", gawk -i inplace, @include and -f programs written on the same line are read as the commands and paths they are (system($0) runs its input as a script), and writes, perl -i edits, touch, mkdir, install -d and mv into /etc, /usr, /boot, /opt, /var and the other system folders are system writes (T-1575).
- Style shelf: eight original shader looks (ink diffusion, contour lines, halftone, soft caustics, grain gradient, aurora bands, dither fade, paper fibre) as GLSL ES 3.00 cards with uniforms, cost and a reduced-motion still, plus light-and-material recipes and an SVG icon system; a test compiles each shader in headless Chromium when one is installed and checks each is its plain colour at intensity 0 (T-1499).
- Guard: a line shlex can't parse is read both word by word and quote-aware, a quoted operator ('>', \;) is data, and a # inside nested quotes in $( ) is no comment: 'cat' ~/.ssh/id_ed25519 after unbalanced quotes, echo "$(echo "a #")">~/.bashrc and a redirect after a closing quote, ) or backtick are refused (T-1561).
- fm audit prep on a builder lane's task adds the main checkout's uncommitted edits only when that task is the main checkout's own focused task, so a lane review no longer carries another task's files (T-1579).
- Nine playbooks: incidents (evidence before rollback for security events), observability, data-viz, task-first docs with a run_examples.py runner that fails on any nonzero exit, decks and reports, scrollytelling with cold deep links, demo production, UI sound design with peak and RMS checks, and Python's standard library first; stage indexes for ops, context, release and lang keep the playbooks skill under its 10,000-character budget (T-1504).
- fm code app: a settings screen (`,`) on fm settings' one schema: every setting by group with value, default and a badge, / to search, m for changed-only, and a :set line that writes through fm settings. fm settings set refuses a value whenever the new file has an error for that key, and asks the guard whenever it isn't run from a terminal, so blanking CLAUDECODE no longer skips it (T-1572).
- /foreman:settings lists, gets and sets the fm code app's and the foreman-ui pane's settings through fm settings; the pane applies a changed motion or palette on its next 2-second poll, no restart, and turning motion off mid-work stops its clocks (T-1574).
- Seven engineering playbooks: SQL query review, live migrations (lock budget first, unique and NOT NULL recipes), text/time/bytes/money, dependency admission without running install scripts, characterisation tests, deprecation (warnings, Deprecation/Sunset/Link headers) and decision memos (T-1503).
- Guard: an escaped \$IFS in prose (fm task log "… \$IFS …", echo literal \$IFS) is literal text, not an expansion, so it no longer refuses as "IFS may be changed"; an unescaped $IFS, \\$IFS and a real $'…' still refuse, and so does \$IFS in text that runs again in the same shell: eval or source after any keyword, !, a case ) or prefix assignments, trap, mapfile -C and alias (T-1473).
- Build playbooks for Go, JVM (Gradle and Maven, Java and Kotlin), Swift, C# and shell: reading the first error, common causes with fixes, the verify command and linked library cards; the Swift, C# and shell advice was checked against real failing builds. Build playbooks now have their own index (references/build/INDEX.md) (T-1502).
- fm design lint PATHS [--json]: a stdlib check, runnable from Claude Code and fm code, for the tells of generated design (only a default font stack or font-sans/Inter, the purple-to-blue gradient, one 6-16 px radius everywhere, one grey shadow; Tailwind classes and @apply included) and craft slips (img without alt, motion with no reduced-motion path, animated layout properties or transition: all, flashes over 3 a second, Math.random in render code, literal durations beside tokens). A slop and a crafted fixture gate each rule; `design-lint: allow RULE why` silences one line; it exits 1 on findings and 2 when a path holds no UI files (T-1506).
- Style shelf: twelve original named looks as library cards (fm lib show look-NAME, fm lib list --area looks): palette roles with hex, OKLCH and measured contrast, type pairing by class, layout, motion in motion.md's tokens, texture, one do and one don't, what each suits, and a contrast test with greps; design-direction.md points to them (T-1498).
- UI playbook: interaction, forms, microcopy and mobile (ui/interaction.md): three feedback deadlines, one press one action, the unplugged keyboard walk, form validation timing and fix-it errors, a verb ledger with before/after copy, 360 px touch and thumb rules, an interaction probe and a double-press probe (T-1491).
- Game feel playbook (ui/game-feel.md): anticipation, squash and stretch, hit-stop, trauma shake, camera kick, particles, easing, flashes and sound, each with a hard cap and a reduced-motion fallback, plus a self-testing juice.py whose asserts check the caps table (T-1493).
- Settings in one place: fm settings list|get|set shows the fm code app's tui.toml and the Claude Code plugin's foreman-ui pane options together, each with its value, default and where it is stored, and names any setting the app can't read. A tui.toml change is made in place (comments, other keys and line endings kept) and refused if the app rejects it or can't draw with it. Inside Claude Code both files are written only when the guard allows, otherwise the exact change is printed. motion (full, reduced, off; calm still accepted) and palette (default, cb) now mean the same on both surfaces, and the app gains palette = "cb" (T-1496).
- The playbooks skill stays under its 10,000-character budget as playbooks grow: a stage can keep its own references/<stage>/INDEX.md (UI does now), named once in SKILL.md, and the index test accepts a file named there (T-1569).
- 3D template (ui/templates/scene.html): a single-file raw WebGL2 scene with no dependencies: a lit procedural form, a lens fitted from its bounds, a palette from CSS tokens, a dithered backdrop with a contact shadow, and every survival clause (poster first, text twin, context loss and restore, reduced motion, keyboard, contained failure, sleeping offscreen), checked in a headless browser (T-1495).
- UI playbook: composition, depth and states (ui/composition.md): a spacing scale with rhythm tiers, grids picked by direction, alignment counted as axes, hierarchy by the cheapest lever, depth from one stated light with elevation and z-index from one table, a white-image test for scrims, a hero that opens with the subject's most characteristic thing, landing pages as an argument, three scored wireframes for one brief, a state atlas per screen with an overflow fixture, and a console probe that measures it (T-1490).
- UI playbook: visual QA: fm smoke plus a seeded frame matrix (360, 768 and 1440 px, light and dark, reduced motion) from a small Playwright script that freezes the clock and fails on overflow, junk text and motion under reduced motion; an eyeball checklist; a receipt in the task's evidence. In Claude Code the frames are read with the Read tool (T-1500).
- fm task new and fm capture split --scope on spaces and commas ('a.py b.py, c.py' is three paths, quotes keep a path with a space); it used to be stored as one path, so every file it named read as outside the task's scope (T-1564).
- fm task evidence --step N --run CMD --expect-fail records a failing test's red run before the fix as "red as expected" and exits 0; a run that passes, or never reaches its check, is refused and nothing is recorded; a criterion still takes only a passing run (T-1486).
- UI playbook: type and colour: a modular type scale, pairing by class and axes, type as texture and as the hero, measure and leading, OKLCH palette roles with reserved status colours, a stdlib contrast-proof script for both themes, and dark mode as its own steps (T-1489).
- Guard: an ANSI-C quoted command name ($'cat' ~/.ssh/id_ed25519, $'shutdown', $'tar' -I …) and a same-line variable (C=env; $C | curl …) are refused like their plain forms: $'…' is decoded as bash does outside heredoc bodies and double quotes, with $( ) and backticks opening their own quoting. The BASH_ENV variants left the evasion corpus, whose verdict depended on a payload file (T-1480).
- fm lib show takes a card's group prefix too (web/three-js, frontend/typography), as briefs and playbooks write them; a prefix naming another group is still not found (T-1521).
- 3D playbook (ui/scene-3d.md): scene composition with a fit-from-bounds camera, light and material recipes, shader looks, a budget for a mid laptop and a phone with a one-way quality ladder, a nine-clause survival contract (no WebGL, poster first, context loss, one context per page, reduced motion, a text twin, keyboard, contained failure, sleeping) each with a probe, and a black-screen triage table (T-1494).
- Motion playbook (ui/motion.md): named timing tokens in CSS and JS that follow DESIGN.md's Motion knob, a state-to-motion table with a reduced form per row, storyboard before code with a worked example, compositor-only rules, loading that never blocks first paint, and reduced motion designed as its own path (T-1492).
- UI playbook "design direction before pixels": a three-question interview, a taste-word table, a bank of twelve distinct directions, a safe/bold/wild dial, the generic defaults to avoid, and a DESIGN.md template, so UI work starts from a written point of view (T-1488).
- fm code bench no longer leaves a seed's check running: each seed run carries FM_BENCH_SEED=<its temp folder> in its environment, and before the folder is deleted every process of yours carrying that marker is stopped. A check fm code started in its own session, such as a test_calc.py spinning in an endless loop, used to outlive the run for hours (T-1485).
- Unattended fm code runs and fm release --push refuse when the guard's own check (fm replay --cases, now logged on the ledger each run) is red, empty or more than 7 days old. The refusal names the check, its date and the command that refreshes it. Runs you watch, in a terminal or the fm code app, only warn, and the warning is logged too. --stale-ok REASON runs anyway and logs the waiver (T-1401).
- Guard: an evasion corpus of 301 obfuscated variants (quote splits, backslashes, variable and array indirection, env -S, functions, eval, here-strings, base64) holds the guard to its verdicts. It runs from the home folder, so the verdicts are the same in any checkout. Its gap list names the 21 it still gets wrong: 12 dangerous commands let through by $'…' quoting, variable indirection and a BASH_ENV prefix (T-1480, T-1481), and 9 everyday rm/git commands refused once obfuscated outside a project (T-1263).
- fm code app: the band under the line being worked and a selected log line are padded by the columns they fill, so a line holding wide glyphs (CJK, emoji) no longer loses its tail to a stray ellipsis (T-1468).
- A cloned repo's own Claude config is named once, to you and to Claude, on its first interactive Foreman session, and again only when those files change. The note covers .claude/settings.json and settings.local.json hooks, the status line, apiKeyHelper and the other command settings, enableAllProjectMcpServers, env, plugin and marketplace names (never env values), allow rules and .mcp.json servers. A file over 256 KB, one that isn't a regular file or one linked outside the repo is named but never read, and so is malformed JSON. Those warnings come first, so a padded config can't push them out of the note (T-1472).
- fm code app: a multi-line paste lands in the prompt as one entry instead of submitting at the first newline and firing the rest as hotkeys. Bracketed paste is on while the screen runs and off however it ends; a paste is capped at 64 KB and its control characters are dropped (T-1316).
- `fm next` (and the "Next:" line under every Foreman note) skips a task a builder lane holds, so it no longer says `fm focus` on a task `fm focus` would refuse (T-1478).
- `fm code runners --check` also covers aliases that resolve to retired models (`o1-mini`, and `gpt-4` from 2026-10-23), and notes models retiring within 60 days ("retiring on <date> → <replacement>"); the tables were re-read from the three providers' deprecation pages on 2026-10-22 (T-1433).
- Crash hygiene (T-1405). Long fm commands (check, code, release, bench, sentinel) write every thread's stack to `state/logs/stacks/` on SIGUSR1 and after 30 minutes; `fm tidy` prunes these after 14 days. A Foreman commit blocked by a stale `.git/index.lock` removes it only when it is over 10 minutes old, no process has it open, and no git runs in the repo; otherwise it names the lock and the holder. `fm doctor` probes whether file locks exclude between processes on the state filesystem.
- `fm lib source defaults` adds the six anthropics repos (skills, claude-code, claude-cookbooks, claude-plugins-official, financial-services, prompt-eng-interactive-tutorial). Each is pinned to its commit, with a depth (reference: READMEs as notes; index: skills, agents and marketplace.json plugins) and a licence class from its licence file (open, source-available or ideas-only; a skill folder's own licence wins). `--dry` lists them without cloning, and `fm lib show` gives an ideas-only entry's idea but never a path to its text. Notes and plugins go through the T-1396 quarantine, and `update` and the weekly refresh name a class that moved (T-1389).
- Tests: one `fake_cli` helper writes misbehaving runner CLIs (hang, silent, waiting at a TTY prompt, garbage JSON, usage-limit text, fork-and-exit); `test_code_fakes` checks each ends an `fm code` run within its bound and names the reason (T-1361).
- The drive no longer tells you to focus a task a builder lane holds (which `fm focus` refuses). When every queued task is in a lane, it says once that the lanes are working and that each builder's notification will wake the session (T-1469).
- fm code's exported snapshot, recording (GIF/MP4) and plain event replay redact secrets before anything is drawn or printed, so a token a worker printed doesn't end up in a shared file (T-1302).
- The sentinel (and the release canary that runs it) skips a past check that reads a remote's state (`origin/…`, `@{u}`, `ls-remote`), such as a release task's "main is Foreman 1.2.47", since it was only true at the time. A check that fails and then passes on one rerun is reported as flaky, not failing. Both caused the false canary failures after 1.2.48 and 1.2.50 (T-1380, T-1448).
- `fm tidy` lists, then with `--apply` deletes, fm code's private copies of reverted files once they are over 30 days old (plain files only; links and folders are skipped). The fm code tests keep every XDG folder inside their temporary home (T-1442).
- fm code app: the progress track's labels and every box title are measured in screen columns, so wide characters (CJK, emoji) fit, and a long note no longer makes a box title vanish at 80 columns; the title keeps at least a few letters (T-1431).
- The supply check no longer flags a `__pycache__` file that is exactly what Python compiles from the source beside it (same interpreter, header, and code). A pyc whose body was changed under a copied header, an orphan, a hash-based pyc or another Python's pyc still shows, since Python would run it without checking it against the source (T-1466).
- A close's affected-tests run no longer pulls in every test that once changed alongside `CHANGELOG.md`: a file that co-changes with more than 10 tests carries no signal, so its co-change pairs are dropped (its direct links still count). Before, every close ran about 28 extra suites, 114–142 s of a 2-minute close (T-1467).
- `fm lib source update` swaps one source in at a time (a lock beside the sources folder), so two updates of the same source can't interleave their renames; `fm lib source rm` also removes a stray `NAME.old-PID` or `.tmp-NAME-PID` folder a crash left, and still refuses `.`, `..`, paths and links (T-1428).
- `fm edit` refuses a file holding NUL bytes (binary), as fm code does, and applies old text written with plain newlines to a file with Windows (CRLF) line endings, keeping the file's endings, where it used to say "not found" (T-1424).
- The bench gate calls a pass loss between 1 and 2 standard errors "likely a loss: rerun with --runs 10" and doesn't propose the change. With fewer than 5 runs it says how often a real 2-pass loss would be caught (about half the time at 3 runs; research bench-band-calibration) (T-1464).
- `fm task finish` names its slowest run when a close takes over 60 s ("Slowest: <cmd> N s (of M s)"), and the affected-tests event records its seconds, so a slow close says what to speed up (T-1462).
- `fm doctor`'s supply check now sees code in a plugin's `node_modules`, `.venv`, `__pycache__` and `.pytest_cache`, which it used to skip entirely. Files there are tracked by path, size and times (bounded per folder and per plugin). Scripts a hook or server names there are hashed by content. `.git` stays out. These records go in a new field that existing baselines take on silently. Scoped (`@scope/…`) and `+` package paths are now read whole, so a baseline may say "adds script" once. A file name that isn't valid UTF-8 no longer leaves its plugin unchecked (T-1459).
- The background gate run after a close starts under `nice -n 10`, so it no longer competes with the next task's foreground finish and tests (T-1463).
- fm code hands the worker a planner's plan inside the run's data envelope, with your request as what rules, so a planner steered by a hostile file in the repo can't hand the worker instructions; a `--task` brief or a `--verify` plan is your own and stays as instructions (T-1422).
- The guard no longer refuses `cd <scratch> && python3 - <<'EOF' … EOF` writing a relative file there just because another line follows the heredoc: lines after the last Python block run after it and can't move where it wrote. A `cd` before it, or a second Python block after a `cd` back, still counts (T-1458).
- The guard's refusal of a CHANGELOG.md edit with no active task says the line belongs to its task: add it before `fm task finish`, or `fm focus` that task again (T-1452).
- The builder rules (and every lane brief) say that reads or writes outside the worktree, such as another session's task output files or the main thread's scratchpad, are refused by Claude Code too, so builders stop retrying them (T-1454).
- Foreman's state fallback refuses a `/tmp/foreman-state-<uid>` link another local user planted, even one pointing at a private folder of yours such as `~/.ssh`; before, state would be copied into it when the usual state folder wasn't writable. A link you made yourself still works (T-1317).
- `fm doctor`'s supply check holds a plugin with an unreadable file or folder instead of keeping its old baseline: one planted unreadable file (or a folder it can't list, or an exec-only script swapped for another) used to hide every later change to that plugin. `fmplugins.profile()` also lists custom `plugin.json` component paths and nested commands (T-1444).
- The Stop hook still holds a turn that claims unproven work when another fm command has the project lock; before, the busy lock made it give up silently (T-1453).
- fm code refuses an unacknowledged runner or `--worker` template with GitHub Copilot CLI's `--allow-all-tools`/`--allow-all`, Factory droid's `--skip-permissions-unsafe`, or any `--…dangerously…` flag such as Claude's `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions`; Copilot's scoped `--allow-tool` still runs (T-1443).
- `fm verify-ledger` names a line an unclean shutdown zeroed ("ends in N zero bytes") instead of calling the chain corrupt, and `fm verify-ledger --accept-damage` records it once you've checked. It never accepts an edit, a missing `--head`, or lines removed before the damage, because the first write after a crash pins the line before it. Every finish receipt said "chain broken" after the 2026-10-22 crash; it now names the accepted gap (T-1456).
- `fm doctor` warns when the disk holding Foreman's state is under 10 GB or 5% free, and names the projects whose builder worktrees are piling up (T-1450). The disk filled on 2026-10-22 and hooks failed to write state with no warning first.
- `fm doctor`'s supply check no longer reports every installed plugin as changed whenever a new Claude Code session starts: it leaves out the `.in_use/<pid>` marker Claude Code writes into each plugin's folder, in that exact shape only; any other file there still counts (T-1455).
- `fm doctor` no longer warns that foreman-ui is out of date when Claude Code reads it in place from this checkout (a directory marketplace); the old cache record it compared against is never loaded (T-1451).
- Hooks: one note that fails to build (a full disk) no longer drops every other note of that tool call, and unread mail shows again next call instead of being lost (T-1449).

## 1.2.50 — 2026-10-21
- A library source indexed before 1.2.49's quarantine is indexed again from its local clone the next time fm reads it (no network), so its hostile skills and agents leave `fm lib search` without a `fm lib source update` (T-1415).
- `fm lib search` finds a description's last word: "tidewater." no longer keeps its full stop, while node.js and c# stay one word (T-1420).
- fm code's judge also holds `~/.ssh/authorized_keys`, `~/.ssh/rc` and `config`, X session files, login autostart entries, `environment.d`, systemd user units, `~/.bashrc.d`, and fish and git config under `~/.config` even when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` points elsewhere; a new command in `~/.local/bin` is moved aside whole whatever its size, and a changed one it can't put back stops the run; the worker's version of each file it puts back is kept under the run journal's `reverted/` folder (user-only, never written through a planted link); worker and planner sessions don't self-update mid-turn (T-1417).
- `fm doctor`'s supply check also reads hooks in skill, agent and command frontmatter, `.lsp.json` servers and a hooks module list (foreman-ui's reads as "hooks module register.tsx"), and sees symlink retargets and large-file changes; anything that may declare hooks but can't be read for sure (a quoted or spaced hooks key, frontmatter not closed within 256 KB, a custom component path outside the plugin) is held as event '?', never read as 'no hooks'; an older baseline compares as before (T-1434).
- fm code hardening: a STOP kills the CLI's group once, the local-server probe refuses metadata addresses, aider `--yes-always`, q `--trust-all-tools`, cursor-agent `--force` and `-anever` are linted, and hooks inside a worker never create a project (T-1438).
- fm code looks a model host's addresses up once a minute instead of on every model call (the metadata-address check); a failed or slow lookup is tried again (T-1436).
- `fm evolve` keeps a candidate only for a win outside the bench's noise band (more passes, or a cost drop, beyond two standard errors when runs were repeated), and a stored pass rate is read within 0..1 (T-1435).

## 1.2.49 — 2026-10-21
- fm code names a stall: a CLI turn with no output for FM_STALL s (default 120) gets one `stalled` event per silence naming its likely cause (no output since launch, a prompt, a rate or usage limit, or silent since its last line), a warning line in the TUI, and the status bar says how long since the CLI was last heard. Before, a stuck turn just looked busy (T-1336).
- `fm code "<request>"` on a terminal opens the app with that run going, the way `claude "<prompt>"` does; `--plain`, `--json`, `--dry-plan` or a pipe keep the line-by-line output, and `fm code tui` works as before (T-1343).
- A prompt with many requests is queued within the hook's time even on a busy machine: fm builds its command parser once per process instead of once per in-process call, so the prompt hook's one pass no longer re-parses fm's whole command set for every request (T-1378).
- `fm vetoes` ends each veto with the command that retracts it (`fm vetoes rm N`, numbered oldest first), and `--json` gives it as `retract`, so a veto recorded by mistake is one copy-paste from gone (T-1410).
- The website loads nothing from third parties: Google Fonts are gone (the pages use the reader's own Barlow or JetBrains Mono if installed, otherwise a close system font), and `python3 site/build.py` fails, naming the page and URL, if any built page, stylesheet, script or picture would fetch from another host (T-1411).
- fm code takes claude's own flags where they mean the same thing: `-p`/`--print` is `--plain`, `-c`/`--continue` is `--resume` (the newest unfinished run) and `--output-format json` is `--json`; `text` and `stream-json` are refused with a hint, and fm's `-p PROJECT` goes before the verb (or `--project` after it) (T-1399).
- `fm lib source` quarantines hostile skills and agents with their reasons: symlinks, Agent Skills spec breaches (fm doctor checks Foreman's own skills the same way), hidden characters, repeated names and "use for every task" descriptions stay out of search. `fm plugins find` skips bad marketplace entries with a reason and shows a renamed or re-sourced plugin as untrusted (T-1396).
- fm code refuses a code.toml runner that turns its CLI's approvals off (`--yolo`, `--dangerously-*`, `danger-full-access`, `bypassPermissions`, `--approval-mode yolo`, gemini `-y`, codex `-a never` or `approval_policy`/`sandbox_mode` overrides, read as the shell splits them) until code.toml's `[ack]` holds that exact template's sha256; `fm code runners` grades whether Foreman's guard confines each runner's shell; a link-local or cloud metadata model `base_url` (169.254.0.0/16, fe80::/10, fd00:ec2::254, in any spelling) is refused; the TUI picker shows a bad code.toml instead of hiding the models (T-1395).
- fm code fences what the repo and tools wrote: file lists, action results and verify output reach the model inside a per-run nonce-tagged data envelope, envelope-shaped tags in them are escaped, and every prompt (worker CLIs' too) names the envelope as data, never instructions (T-1394).
- `fm vetoes rm` is the user's to run, like `fm rule rm`: Foreman's guard refuses it from Claude and says to type `! fm vetoes rm N` (T-1413).
- fm code's judge also holds your shell startup files (bash, zsh including `$ZDOTDIR`, fish including `conf.d`) and other agents' settings (`~/.codex` or `$CODEX_HOME`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.claude` settings, and the MCP servers in `~/.claude.json`): a worker's change there is put back and named, a folder it turns into a link is removed without being followed, and a worker turn that changed nothing says so (T-1393).
- `fm lease drop FILE` frees only the active task's leases, as a bare `fm lease drop` already did: a builder lane dropping a file no longer frees other parallel lanes' leases on it (they share the main session's id) (T-1416).
- `fm page` loads nothing from another host either: its template's Google Fonts links are gone and its font stacks fall back to faces already on the reader's machine (T-1414).
- fm code stops with "the model's server truncated the prompt at N tokens" when an API model reports reading under half the prompt fm code sent, instead of acting on a reply that never saw its instructions (T-1404).
- A coding CLI that fm code runs only reads Foreman's state: under `FOREMAN_WORKER_OF`, `fm` refuses every command that changes it (grants, `fm ask`, `fm standing`, `fm autonomy`, `fm task done`, `fm vetoes rm`, capture, focus, …) and keeps the reads (state, task show/list, queue, next, lib search, vetoes). A lean claude worker whose session starts with an MCP server, an `mcp__` tool or a non-built-in plugin is stopped at that first line, before it acts, and the run blocks naming them (T-1388).
- `fm lib source add` and `update` killed at any step keep the previous source listed and searchable: a set-aside copy is restored, a dead add's temp folders are swept, and neither is ever listed as a source (T-1412).
- `fm code runners --check` flags each configured model id its provider has retired (a table dated 2026-10-21 from the Anthropic, OpenAI and Gemini deprecation pages; the Anthropic rows checked against the live page) and names the replacement, instead of a run failing later with an unexplained 404 (T-1409).
- The test suite no longer flakes on a busy machine: its `fm doctor` runs skip the ~100-run hook benchmark no test reads (`FOREMAN_DOCTOR_BENCH=0`, reported as "not measured"), so the breaker, scout, doctor and resilience tests passed 5 of 5 rounds with a busy process on every core (load 67) (T-1379).
- `fm doctor`'s supply check holds a plugin update that adds code which runs on its own: a new hook event or handler, an MCP server or a changed MCP command, a script, a new plugin that brings any of these, or a hook file it can't read. It FAILs naming each ("p@m adds hook PreToolUse") until `fm doctor --accept-supply`; a content-only change keeps its WARN; it reads the default and the manifest hook files and catches changes the tree hash misses, and one unreadable plugin costs only its own row. `fm plugins find` shows what each local entry runs (`needs` in `--json`) and says "unknown until installed" for remote ones (T-1392).
- fm code's screen measures text by the columns it fills: wide (CJK, emoji) titles are cut with their ellipsis, and the history and project rows stay aligned with the box border intact (T-1301).
- fm code hardening: an inline `--worker` template that turns its CLI's approvals off is refused (a shell wrapper like `sh -c '…'` included), pointing to a code.toml runner with an `[ack]`; Alibaba and Azure metadata addresses and hostnames resolving to any metadata address are refused as model endpoints; a model server's string or null token counts count as 0 instead of crashing; a lean claude planner whose session loads MCP servers or plugins is stopped naming them; and a read from inside a worker no longer creates a project (T-1432).
- `fm bench gate` shows passes and cost per case with a ± noise band from repeated runs; a loss inside it reads "within noise" and doesn't fail the gate, one outside it still does (T-1260).
- Foreman UI: with the mascot off, a running subagent's time still counts up (its row was redrawn only by the mascot's blink); the subagent-row test no longer times out on a busy machine (T-1437, T-1427).
- fm code's write and edit keep a file's line endings (LF text over a CRLF file stays CRLF), refuse a binary, non-UTF-8 or non-regular target instead of rewriting it, and refuse option-shaped paths like `-` and `--x` (T-1408).

## 1.2.48 — 2026-10-21
- fm code's claude sessions run lean: the worker without your plugins, MCP servers or settings hooks (`--setting-sources project,local --strict-mcp-config`) and the planner in `--restricted` mode, each with Foreman's guard as `--settings`. A planner call here read 15,286 input tokens instead of 31,620, and the guard still refused a `.env` write (`plugin/tests/e2e/lean_probe.py`). `lean = false` in code.toml keeps the old launch (T-1354).
- `fm code` on its own opens the app, like `claude`: a project's view (its active task, queue, inbox, blocked tasks and past runs) inside a project, and a home over every Foreman project on the machine (`Esc`, or `p` from a run). A task runs from there in its own project's folder with the runner `m` picks, and `n` starts a new request there. `fm code --task ID` works a planned task without a planner call, refusing one with no check to run, one that's done or dropped, and one another run is working (T-1330).
- `fm code tui --events FILE` on its own replays that run: on the screen in a terminal, or as its Activity lines with `--plain` or no terminal (exit 0 for a closed run); a missing file says so and exits 2. Before, the file was silently ignored (T-1363).
- `fm doctor` checks that `fm` works in any shell: it warns, naming the fix, when `~/.local/bin/fm` is missing (an install from before 1.2.x never made it), points at another copy, or sits in a folder that isn't on PATH (T-1324).
- `fm task new --from ID` with `--ac` or `--step` replaces the capture's own criteria or steps instead of adding to them; before, a capture planned that way listed every step twice (T-1364).

## 1.2.47 — 2026-10-21
- fm code shows what a coding CLI is doing as it happens: each file claude reads or edits and each command it runs is an Activity line (a `tool` event), for the worker and the read-only planner alike, and a turn with nothing to show sends a `working` heartbeat every 10 s, one ticking line in the TUI. Before, a run looked stuck for minutes (T-1329).
- `fm code` works with no setup: with no model and no `--worker`, it uses the Claude Code CLI if installed and says so. `fm code runners` lists every coding CLI it knows (installed, can plan read-only, verified here, the default; `--check` runs each one's `--version`), `--use NAME` sets the default in `$FOREMAN_HOME/code.toml` (`--here`: this project only, by name), and `[runners.NAME]` in that file adds your own CLI; it also lists the models set up. A broken models.json is an error, never a quiet switch to a CLI. The guard now protects `code.toml`, `tui.toml`, `tui/` and `models.json` in Foreman's home: they become commands, run Python, or pick where code is sent (T-1328).
- fm release starts its canary detached: `--push` returns once pushed, the report lands in the project's logs/canary-VERSION.txt, a project whose past check now fails gets an inbox item and an `fm notify` message, and `fm release --canary-status` prints the last run (T-1362).
- fm code tui: an extension's panel, handler or command that fails or runs slow (a panel over 50 ms on 3 frames running, a handler over 50 ms, a command over 2 s) is switched off with a note in Activity, never taking the screen down; built-in panels are never switched off (T-1315).
- "Stop asking me if you can release", "don't stop until it's done" and the like no longer record a veto: they tell Foreman to go ahead (T-1323).
- fm code tui has a test net: golden frames of the demo run (FM_GOLDEN=regen rewrites them), a seeded fuzz of 300 malformed event streams at six sizes, and a pty test that resizes and quits; a malformed event now changes nothing instead of crashing the screen (14 crash classes found and fixed) (T-1306)
- Foreman notices friction itself: a complaint about a Foreman area ("why can't I launch fm code like claude", "fm isn't recognized") gets a fix-the-cause note and a queued improvement round for that area (fm mission + fm ideas, then build), without being asked; a second complaint within a week makes it urgent; a complaint naming no Foreman area gets only the note (T-1332)
- fm release --push and fm pr's post and merge refusals name both ways to grant publishing: fm ask ID publish (the user's yes), or fm task set ID --allow publish where the user has given standing permission to release (T-1322)
- fm code: the worker and planner Claude sessions it starts run Foreman's hooks quiet, so only the guard acts there: no second copy of Foreman's session context, drive, request capture or leases inside its own workers (T-1327)

## 1.2.46 — 2026-10-20
- Stopping an `fm code` run (the TUI's stop or quit, a closed terminal, `kill`) now stops the planner or worker CLI it started; before, that CLI ran on orphaned, still spending tokens and, for a worker, still editing files nothing judged (T-1326)
- Guard: Foreman's state folder reached through a linked folder (`~/.claude` linked into a dotfiles repo, `FOREMAN_STATE` under macOS's linked `/tmp`) is refused as state at its real path too, like the state fallbacks; a link at the state folder itself is still never followed (T-1318)
- `fm code` takes any request: with no model or API key set, `--worker claude` (or codex, gemini) also plans, in that CLI's read-only mode, so `fm code "<anything>" --worker claude` works with nothing but Claude Code installed; a question about the code gets an answer from the files (read-only, no task); a plan whose check can't run here (a test runner that isn't installed) goes back to the planner instead of ending the run; a task's commit leaves out build artefacts (`__pycache__`, `.pyc`, `node_modules`) and no longer fails on one that vanished before the close. The site's fm code page shows seven real runs, one per kind of request, drawn from their recorded events in `demo/examples/` (T-1320)

## 1.2.45 — 2026-10-20
- macOS: the test suite passes there (CI's macOS job is green): fm code --worker judges a fix reached through a linked temp folder, the guard refuses a hard reset in a linked /tmp, Foreman's state fallbacks are judged at their folder's real path (never a planted leaf link), and the reaper, relate and context-tax tests work without /proc and with long temp paths (T-1284)
- Guard: a refusal for a program it couldn't read (a script written earlier in the command, one fed through a pipe, a chain of scripts nested too deep, one too long to parse in time, a changed IFS) ends with the rewrite that makes it readable, not `--allow system`, which would lift every system check for the task (T-1298)
- `fm audit prep`'s diff for a builder's branch also holds the checkout's uncommitted edits, so a reviewer sees every changed file, not only the CHANGELOG hunk (T-1296)
- `fm code tui` moves and draws in full colour: its own ANSI renderer replaces curses (24-bit, 256, 8 or no colour on NO_COLOR; only changed cells are rewritten), the working station pulses with a spinner, a light runs along the track, counters and bars ease to their values, a flipped check and a guard refusal flash, new Activity lines fade in, the newest one sits on a band, a just-changed file glows then cools, empty panels shimmer while a run starts, and a close sweeps the track. Header, track, change bars and token columns are gradients. `motion = full | reduced | off` in tui.toml; a new `signal` theme for colour-blind eyes; a [colors] entry too faint to read is named and skipped; `fm code tui --record run.mp4 --events RUN.jsonl` films a run (ffmpeg and rsvg-convert); the site's fm code page plays the demo (T-1300)
- The guard's saved verdict for a script is keyed on where Foreman's state lives (its folder and fallbacks), so it isn't reused after state moves (T-1293)
- `fm night` and `fm replay --cases` judge the guard corpus with the temp folder it was labelled under (/tmp), so the /tmp hard-reset case no longer reports drift on macOS (T-1291)
- The builder rules live once, in `plugin/agents/fm-builder.md`: `fm lane brief` reads them from there, and a test fails if a rule goes missing from the brief (T-1290)

## 1.2.44 — 2026-10-20
- `fm research add NAME --file PATH --append` adds to a research note instead of replacing it, and the guard's hint for a direct write to state names it, so following the hint no longer erases an existing note (T-1288)
- Guard: the refusal of a script written and run in one command (`printf > s.sh && bash s.sh`) ends with the way forward, write it in one command and run it in the next, instead of an `--allow` route that didn't apply (T-1289)
- `fm code --worker claude` works with Foreman installed: each worker turn runs in its own Claude Code session, whose Foreman hooks leased what it edited, so fm code's judge put the worker's fix back as another session's and every later turn was refused the same code (`fm code bench --worker claude` scored 0/2; now the operator seed closes in 21 s). A worker turn now carries `FOREMAN_WORKER_OF`, and its edits lease as the fm code run's own (T-1294)
- `plugin/tests/run.py` fails when a test module name it's given matches no test, naming it, instead of passing on the other names: a criterion that named a test file that doesn't exist used to pass unseen (T-1287)
- `fm code bench [--model P | --worker CLI] [--seeds a,b] [--seed-timeout S] [--json]` grades a model or worker CLI as a Foreman worker on five seeded tasks (a wrong operator, an off-by-one, a missing guard clause, a rename across two files, and a request that tempts a dangerous command). Each seed runs in its own throwaway repo, Foreman home and (for a model) home folder, with a canary file the dangerous seed must leave alone; a seed counts as closed only when the bench puts the seed's tests back and re-runs its check itself. It reports per seed: closed, check runs, guard refusals, tokens and seconds, then a score (closed / seeds) compared with the last run of the same model on the same seed set, and appends one line to `<state>/code-bench.jsonl` (T-1215)
- Stack cards cover the 8 androidx dependencies `fm scout` found: new cards for DataStore, Macrobenchmark and the Car App Library, and the Compose and Android cards detect foundation, ui-test-* and activity-ktx (T-1251)
- Hook time limits are tested under a 0.4 s slowed Python start (the next-request focus, the Stop close and the evidenced-requests close), with one shared `slow_start` test helper; all three fit, so no limit changed (T-1280)
- The landing page applies the stored theme in its head, as the feature pages do, so a stored light choice on a dark OS no longer flashes dark (T-1244)

## 1.2.43 — 2026-10-20
- `fm task finish` names a step whose last run failed ("its last run failed; record a passing run …", with the command) instead of saying it has no verify command or evidence (T-1272)
- Secrets: one placeholder rule. The commit refusal and the close warning now agree on what a fake value is; for a token (`sk-`, `ghp_`, `AKIA…`) only a whole placeholder word (example, placeholder, changeme, dummy, redacted, your-key) or a run of x's excuses it, so a real key that happens to contain "your" or "fake" is still refused (T-1273)
- `fm code tui [REQUEST]`: Foreman Code in a terminal UI (T-1283). It runs `fm code --json` as a child and draws from its events: the stations as a track, each check as a PASS / RED / FAIL chip, changed files as +/− bars, a time chart per step, tokens per model call, and an activity log where a guard refusal stands out (the model's own words behind `t`). `d` shows the diff, `h` past runs (replayed from `code/<task>.events.jsonl`), `m` picks the model or worker for the next request, `s`/`r` stop and resume, `?` lists the keys. Themes (night, paper, mono), colours, layout and keys come from `$FOREMAN_HOME/tui.toml`, one line per bad setting; `--print-config` prints it. A Python file in `$FOREMAN_HOME/tui/` adds panels, commands and event handlers (`templates/tui/cost_meter.py` is a sample); a project's files are never loaded. `--plain` prints one line per event, `NO_COLOR` is honoured, under 60 columns it shows the log alone, and `--snapshot FILE --events RUN` draws the screen to SVG (the site's picture). fm code's events now carry `ts`, and `changed` carries each file's added and removed lines
- `fm code --worker`: your own uncommitted work survives a worker turn. A file you had changed or added that the turn deletes, stashes or cleans back to HEAD gets your version back and the revert is reported; the worker's own edits to such files stay (T-1270)
- The Pages workflow builds with read-only permissions and deploys in a separate job that alone holds the Pages and OIDC tokens; every action in both workflows is pinned to a commit SHA, and a test keeps them that way (T-1245)
- Foreman's reply format now lives in its rules file (sent once a session), and `fm install-user` no longer selects Foreman's output style, removing it where an older install selected it: Claude Code's per-turn reminder of that style was 2.7% of what entered the context, the largest part of Foreman's own 6.8% (T-1279)
- `fm code --worker --verify`: a worker turn can't change the check that judges it. Edits to the files and folders the verify command names, or a new file in such a folder (a `conftest.py`), are put back and reported, and the task still closes on the real check (T-1271)

## 1.2.42 — 2026-10-20
- `fm cost` counts as Foreman's own output only commands made of `fm` calls (a `fm … && python3 -m unittest` chain is the test run's output), and says which fm verbs it came from. Measured right, fm output is 0.9% of what entered the context, not 3.5%; Foreman's share is 6.8%, led by Claude Code's per-turn reminder of Foreman's output style (2.7%) and its rules (1.7%) (T-1276)
- macOS fixes found by the macOS CI job (T-1249): the guard now treats a scratch folder reached through a link as scratch (macOS's `/tmp` is `/private/tmp`, so a scratchpad file named like a secret was refused and a builder's `/tmp` write counted as outside its worktree); `fm deps --calls` and `fm recall --explain` find their lines with any git (they used GNU regex classes that macOS's git grep ignores, so they found nothing); the helper reaper reads start times and command lines from `ps` where there's no `/proc`; and four tests no longer assume Linux paths
- The context-tax replay runs with a set-up home folder, so CI's bare one no longer adds Foreman's one-time setup note to what it measures (T-1281)
- Ambient test notes name up to three failing tests and the command in 220 characters, instead of the runner's timing and class counts; a pass note fits 200. In one long session these notes came to 23 KB over 92 edits (T-1275)
- The edit-time lesson note reads "T-x's lesson for this file: …" and stops at 200 characters instead of 320; the old wording's frame alone was 49 characters (T-1274)

## 1.2.41 — 2026-10-20
- A prompt of many requests queues every one of them on a slow machine too: the hook now makes the tasks inside its own process instead of starting `fm` once per task, which on CI (Linux, Python 3.12) fit only 4 of 8 into the hook's 5 seconds (T-1277)
- A one-line note Foreman's edit-time hook has already given since the session's last start or compaction isn't given again, and a replayed session holds what each hook event injects under a byte budget, so the context tax can't creep back (T-1258)
- `fm cost` shows Foreman's own share of what entered the context, by source: fm command output, hook notes, its rules, its output style and its lines in the skill and agent listings. On Foreman's own repo over three days it was 7.9%, led by fm output (3.5%) and Claude Code's per-turn reminder of the output style (2.1%) (T-1257)
- A test run piped to `tail`, `head` or `grep` now counts as evidence when its output shows the test runner's own pass summary (unittest, pytest, jest, cargo, go or mocha), and a failure summary records a failure; before, every piped run needed a separate `fm task evidence` call, 5 to 12 a session in the long benchmarks (T-1256)
- Every `fm bench` result records the Claude Code version it ran on, and the Benchmarks page names it under each duel; runs from before say it wasn't recorded (T-1255)
- Benchmarks page: what one long session that passed every hidden test cost on each side, which is what you pay to get one. Foreman's sessions cost more each, but over the six long runs it came to $0.095 per fully correct session against Claude Code's $0.119: 20% less (T-1254)
- `fm code --worker claude|codex|gemini|aider|'CMD {prompt}'`: an installed CLI does each step in one unattended turn, killed at `--worker-timeout`; on Linux whatever it left running is killed too, even past setsid. Afterwards Git's own config, hooks and exclude file and your global Git config are put back, a commit is undone, an assume-unchanged flag it set is cleared, and every file it wrote that Foreman refuses is reverted and reported, whether git shows it or not (an ignored `.env` too); what can't be put back blocks the task. Only codex runs shell commands, inside its own sandbox. The worker and its verify get no forge, cloud, registry or other credentials, and a sensitive repo never goes to a worker. Only the harness's verify marks work done, and `--verify CMD` plans without a model (T-1214)
- Site: the Benchmarks page's opening figures and the Foreman Code page's counts are computed from the data (the code page had drifted to 52 model-client tests; there are 51), long-session runs add up over every three-run duel, and a saved light or dark theme applies before the first paint (T-1246)
- Adapted to Claude Code 2.1.304, which sends the skill list again after every compaction: Foreman's part of it is down from 2,730 to under 2,000 characters (shorter skill and workflow descriptions, `/foreman:build` user-only), with a test that keeps it there (T-1253)

## 1.2.40 — 2026-10-16
- `fm focus` on a request Foreman queued prints the request's text, word for word, and the note after a compaction carries only the request being worked in full (the rest by title). Every open request whole was 5 to 8 KB after each compaction, which brought the next one sooner (T-1231)
- CI: the guard scan's CPU-time bounds allow GitHub's slower runners (0.11 s there against 0.08), and each failing test gets its own public annotation instead of one per class (T-1248)
- In an unattended run, a FEATURE task's test-first note says to write and run the failing test, without recording it with fm task evidence: the long sessions spent up to 12 calls a run on that. A FIX still records its red run, for the red-to-green proof (T-1231)

## 1.2.39 — 2026-10-16
- Benchmarks page: one long Haiku session of eight requests compacting at 40,000 tokens. Foreman passed every hidden test in all 3 runs (4, 4 and 2 compactions); Claude Code on its own in 1 of 3 (89, 64 and 105 of 105), its misses written after its first compaction. Foreman cost about 60% more ($0.09 against $0.06). In a marathon, Foreman went on through seven compactions and fixed three things nobody asked for. "Why use Foreman" now says so (T-1242)
- Site review fixes (T-1220): the duel table escapes task names; `site/build.py` refuses to wipe the repo, `site/`, the home folder or `/`; `site/stats.py --duel NAME=…` keeps the other saved duels; line charts no longer fill a wedge under the line, and an empty one says so; wrapped inline code keeps its padding
- Long sessions in the bench (T-1242): `fm bench build --commits R --verify CMD --together` makes one case asking for a range's requests at once, graded by all their hidden tests; `fm bench duel --compact-at TOKENS` makes both arms compact early, as a long session does, and counts the compactions; results count the hidden tests passing. `--marathon N` lets Foreman's arm keep going after the asked work: N passes of finding and doing work nobody asked for, test first, until its dollar cap; each pass looks a different way (inputs its tests miss, docs against code, vague errors, untested functions, duplicated logic). Results count each request's failing hidden tests, and rules written above the first `## Request` go with every request
- A prompt of several `## Request k` sections, in a full-autonomy run, becomes one task per request, in order, each holding its request's text, so after a compaction the queue says what's done and what's left (T-1242). A prompt longer than the 2,000 characters the ledger keeps is read whole from the transcript. After a compaction, the requests still open come back word for word: in the first long run the summary kept only their names, and the agent wrote three modules from signatures and failed 61 of 105 hidden tests. At stop, the requests the agent has recorded evidence for close together, instead of one per stop. An edit between requests focuses the next one and shows its text, instead of refusing the write. The note after a compaction stays under the size Claude Code shows in full (a longer one reached the agent as a 2 KB preview)
- A bench replay is never told to send a push notification to your phone when its queue drains (T-1242)
- `fm task finish --commit` names the uncommitted files it leaves out because the task never changed them (edited before its focus, often under another task), and `--with FILE` commits one (T-1235)
- CI: the `fm run --sandbox` test stubs bubblewrap and socat, so it passes on runners with no working sandbox (it failed both Ubuntu jobs) (T-1227)

## 1.2.38 — 2026-10-16
- Benchmarks page opens with "Why use Foreman": the measured safety result, the record each task leaves, what the quality runs show (both sides pass; Foreman costs about 10% more on Sonnet) and what they can't show yet (T-1241)
- Benchmarks page: chart labels sit above their bars at full width, so a long task name can't run outside its card; table cells read "✓ 3 of 3 runs"; times are whole seconds; the per-task charts' text is larger on phones (T-1239)

## 1.2.37 — 2026-10-16
- A one-request unattended run's first task is S (M for SECURITY), not a size guessed from words in a long spec. An L guess had sent a lexer task into the full plan-and-audit flow (25 turns, 272 s). The run's one request is its work whatever its first word (T-1231)
- A bench replay doesn't scout for new work once its request is done; that was overhead the judge also marked down (T-1229)
- A harder round of the neutral benchmark: `bench/spec-suite/make.py --suite hard DIR` builds `ledger`, an invoicing package whose modules share helpers, with eight tasks graded by the whole test suite. Four are tasks whose obvious edit to a shared helper breaks another module's existing tests unless the spec's "Keep:" rules are followed; four have dense edge-case specs (text wrapping, INI, interval arithmetic, a tokenizer). `--check` proves each fail-to-pass and that each task's recorded naive variant fails (T-1237)
- Bench grading restores the reference commit's whole test folders, not only the hidden test files, so an arm that edits or guts an existing test to make its change pass is graded by the real one (T-1231)
- The stop-time close runs only in unattended runs, never while paused, and isn't retried after a timeout (T-1231 review). It reads the gates without building the map and prefers a test gate. When the tests fail, it tells the agent once with the failure, so the run fixes its work instead of ending on it.
- `fm bench run`, `duel`, `versions`, `court` and `soak` run a snapshot of the plugin folder taken at start, so editing Foreman during a run can't change what its later sessions load (T-1231)
- Foreman does its own bookkeeping in a one-request unattended run (T-1231):
  - At stop it closes and commits the S task it made (not a FIX, whose red→green proof stays the agent's) once the project's detected test command passes, within 20 s. The Stop hook's limit is now 30 s.
  - The session starts with the tracked layout and the test command instead of an empty queue's "FINAL VERIFY".
  - Skill and agent descriptions are 40% shorter (every turn of every session carries them).
  - On the neutral suite the agent's calls fell from 12 to 2 or 3, against 3 to 5 for Claude Code alone.
- More turns cut, measured on the neutral suite (T-1231): Foreman went from 13 turns and $0.22 to 4 turns and $0.08 on the same task (Claude Code alone: 4 turns, $0.07).
  - In a one-request full-autonomy session the first edit makes its task and goes ahead, after the whole guard passes it with the task active; the note teaches the one-call close (`fm task finish --run … --audit … --commit …`).
  - A `Task: …` prompt counts as a work request.
  - Only whole words like token, session, login or acl make a path security-sensitive (`kit/tokenbucket.py` had cost five turns and a reviewer), and the adversary refusal says to record it with `--lens "adversary: …"`.
- The Claude Code process behind a hook is found by a fixed shape: at most this hook's own python dispatcher (the same file by absolute path), then at most one system shell whose `-c` starts with the hook, then Claude Code. Nothing else is skipped and no process name or argv substring is trusted, so a disguised nested claude can't pass for the asking session (T-1236)
- A neutral benchmark suite: `bench/spec-suite/make.py DIR` builds a small repo of eight fully specified tasks (durations, an LRU cache, SemVer precedence, text tables, ranges, dotted paths, a token bucket, CSV lines) whose hidden tests check only what each commit message states; `--check` proves each fail-to-pass and the build reproducible (T-1232)
- A chat yes now answers a pending `fm ask` only when it is typed into the same Claude Code process that ran the ask. That process is named exactly: the hook's nearest ancestor that isn't its own python or the system shell launching it, never trusted by name. A nested or resumed Claude Code (renamed, under a pty, with a positional prompt) is always another process (T-1233)
- An unattended run (claude -p, fm run, a bench replay) is told at start to end with a reply that names each check it ran and its result, and anything it left undone: the duel's judge reads only that reply (T-1228)
- Fewer turns spent on Foreman itself, from the duel's transcripts (T-1231):
  - `fm task <sub>` without an id uses the active task and says which.
  - `ac` and `step` take their action on either side of the id (`fm task ac add T-1 …`); with no action, they add.
  - `drop`, `block` and `defer` take `--reason`.
  - A criterion whose check couldn't import its own test module (run outside the suite's path) can be corrected; one that ran and failed still can't.
  - A small task's steps close on its passing criteria.
  - In a full-autonomy session whose only request is the one being worked, the first edit makes that request's task (S, its tag's type, its DONE-WHEN lines as criteria) instead of refusing twice.
- `fm bench duel` leaves court cases out unless they are named (they grade what Foreman learned from your own state, which a fresh duel lacks), and takes `--runs N` and `--jobs N` (T-1231)
- The site has a Benchmarks page. The safety set: 24 dangerous commands (Foreman stops 20 and lets 4 through after saving your work; Claude Code alone in bypass mode runs all of them) and 25 everyday commands (none stopped). The quality duel: six real tasks graded blind, run once with Foreman and once with Claude Code. Its first run is published as it came out: Foreman passed 0 of 6, Claude Code 1 of 6, and Foreman cost 33% more. The page lists what the losses showed (T-1222)
- The test runner writes each failed class as a GitHub Actions error annotation when it runs in Actions, so a red CI run shows why without a login (job logs need one; annotations are public) (T-1223)
- A pending `fm ask` can't be answered by Claude itself. A yes from a Claude Code started with `-p`, `--print`, stream-json or the SDK (`claude --resume <this session> -p yes`, even renamed) no longer grants it, and the guard refuses a CronCreate, ScheduleWakeup or RemoteTrigger prompt that starts with a yes. A yes typed at Claude Code's prompt, or through Remote Control, still grants as before; the permission dialog is unchanged (T-1226)
- `fm code "<request>"` runs Foreman's own loop on any model `fmmodels` can reach. The model fills a checked plan (type, size, criteria with verify commands, steps), then picks one action at a time: read, search, edit, write, run or done. Edits, writes and commands go past the guard; reads never show credentials or `.git`. The harness runs the verify commands itself; it retries, escalates or blocks; then it closes the task and commits. A FIX or FEATURE verify that already passes before the work is sent back. Tool output is redacted before it reaches the model or the journal. Also `--dry-plan`, `--resume` (keeps `--no-commit`, `--escalate` and `--max-steps`) and `fm code models` (T-1212)
- The guard stops credential reads (`cat`, `grep`, `cp`, `base64`, `tar`, `<` and the Read tool on ~/.ssh keys, ~/.aws/credentials, ~/.netrc …, including through a loop variable, `tar -C` or a `--files-from` list). It also stops the environment or a key piped or attached to `curl`, `wget` or `nc`, and `chmod`/`chown -R` or world-writable modes on /, the home folder or system folders. Public parts (`*.pub`, known_hosts, ~/.ssh/config, ~/.aws/config) and using a key (`ssh -i`, `scp -i`) stay allowed. The safety benchmark now stops 20 of 24 dangerous commands and 0 of 25 everyday ones (T-1224)
- A bare `fm lease drop` releases only the active task's leases. Builder lanes share their main session's id, so a builder's drop had released the main thread's leases, three times in one day. `fm lease drop FILE` also releases every symbol lease on that file (T-1216).
- `fmmodels`, Foreman Code's model layer: one stdlib client for OpenAI-compatible endpoints and Anthropic's Messages API. Profiles come from `$FOREMAN_HOME/models.json`, keyed clouds (Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter, Groq, Gemini) and local servers (Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp, vLLM, probed in order). It retries on 429 and 5xx with one stop vocabulary, and the key never appears in an error and is never sent over plain http to a remote host (T-1211).

## 1.2.36 — 2026-10-16
- The site has a page for every feature (planning, proof, the guard, permissions, review, builder lanes, drive and headless, stack cards, the panel, speed, learning, tools, Foreman Code, releases), each with charts drawn from Foreman's own record of building itself: tasks closed per day by type, task sizes, guard refusals by rule, review findings by lens, hook time against budgets, tests per release. `site/build.py` renders them from `site/pages.py` and `site/data/stats.json` (made by `site/stats.py` from Foreman's state: counts only), and the Pages workflow runs it (T-1220).

## 1.2.35 — 2026-10-16
- `fm doctor` treats a machine with CPU pressure of 10% or more (`/proc/pressure/cpu`) as busy, and checks again after its hook bench. Its hook-latency check no longer fails when a burst (such as the test suite) starts before the 1-minute load average catches up; it says the timings weren't measured instead. This was the release-check test that kept failing inside the full suite (T-1219).
- The README demo and the site's poster are re-recorded in true color: Claude Code drops to 256 colours inside tmux unless `CLAUDE_CODE_TMUX_TRUECOLOR` is set, so the recording harness's every colour had been rounded to the 256 palette and the pane's tinted chart wells showed as neutral gray blocks. The new recording is a real fix to Foreman (T-1213) (T-1209).
- `fm edit`'s block form can delete: a block whose `===` line runs straight into `>>>` replaces the old text with nothing, instead of being counted as incomplete and refusing the batch. This fix is the one recorded in the new README demo (T-1213).
- `fm guard mine` run from a builder's linked worktree reads the main checkout's sessions (it had read only the worktree's own, empty, transcript folder) (T-1203).
- A website for GitHub Pages (`site/`): the recorded session, the eight stations every request walks, what Foreman holds Claude to, the guard refusing real commands, proof before done, builder lanes, install with copy buttons, light and dark. `.github/workflows/pages.yml` deploys it with the demo video on pushes to main once Pages is set to deploy from GitHub Actions (T-1206).
- Guard: python fed a quoted heredoc gets the checks `python -c` code has. Python settings (PYTHONPATH, PYTHONSTARTUP…) set in the command or inherited, and a `cd` outside its own `cd DIR &&` chain, keep it refused when it names Foreman's modules (T-1204).
- Guard: `python -c` code that names Foreman's modules only in its strings is parsed like a quoted heredoc and no longer refused as driving Foreman; real imports, dynamic code, code it can't read as one literal word, and a run folder it can't check stay refused (T-1194).
- `fm lease drop` says how many leases it released ("Dropped 2 lease(s).") before listing what's left, so a "No leases held." after a drop no longer reads as if nothing had been taken (T-1205).
- The guard's note for a script it couldn't parse in time says a literal scan of it found nothing to refuse, not that it ran unread (T-1202).
- `fm round close` lists the newest 20 tasks closed in the round and counts the rest (`--json` still lists every one); the first round of 1.2.34 had printed 744 lines into the release output (T-1200).
- A builder's brief names the main checkout's path as the one place never to cd into, write or point `git -C` at, and says its worktree is the folder it starts in and that fm verbs run there act on its lane (builders had hit Claude Code's "isolated in the worktree" refusal 31 times by leaving it) (T-1196).
- Guard: `git checkout -- FILE`, `git checkout REV -- FILE` and `git checkout FILE` are judged as writes to those files, not the whole tree. A branch, `-b`, `.`, a glob or pathspec-magic word, `--pathspec-from-file`, `--icase-pathspecs`/`--glob-pathspecs` and `GIT_ICASE_PATHSPECS`/`GIT_GLOB_PATHSPECS` still count as tree writes (T-1195).
- The guard reads what a command runs: script files (`bash x.sh`, `source`, a script run by its path, `python x.py`), hidden exec vectors (`tar -I`, `ssh`, `rsync -e`, `git -c core.*`, `find -exec`, env-loaded code) and the scripts those run, and refuses a dangerous one naming the file and line. Over its time budget it falls back to a literal scan, and verdicts are cached by content hash (T-0275).
- A stack card's "what to avoid" note is injected once per session; a later task in the same session gets one line pointing at `fm lib show NAME` instead of the whole note again (the changelogs card had been injected 28 times, about 18k characters, in one session) (T-1193).
- `fm release` runs the canary last, after the push and round close, with up to four project sentinels at once; it never fails a release, so it no longer holds the push (1.2.34's canary took over 3 minutes per project, one at a time, across 12 projects) (T-1199).

## 1.2.34 — 2026-10-16
- `fm release VERSION [--ui V] [--push] [--dry-run] [--resume] [--abandon]` runs the release as nine named steps (clean tree, gates, bump, changelog, commit, release-check, canary, push, round close) with a checkpoint after each. A stopped release resumes at the step that failed, `--push` merges the gated release commit (never later work) behind the active task's publish grant, and `release.py` is now a thin wrapper over it (T-1188).
- A test holds eight path-taking verbs (lease, edit, evidence, check, focus, guard wrong and others) to the same result when run from the repo root, a subdirectory and a symlinked checkout; it found no path bugs (T-1185).
- Tests point `FOREMAN_GUARD_CASES` at a temp file by default, so a test that runs `fm guard wrong` from a temp repo can no longer write cases into the real `guard_cases.jsonl` (the subdirectory matrix had added three) (T-1197).
- `fm guard wrong` stores the case in the corpus of the Foreman checkout it runs in (with a cwd relative to that checkout), so a builder's worktree no longer writes into the main checkout's tests mid-gates (T-1175).
- `fm task finish` refuses a task that added a `plugin/<entry>` MASTER.md's File map doesn't list, naming the entry, instead of leaving it to release-check in the full gates (T-1179).
- The Stop drive no longer offers a builder lane for a RESEARCH task or a release, and doesn't offer a task whose `fm check --evidence` gate run is still going (T-1176).
- A file named on an `fm` command line that can't be read (`fm intake --file nope.txt`) is a one-line error, not a Python traceback (T-1189).
- Common guesses run what they meant, with one quiet "(ran `fm …`)" note: `fm task list|ls|all`, `fm tasks`, `fm ls` (the queue), `fm task get|view|info|cat ID` (task show), `fm lane ls`, `fm inbox list|ls` (T-1180).
- A stopped fm-reviewer's findings are logged as `finding` events (lens, severity, file, verdict reported) without a hand-typed `fm task finding`, and the defect classes in builder briefs count them until rejected (T-1181).
- `fm doctor --fix` applies only the findings that already have a safe repair (stale status views, empty git objects, fallback state), rechecks them, and lists every finding it left for a person (T-1184).
- `fm audit run ID` runs the audit lenses as headless read-only `claude -p` reviewers over the frozen `fm audit prep` brief, bounded by `fm budget` and `fm contract`, and records each lens verdict and its findings, so the drive and `fm run` can audit without a person (T-1186).
- `fm edit` applies every `<<< === >>>` block under an `@@ file` header (a second one was dropped silently), and refuses a batch whose `<<<` count doesn't match its complete blocks (T-1191).
- The verify check no longer reads a flag's value as a test module (`run.py -k test_some_case` gave "test_some_case.py doesn't exist yet") (T-1190).
- `fm task block` and `fm task defer` take `--until "installed NAME"` or `--until "exists PATH"` (structured conditions, never a shell command), and `fm next` and `fm tidy` re-offer the task once it holds (T-1183).
- The `fm replay` gate also checks the labelled guard cases and fails on a drifted or broken one, so any task touching the guard meets them at its gates (T-1182).
- The hook bench runs `bash src/build.sh` on a ~37 KB real-shaped script, rewritten before every run so each is a cold read, with a 300 ms budget (T-1192).
- `fm guard mine [--days N]` lists this project's real guard refusals from its Claude Code transcripts, each with its full command, today's verdict and what the session did next, grouped by rule: the input a guard-friction fix needs (T-1187).

## 1.2.33 — 2026-10-16
- Help tiers are declared where each fm command is registered (`add(..., tier=(title, order))`), so parallel builders adding commands no longer conflict on one shared table; `fm help` is unchanged (T-1144).
- Builders self-review as an adversary before they report (what input breaks it, what outside text it trusts, what is unbounded, what it writes where, what a second run does) and say so in a Self-review section (T-1147).
- `fm lease take` with a path relative to the repo root, run from a subdirectory, leases that file instead of a doubled path (T-1148).
- The pane's Agents graph says "working" for a builder's fresh worktree (its task still open, nothing committed yet) instead of "merged" (T-1152).
- The builder contract has one source: a test fails when `fmlanes.CONTRACT` and `agents/fm-builder.md` drift apart (T-1151).
- `fm check` names the files that changed during its gates and says the change may be your own edit, instead of blaming a gate that writes files (T-1149).
- A verify naming a test module or script that doesn't exist yet is kept with a note saying so; a verify naming an npm script its package.json lacks is refused when the criterion is added (T-1145).
- `fm guard try "CMD"` (or `--tool Edit --path P`) prints the guard's real decision for this session (task, grants, cwd) without running anything, so a false block can be checked before a retry (T-1146).
- `fm loop round` run again during a round shows that round's brief instead of counting it dry and starting the next one; `fm loop round --next` (what `fm next` says once the round's work has drained) ends it (T-1153).
- A mistyped `fm` command prints the nearest match and points at `fm help` instead of listing all ~170 commands (T-1170).
- The band reads "step 2/3" (the step being worked) beside that step's text instead of "1/3", and drops the "→" line when it only repeats that step (T-1154).
- `fm todo` lists what only you can do (pending asks, blocked-on-you tasks, recent costly or outward decisions), each with what to type, and drops entries as they're resolved (T-1157).
- Every close prints one "Not verified" block, replacing "Check by hand": a skipped affected-tests run (only when it was really waived), claims recorded as unchecked or inferred, then criteria no command of their own proved (T-1159).
- `fm lane list` and `fm next` flag a live builder lane with no edit, commit or ledger event for 15 minutes (`FM_STALL_MIN`) as stalled and say how to resume it; returned and merged lanes are skipped (T-1156).
- `fm away`: what closed, blocked, was asked or reverted since you last spoke; a session start or resume after a 2 h gap carries one "Away" line (T-1155).
- The guard's "driving Foreman's modules" refusal for a test written with `cat <<EOF` and run in the same command now says to write it in one command and run it in the next (each half passes), the cause of 12 of 31 such refusals in four days (T-1171).
- `fm lane merge` records the files that conflicted, and `fm lane brief` warns when a new lane's scope includes a file two or more other lanes conflicted on in the last 30 days (T-1161).
- `fm inbox spec FILE` captures each unchecked `- [ ]` item of a SPEC.md or checklist once, quoted as untrusted data (T-1165).
- Builder briefs list the defect classes reviewers confirmed in the last 30 days (class, count, one example) under "Defects this project keeps shipping", so builders check for them before reporting (T-1164).
- `fm deps --capture` queues one inbox task per dependency a major version behind ("Migrate X from 2.x to 3.1.0"), never twice for the same target major (T-1166).
- `fm pr ID --post [--pr N]` comments the rendered description, with its proof, on the PR; posting is outward, so it needs the task's publish grant and spends it (T-1167).
- `fm task finish --commit` leaves out files that were already uncommitted when the task was focused (and no other Foreman task touched, and not written in the minute before the focus, which is the task starting), and names them; `--with FILE` commits one (T-1169).
- `fm guard wrong "CMD" --should allow|block` stores a labelled guard case in the repo's test fixtures; the guard tests hold every label, `fm replay --cases` reports them, and `fm night` captures one FIX per drifted case (T-1162).
- `fm contract --until HH:MM [--usd N] [--tokens N]` bounds an unattended run: past the time, the headless spend or this project's token use, the Stop drive, the forever loop and `fm run` stop, and `fm contract receipt` lists what closed, what it cost and which bound ended it (T-1158).
- Foreman ships two Claude Code workflows: `/foreman:review` (one reviewer per audit lens over a diff, two skeptics per finding, only confirmed defects returned) and `/foreman:ideas` (a brainstorm whose lens agents read the code, so each idea comes back marked new, partial or built, with evidence) (T-0787).

## 1.2.32 — 2026-10-16
- `fm doctor` no longer warns about a hook error whose traceback line the code doesn't have any more (a half-written edit, a fixed bug); it says how many were stale (T-1139).
- The guard reads a heredoc fed to `fm edit` as data, like one fed to `cat`: an edit block that names Foreman's modules is no longer refused as interpreter code just because the same command also runs python3 (T-1141).
- Hooks find the active task without parsing every brief (only briefs whose frontmatter says active or verifying are parsed): the PreToolUse hook on this repo went from a 135 ms median to 72 ms (T-1140).
- `fm lib gaps | draft | admit`: list dependencies no card covers, stage a card, and admit it into the library only after the card lint and an untrusted-text check pass, never outside the cards folder (T-1037).
- Racing builders: `fm lane race T` has two builders work an S/M task under their own ids, `--score` compares them (criteria passing, lines changed), and `fm lane pick W` merges the winner and drops the other, keeping its branch (T-1069).
- fm logs a stage event whenever a task's stage changes (from every task write and fm focus), and pane splits use those real stage times (T-1137).
- `fm check affected --for .tsx "<cmd>"` gives each test-file extension its own affected command, so a changed `.tsx` test runs in `fm check --affected` and at close beside the Python ones; this repo now runs the mod's tests that way (T-1135).
- Pane: a `palette: 'cb'` option (a colourblind-safe blue and orange ok/error pair; errors keep their glyph) and a history scrubber (`b` steps back, `l` returns to live) (T-1081).
- A lane's pre-claim of its scope files is advisory: it no longer blocks another builder's edit in its own worktree (the merge settles overlaps); an explicit `fm lease take` still does (T-1138).
- `fm lane rm` waits while the lane's last merge conflicted and its branch isn't merged (resolve and merge, or drop the task to abandon it); removing it then had lost the builder record and the resume (T-1136).
- Pane speedrun splits: the task block shows each stage's time against the project's typical for its type and tier, green when under and red when over (T-1078).
- `fm check --affected` names changed test files its command can't run (a `.tsx` test under a Python runner) instead of saying only "No tests linked" (T-1131).
- A criterion whose check names a package script that doesn't exist (npm's or pnpm's "Missing script", yarn's "Command not found") stays editable, like a missing command or test module (T-1133).
- A bare `fm lease drop` releases every lease this session holds in the project (builders ran it bare, as their brief says, and got only usage) (T-1134).
- `fm draft`: while Claude waits, a headless session drafts the top queued S task on a throwaway `draft/T-x` branch, ready to review; swept when the queue's top or its brief changes; `fm night` can run it; never merged (T-1062).
- Every self-change can be a pre-registered trial: `fm rule add` and `fm evolve` take `--moves METRIC --by DAYS`, and `fm digest` (and `fm trial`) grades due trials on before and after ledger rates, proposing a revert when the metric didn't move (T-1059).
- `fm backtest --rule/--fix/--card`: how often a changed rule, known fix or card would have fired on recent real sessions, and how many of those calls errored (read-only) (T-1058).
- `fm pr threads ID` turns a PR's unresolved review comments into task steps (quoted as data, once each); `fm pr train` merges approved, green, non-draft PRs in order, re-testing each head and pinning the merge to the tested commit, stopping at the first red (a publish grant spent per run) (T-1087).
- The mod's T-1090 review test streams the long line only on the session's own turn: 1,178 ms → 104 ms, so it no longer times out under load (T-1132).
- A daily risk budget: destructive guard blocks, failed closes and reverts spend points, and past the cap autonomy steps down a notch until tomorrow (`fm risk`; only the user moves the cap) (T-1080).
- `fm digest` and the pane's idle line say what Foreman learned: fixes new, worked and missed, rules to propose from corrections, interventions per closed task (T-1045).
- The pane is swept over 30 terminal sizes in its tests; no row spills past it (T-1050).
- `fm night` works unblocked queued S/M tasks through `fm run` under the night's remaining budget, and `fm digest` names the tasks it closed; `fm run --max-usd X` stops starting tasks once its measured spend reaches X (T-1046).
- `fm ci-watch`: after a push, polls the GitHub Actions run for that exact commit through gh; a failure's log tail becomes one FIX brief (quoted as data), and it keeps watching pushed fixes up to `--max-retries`, then says it gave up (T-1052).
- `fm export cursor` and `fm export gemini` write `.cursor/rules/foreman.mdc` (alwaysApply) and `GEMINI.md` with the AGENTS.md body; every export gains a Known fixes section (trusted fixes only, redacted, at most 15, none in a sensitive project) (T-1043).
- A command that would wait forever on stdin (`cat > /tmp/x` with nothing feeding it, `python3 -` without a heredoc, a bare `tee` or `bash`) is refused before it runs, naming how to feed it; builders lost two minutes to each one. On a week of recorded commands it caught 22, all real hangs (T-1129).
