- `fm task finish ID --ablate` reverts each hunk of the task's change against the linked tests after the close's checks and names the hunks no test notices; informational, time-bounded, never fails the close (T-1067).
- `fm adopt` learns from git history: fix/revert commits become a `git-history-lessons` note recall finds, and `error: X → Y` commit lines become known fixes that stay hints until trusted (`fm adopt --history` alone) (T-1047).
- Lane claims: `fm lease take FILE[::SYM]...` refuses a claim that overlaps another session's, naming it and its task (`--warn` takes an advisory one); lane briefs pre-claim the task's scope files, released on rm, merge and resume (T-1068).
- Night gardening: `fm night` composts closed tasks' worktrees and branches (`fm tidy --lanes-only`) and files one task proposing merged near-duplicate lessons (`fm recall --lessons --consolidate`) (T-1071).
- A new session's band tree starts at its own height; it kept the last session's padding when a turn never completed (T-1041).
- Chat tree: the ↻ resume row and a background task's note hang on the rail like every other row; it no longer breaks at them (T-1088).
- A refused interpreter write made from a shell left inside `state/` says so ("The shell is in …, so a relative path lands there"); the reported false positive was a real state write from a stale cwd (T-1125).
- A criterion whose check ran no tests at all (a mistyped test module: unittest's or pytest's "no tests ran" in the run's last lines) stays editable with `fm task ac ID edit`, like a missing command or file already did (T-1128).
- Install commands are checked before they run (`npm`/`pip`/`uv`/`poetry`/`pipx`/`yarn`/`cargo`/`go`): a name one edit away from a popular package, or (when the network is allowed) first published in the last 30 days, gets a note; `fm deps check "<cmd>"` runs it by hand. Lookups share a 1.2 s budget, skip popular, scoped and private-registry names, and are off under `FOREMAN_NO_NET=1` or a sensitive repo (T-1049).
- A command copied from a web page or MCP result asks before it runs (once per line per session); headless or in full autonomy it is denied with a way forward, every time (T-1053).
- The user proxy: once the user has given the same answer twice to the same ask (same question and options, within 180 days), `fm decide --ask` answers it for them with `proxy: true` and says so in the digest. Two overruled proxy answers stop it; guard asks and an empty key are never proxied; `fm decide --answer` is user-only (T-1064).
- A hit cost cap is recorded on the task (`cap_hit`), so `fm run`, `fm next`, lanes and the drive all hold it until its cap changes, and `fm next` says so; before, only the interactive drive did (T-1127).
- Per-task cost cap and forecast (T-1083): `fm task set ID cost_cap=2M` (input-equivalent tokens; empty or 0 removes it, and every change is logged, raises listed in `fm digest`) makes the drive ask for a checkpoint at the cap and stop pushing that task until the cap changes; `fm cost --task ID`, `fm focus` and `fm task show` show spent, cap and a forecast from finished tasks of the same type and tier. Spend is attributed to the task the turn's own session was on, cached in `state/cache`, and refreshed off the Stop hook's path. `fm task set ID key=` clears an optional field (T-1126).
- Plan calibration (T-1061): a plan can predict its minutes and attempts (`fm task set predict_minutes=… predict_attempts=…`, frozen at first focus); a close is a miss when more than 2 files land outside the scope it started with, or the active minutes or attempts overrun the prediction. When 3 of the last 5 closes (14 days) missed, autonomy slows: M tasks need the user's approval until closes land on plan again, and fm next, fm run, the drive and fm digest all say so.
- Steers compile to checks (T-1063): `fm taste` types each repeated steer (safety, scope, style, wrong approach), and `fm taste compile N` turns one into a warn rule anchored to the command it names (`git … push`, `--force`, `rm` at command position; two shape words at least); a block rule is the user's (`fm taste compile N --block` is user-only in the guard).
- `fm tidy` and `fm next` judge "what a blocked task waited on changed" by git (a commit after the block, or uncommitted edits), not by the file's mtime: a fresh clone or a builder's worktree made every blocked task look ready (T-1106).
- State rollback on upgrade (T-1085): the first session after a Foreman version change snapshots every project's record in the background (keeping 3, with a manifest), and `fm rollback [--list] [--to NAME] --yes` restores it after a safety copy, file by file under each project's lock, never touching the ledger or consent changed since; the guard leaves `fm rollback --yes` (and every abbreviation of it) to the user, from one table of user-only fm commands.
- A brainstorm's `ideas.md` states its distinct idea count at the top, and `fm ideas --seen` reads only its idea sections: the per-lens, coverage and kill bullets were read back as idea titles, and a bullet count overstated a run (409 bullets for ~290 ideas) (T-1056).
- The pane flags a stopped builder (T-1119): its worktree node in the Agents graph reads "stopped · fm lane resume T-…" in the warning colour, from `fm ui`'s new `stopped` mark on that worktree.
- `fm task set` applies every `--section`/`--text` pair given in one call (only the last one landed, silently) and refuses a section without its own text (T-1124).
- One `c.kill_group(proc)` kills a child's whole process group for run_command, the bench, fm serve, guard mutation and the claude -p helpers; the bench's own copy had no guard, so a run that ended just as its timeout hit crashed it (T-1094).
- Guard (T-1123): `rm` fed targets on stdin is checked whether recursive or not, against the command directly feeding xargs (only plain `echo` operands with no shell syntax are trusted as targets; any xargs option that changes how items are read, in any short-option cluster, makes them unknown), explicit operands don't hide the stdin ones, and `eval` reads the text an `echo` substitution prints, flagging any substitution it can't read.
- Decision sunsets and a weekly decision digest (T-1084): `fm decide X --sunset YYYY-MM-DD` gives a decision an expiry that `fm tidy` warns about until a later `--revisited` or `--reverses` row settles it; `fm digest` lists every decision made without you that week (costly and outward ones once, under review), and `--json` carries them as `decided`.
- Guard tests pin every rule site the mutation run found untested: eval of a download, `fm posture lite` / `pause off` / `approve-reads on` / `rule rm`, recursive rm with stdin targets, nesting depth, ufw, firewall-cmd, cryptsetup, dd and `git core.hooksPath`; dropping any system, rm-outside, self-authorize or pipe-shell site now fails a test (T-1120).
- Tools forged from repeats (T-1070): `fm repeats --forge` drafts a script for each repeated command shape fm check doesn't cover and a SKILL.md for each repeated step (in state `research/`, never executable, secrets redacted, labelled as data), and captures one task per draft with a step and a criterion to adopt it; a re-run restores a deleted draft and files nothing new.
- `fm bench guard --mutate --flip` flips one operator or constant inside the guard's check functions per mutant, the class the old `plugin/tests/mutate_guard.py` script covered; the script is gone (its copy skipped the test fixtures, so every mutant looked killed); run from a builder's worktree, `--mutate` now mutates that checkout's guard, not the main one's (T-1121).
- Deferred-work triage (T-1048): `fm tidy` and `fm digest` propose do (its revisit date or watched file came), drop (90+ days, its scope gone from git history, no revisit trigger) or ask for each deferred brief, applying nothing; the digest lists do and drop first.
- Mutation-test the guard (T-1066): `fm bench guard --mutate` drops (or `--weaken`s) one guard rule site at a time (every shape: appends, list returns, `+=`, comprehensions, `Block`s), runs the guard's tests on a throwaway copy in its own process group, and lists the rules no test catches (exit 1 when any); categories with no site are named. Its first full run found 13 untested sites, now being pinned (T-1120). `--coverage` keeps the command-corpus view.
- `fm task finish --commit` keeps the files the task names as its own (its scope and its `scope:` log reasons) even when another task touched them meanwhile: a doc edited in the same command as the finish has no touched record yet and was left uncommitted (T-1122).
- `fm ui` runs in under half the time (0.67 s → 0.30 s on 910 briefs), which the pane pays on every state change: a process parses each unchanged brief once and reads an unchanged ledger once, handing out fresh copies (T-1082).
- Cross-project memory (T-1073): `fm recall --others "<failure>"` finds a task in your other opted-in, non-sensitive Foreman projects that met the same failure (by signature, then by shared words) and its lesson; the failure hook adds one "met in <project>" line, labelled as data and defanged, when no local task met it.
- Outbound ledger and opt-in sandbox (T-1086): every outbound request (WebFetch, WebSearch, MCP tools, and curl, wget, git remote, ssh and scp hosts in Bash) is logged per task, host only, never the query; `fm outbound [ID]` lists them. `fm run --sandbox` or `fm sandbox on` runs headless sessions under Claude Code's sandbox after a real probe, with `fm sandbox allow DOMAIN` (default: the git remote's host and the project's package registries) and writes allowed for the project, caches and state.
- A stopped builder resumes (T-1028): `fm lane brief` records the session's Claude Code process (host, PID namespace, pid and start time); `fm next` flags a lane whose process is gone with `fm lane resume ID`, which keeps the work (a WIP commit on its branch, secret-looking files left out, ignored files listed), frees the worktree, resets the task and writes a brief that merges the branch first. A live builder, another host's, or a detached HEAD is handled rather than lost; `--force WHY` overrides on the record.
- Foreman keeps the suite green itself (T-1117): a close runs the tests linked to what the task changed (one rerun for a flake) and refuses a new failure (one already red in another task's last full run, or an environment failure, is noted instead; `--skip-affected WHY` waives it on the record); a red full-gate run captures one urgent baseline FIX naming the failing tests, and a green run names it as green again; after a close, the full gates run in the background (one at a time, and a run that outlives the close's tree captures nothing) when the last full run is older than 3 closes or an hour. A change that broke another module's test (T-1089 → test_builder) had gone unnoticed for a dozen closes.
- `fm cost` runs in half the time (7.6 s → 3.8 s on 559 MB of transcripts): turns are matched to tasks by bisect, transcript files and lines wholly before the window are skipped before parsing, and task attribution reads the archived ledger months (T-1116).
- `fm cost` and `fm digest` flag unexplained spend (T-1079): tokens from sessions with no Foreman activity, or turns more than 30 minutes from any, once they reach 20% and 100k tokens, with the top session named; the archived ledger months count.
- `fm tidy` also lists the user's rules that no call matched in 60 days (each hit's time is now recorded) and the hints shown six or more times running without use; both are listed only, since only the user removes a rule (T-1115).
- `fm tidy` hygiene (T-1072): known fixes that keep missing are removed by `--apply` (a copy goes to `archive/fixes-removed.md`; fixes synced from the repo are left to the repo file), ones unused for 60 days are listed, and so are the project's picked library cards no edit used in 60 days.
- Hotspots (T-1074): files with 3 or more fix or revert commits in 180 days (sweeping commits over 20 files left out) are named at `fm focus` ("Hotspot: x.py (4 fix/revert commits in 180 days)") and in `fm audit prep`'s findings for a diff that touches them.
- Evidence and other verification runs never inherit an exported `FOREMAN_SKIP_TESTS`, so a run of the test suite can't leave modules out silently (T-1114).
- `fm check` leaves test files that are only another open task's red-first tests out of the suite run (`FOREMAN_SKIP_TESTS`, honoured by `run.py`, never inherited from the environment) and says which task's tests it skipped; a cached pass is reused only for the same skip set (T-1101).
- The cached Claude Code version refreshes as soon as the `claude` binary is newer than the cache (it waited a day, so `fm doctor` said to update right after an update), and failure signatures zero hex ids such as `agent-a0bd0ad0edc0`, so one failure in two builders' worktrees groups as a repeat (T-1109).
- A subagent's work no longer puts notes into the main thread's context: its repeated edits raise no thrash note, its failed commands no known-fix, recall or suspects note (nor a failure filed against the main thread's task), and its edits start no ambient test run (T-1110).
- Corrections become rules (T-1042): when the user corrects the same thing twice (shared rare words, stemmed), `fm taste` proposes it with a ready `fm rule add … --warn` line; `fm taste adopt N` / `decline N` act on it (a bare adopt or decline acts on steer proposals only), and a recorded veto, rule or decline hides it.
- `fm tidy`'s ledger roll takes the ledger's own lock, reads bytes and never reorders the chain: lines with no ts, a bad ts or a future ts go with their neighbours, a racing writer's line survives, and a roll that crashes resumes from a sidecar without losing or double-archiving lines (T-1099).
- Refuted hypotheses carry over (T-1075): `fm task hypo ID mark N ruled-out --why "<reason>"` keeps why a hypothesis was refuted; `fm task hypo ID ruled [--write]` lists this task's and same-scope tasks' refutations (rarest shared path first, with age, quoted as data) and writes `handoffs/ID-ruled-out.md`, which fm-debugger reads first; checkpoint resume lines carry them.
- The guard treats a direct write to a git config file (a repo's `.git/config`, `config.worktree`, a worktree's config, `~/.gitconfig`, `~/.config/git/config`) as `system`, as it already did for `.git/hooks/` and for `git config` setting a key that runs code: the file can set `core.hooksPath`, a `!` alias or a filter driver (T-1107).
- Survival grading (T-1060): `fm outcomes` grades each close by whether it survived 30 days: lost (with the revert, later FIX or correction and its date; the monthly ledger archives count too) or survived once the window passes, settling until then, plus days since a lost task (in `fm digest` too). `--settle` records each grade once as an `outcome` event (the pane's History shows the lost ones); `--claims` compares survival by close grade, `--regret` lists lost tasks with the approach they took, `--graveyard` the lost and dropped, `--seen` corrected-mid-task against the rest.
- `fm tidy` and `fm digest` flag queued plans that went stale since planning: a scope file changed (judged by git commit time from the main checkout), went away, or a dependency was dropped. Each reads "re-plan: <why>", and `fm task log ID "plan re-read: …"` clears it (T-1077).
- `fm doctor` names the upstream Claude Code issue behind an error seen twice in 7 days (hook errors and every project's failure signatures, counted only after the installed version arrived) and the version that fixes it, from a bundled list verified against GitHub and the upstream changelog (`plugin/lib/upstream-issues.json`) (T-1076).
- The `claude -p` helpers (ideas, second, research, relate) run in their own process group: a timeout or ^C kills the whole group, grandchildren too, and SessionStart sweeps helpers a dead run left behind (checking the start time and command line, or for a dead leader, that a member it started still holds its group), logging `orphans_reaped` (T-1044).
- Change many spots in one call (T-1089): `fm edit` applies a batch of exact replacements from stdin (JSON or `@@` blocks), all or none, inside the task's scope and only where the guard would let an Edit write; `fm sub PATTERN REPL --glob G` previews a regex change and writes it with `--apply`; the third edit of one file in a row points at them once; `fm lane max N` raises the builder cap (default 2, up to 6).
- Tamper-evident ledger (T-1065): each ledger line carries `prev`, the hash of the one before (written under an flock, so racing writers chain in order; a failed lock or unreadable archive writes the event unchained instead of losing it); `fm verify-ledger [--head H]` walks the monthly archives and the live file and names the first edited, removed or cut line; `fm task finish` prints the chain head.
- `fm ui` no longer crashes when a subagent's outline list is the newest file in the sessions folder: that crash blanked the band and the pane (T-1097).
- foreman-ui 0.17.0 (T-1090): every node of the live tree above the prompt holds its own words: this session shows the last thing Claude said it is doing, and each subagent its own (the first line of each model step, streaming in as it is written; tapped from `turn.step` per subagent, each chunk passed on unchanged), fewer lines each as agents multiply; a builder's node names its worktree; a fading rule separates the tree from the chat.
- The forever loop (T-1057): `fm loop on` turns an empty queue into the next improvement round (review what shipped, brainstorm past the newest earlier runs, ground, queue, build), enforced by `fm next` and the Stop drive rather than left to the session; a round's yield is the tasks it made unless `fm loop yield N` says otherwise, two dry rounds in a row or `fm loop off` end it (with a decision on record), and usage ahead of pace pauses it. The pane and band show `∞ round N`.

## 1.2.31 — 2026-10-16
- The README's demo is re-recorded with the current UI (T-1035): the chat as a tree, the live tree above the prompt with a scout subagent's branch, the pane's work bar and Agents graph. Its change ships too: `fm lib stats --days N` counts only the last N days of card use.
- foreman-ui 0.16.0 (T-1040): the whole chat is a tree, not only the band: each turn hangs off your prompt, every call and reply on a `├─` branch with a rail down the left and the turn's closing line as `└─`; finished calls are compact (a passing command's output folded to a count, one line of the command, diffs four lines, failures in full), so the transcript scrolls far less. The `chat` setting `full` restores the long rows. Engine-drawn rows (questions, folded reads, long results) keep the rail beside them.
- Stack cards fire by what a file imports, not only by its extension (T-1036): only 6 of 1,084 cards had ever reached Claude, all language cards. `fm map` now keeps cards' dependency signals; an edit of a file importing a framework the project depends on (Python, JS/TS, Rust, Go) gets that framework's card beside its language's, at most two an edit. `fm audit prep` adds the review checklists of the cards a task used; `fm lib stats` shows which cards reach Claude.
- foreman-ui 0.15.1 (T-1039): the live tree above the prompt is sticky: one height for the whole turn (it grows when it must, never shrinks mid-turn) with a blank row under it, so the chat doesn't shift; every node has a time bar in one column on a shared axis, as in the pane's Agents graph; the Foreman spinner line no longer repeats what the tree's root says.
- foreman-ui 0.15.0 (T-1033): the band above the prompt is a live tree of the session and its subagents, each with its latest calls (running, ✓ or ✗, with times), so what is happening now stays on screen while the chat scrolls; beside the docked pane it is the band's only content.
- Known fixes (T-1032): a failed command and the next command on the same program that gets past it are kept with no one asking, and offered the next time any session meets that failure, in any project on the machine; a command that failed before is flagged before it reruns; fixes that keep missing stop being offered, and a failure met three times with no fix captures a task. Project fixes travel with the repo through `fm sync` (`.foreman/fixes.md`), but one that came only with the repo is a hint to check until `fm fix trust` adopts it (security review); a sensitive project's fixes stay in it; an edit between the failure and the pass teaches nothing. `fm fix list|add|rm|trust`. Failure signatures skip `Try '--help'` pointers and also take unrecognized/unknown/invalid/unexpected/illegal/not allowed lines as the error, so some older failure records stop matching. The UI harness answers Claude Code's folder-trust prompt for folders Foreman made.
- foreman-ui 0.14.0 (T-1031): the chat matches the pane: every row (your prompt, Claude's reply, commands, edits, subagents and any other tool, review findings included) is a section with a bold label and a rule fading in its colour, no box, a blank line above; diffs keep a line of context, break between hunks and wash only the changed words.
- foreman-ui 0.13.0 (T-1026): Agents is a node graph (the session at the root, a branch to each subagent, brainstorm and worktree, a builder's worktree under its agent), and keeps room for its children; the head has a bar of the work, done today against what's left (now, queued, inbox, blocked), and the task a segment per step with *N of M steps done · K left*; History shows each event in its colour with the task's title (`fm ui` carries a structured `history` and `blocked_total`); dividers are gradients in their section's colour and secondary text reads in a lighter gray.
- foreman-ui 0.12.0: an Agents graph in the pane (T-1023): a Gantt row per subagent (running or finished in the window), per git worktree (a builder's joins its agent's row; a lone one says whether it has commits to merge) and per brainstorm, with dots where calls or answers landed; `fm ui` lists the worktrees.
- Stack detection reads only a go.mod's direct requires; `// indirect` modules aren't the project's dependencies (T-1021: the scout had asked for cards for dozens of transitive Go modules). `fm scout` names a Gradle dependency by its group:artifact (the form cards detect), not the bare id (`rules`, `runner`, `json`) (T-1022).
- Stack library: cards for SciPy, Python audio (librosa, soundfile, demucs) and pmndrs postprocessing, and detect lines so playwright-core, vite-plugin-svelte and svelte-check projects get the Playwright and Svelte cards (T-1016); a CodeMirror 6 card (with Lezer), and Tauri crates and plugins, troika-three-text and @eslint/js picked up by their cards (T-1018); VS Code extension and xterm.js cards, and SvelteKit adapters and Vite plugins detected (T-1020); all found by `fm scout --slow`.
- `fm task finish --why-not-caught` captures no "Catch it earlier" task when the task itself edited a test file; it says the catch landed with it (T-1009, first written by the recorded demo session).
- `fm doctor`'s self-docs check (which also refuses the close of a task that changes `fmcli.py`) holds `fm help`'s tiers to every command once: `fm approve-reads` shipped with none, and only the full suite noticed, after the commit (T-1010, T-1011).
- The UI harness marks a session one-off only on your own Foreman state; on a state of its own (the demo recorder) it is that state's working session, so the drive takes it to the close (T-1013: a recording stalled before its task closed).
- A one-off session (`FOREMAN_ONEOFF=1`, or `claude -p` that `fm run` didn't start) records no evidence on the project's task and none of its edits count as the task's files: the UI harness ran the suite and closed the working session's step (T-1004, T-1007). The test helpers clear those variables, so a suite run from such a session behaves like any other.
- The fleet drops a killed session even after a later SessionStart swept its by-pid records: the status line's snapshot carries its Claude Code pid and start time, and a snapshot whose process has ended or been reused isn't live (T-1003).
- `fm approve-reads` (T-1005): flags that run a program or write are not reads, in any spelling: `git grep -O`/`--open-files-in-pager`, git's `--ext-diff`/`--textconv`/`--output`, `sort --compress-program`, `rg --pre`/`--pre-glob`/`--hostname-bin`/`-z`, a GNU abbreviation (`sort --comp=`, `--out=`), a bad letter in a cluster (`sort -uoFILE`, `tail -qf`), anything after `--` (`find -- . -delete`), `uniq IN OUT`, `file -C`, `tree -R`.
- foreman-ui 0.11.0, the pane remade (T-1002): titled dividers with room above them; the attention row moves into the head; the task block drops its criteria list for a proof count beside the time; Activity plots one braille lane per kind of call (skill calls get their own) with what runs now, or ran last, at each lane's end; History lists what happened newest first in one short line each and takes the rows left; Today puts four tank gauges (context, spend, hooks, gates) beside the 14-day closes, in place of the small bars. Rows History can't fill go to more lanes and taller Today charts first (T-1012); a task block with rows still to spare lists what proves it done (T-1015). Spend by feature, bench, research, vetoes, files and the week's skills leave the pane (fm has them). `fm ui` keeps 24 history lines.
- `fm approve-reads on` (yours to type; T-0974): in Claude Code's default permission mode, Foreman's PermissionRequest hook answers the dialog for a command that only reads (a conservative allowlist: every part a reading program without its writing flags, no redirect but to /dev/null, nothing substituted, backgrounded or assigned). Anything else still asks.
- The fleet drops a session whose Claude process has ended at once (a killed session sends no SessionEnd and read as live for 15 minutes), and one-off sessions (`FOREMAN_ONEOFF=1`, the UI harness) never join it (T-1001).
- The pane fills the dock exactly with no gaps (T-1000): sections take the rows they hold, Live keeps its height and draws the session's tool lanes in rows nothing runs in, and the Pulse charts at the bottom stretch into the rest (14-day bars as tall as the section, then gauges, then the newest news). Every More line has a picture: a context meter per session, a +/− bar for files, one stacked bar for spend by feature, a bar per skill, pass meters for bench and research. Plans waiting for a yes sort first in the queue.
- Ambient tests (T-0994): a pass no longer counts as the current step's evidence (it closed steps mid-work: 'look at it live' and 'docs' on T-0989); the pass-again note names the `fm task evidence` line for when the step is done. `fm check --affected` and ambient runs leave out tests the template's runner can't run (a `.tsx` test handed to a Python runner ran nothing and read as a pass).
- The Foreman pane is one panel (T-0989): fixed sections sized from the dock's rows, never from what they hold, so it fits one screen and nothing jumps as work comes and goes; rules between sections instead of ~18 bordered cards; one attention row for the most urgent thing; Live merges tool calls, subagents and background shells; Pulse puts 14 days of closes beside context, spend, hooks and gate-dot gauges with a tool-call sparkline; each fact shown once (the stage, the step count, spend and the closed count were each drawn twice); what doesn't fit is counted. With the panel open the band steps aside. Folding and held heights are gone.
- `FOREMAN_ONEOFF=1` makes any session a one-off (no queue in its context, no drive, no claim on the project); the UI harness (`shot.py`) sets it, after its session was driven into the queue, planned three tasks and took the working session's focus (T-0995).
- fm finds Claude Code's native install (`~/.local/bin/claude`) when the shell's PATH predates it, so the gates, `fm run` and doctor's script checks work once the npm copy is gone (T-0990); `FOREMAN_NATIVE_BIN` overrides that folder, and the tests point it at nothing so no test reaches the paid binary (T-0992).
- `fm run --sandbox` (T-0975): each headless session runs under Claude Code's sandbox (`--settings` with `sandbox.enabled`, `failIfUnavailable`, no unsandboxed fallback); Bash may write only the checkout, Foreman's state and the git dir, where a lane's commits land. Ambient tests (T-0705) are on in this repo.
- SessionStart writes Foreman's two exports to Claude Code's env file once per session, not again on every compaction; a long session's Bash prelude had them eight times (T-0988).
- Small-model gaps (T-0987, measured in T-0978 with a headless Haiku session): `fm check`'s first-run hint offers the repo's own test runner (`tests/run.py`, `run_tests.py`) before `unittest discover`; a foreground `for` loop that sleeps between checks is refused like `until`/`while`; an unknown task id names the active and newest open ids here.
- `fm next`'s due-back line for a deferral waiting on a file says to defer again with the same `--until "when PATH changes"`, not `--until <date>` (T-0986; found and fixed by a headless Haiku session, T-0978).
- `fm rule` (T-0971): your own regex rules on Bash commands or edited paths that block (denied with your why) or warn (a note), checked in PreToolUse after the guard. A path rule also covers Bash writes to the path and symlinks to it; a command too long to check fails closed. `fm rule rm` is yours to type; `fm rule add --from-veto N` turns a recorded veto into a stop.
- Test first at edit time (T-0969): the first source edit of a FIX/FEATURE task with no changed test and no failing run gets one line to write and record the failing test; once per task, never a block.
- A criterion whose check never actually ran (the command, file or test module it names didn't exist) can still be fixed with `fm task ac edit`; a check that ran and failed stays locked. Both recorded demo sessions had to drop and redo their task over this (T-0985).
- The README's live demo is a real session now (T-0979): Claude Code 2.1.301 with the current pane, working a real Foreman fix in a copy of this repo from one typed request to a red-then-green proof, close and commit. `demo/record_live.py --repo DIR --request TEXT [--dry] [--max-frames N]` records one; names, e-mails (account and git) and the home path are scrubbed.
- Postures and rivals (T-0963): `fm posture lite|full` (yours to type: only the guard, the evidence gate and `fm ask` grants run in a lite project); `fm compact-output shadow` records what it would cut and never shortens; verify commands that read Foreman's own task files are flagged; `fm bench duel --vs none` races Foreman against Claude with only the guard; a near-empty builder or reviewer report is logged and named by `fm next`.
- Stack cards reach the work (T-0977, T-0980): a request that names a library or topic with a card (react, postgres, kafka …) gets those cards named at prompt time, read only on demand; the map keeps the repo's language cards, so the first edit of a file gets its language's pitfalls; `fm doctor -v` counts cards named and read.
- New verbs (T-0960): `fm oops` (the newest work snapshot; `--apply` restores it after saving the current files), `fm round close` (tags round-N and lists the tasks closed since the last), `fm show` (an id, a file or words, routed to task show, why or recall), `fm release-check` (doctor and demo in a temporary HOME), `fm sensitive suggest` (signals only), `fm outcomes --readme` (one track-record sentence).
- Surfaces (T-0962): the status line honours NO_COLOR and names the current step ("step 2/3"); `fm ui --demo` prints a canned view; `fm page --catalogue NAME --picks FILE|-` stores an Artifact's add/veto picks; `fm sync status` prints the teammate join line; README shows a tmux status-bar line that follows the pane and never creates a project.
- `fm cost --forecast` says when the 5-hour and weekly usage windows fill at the current pace (T-0973).
- Lanes and internals (T-0961): a lane merge saves its base before `git merge` runs and restores it if the merge fails; one git runner (fmcore) behind lanes, secrets, the map and the bench, which tells a timeout from git's own "no match", so the bench's filter-driver guard and the secrets audit fail closed.
- Outside text (T-0972): a fetched page, search result or MCP answer that holds instruction-like text ("ignore previous instructions", `<system>` tags, curl piped to a shell, …) gets a note that it is data, not your request; no tokens spent.
- Knowledge out (T-0958): `fm recall --export-rules` writes each finished task's lessons to `.claude/rules/foreman-lessons/T-NNNN.md`, scoped by `paths:` to that task's files, so Claude Code loads a lesson only with its files; `fm decide --export-adr DIR` writes one ADR per decision; `fm task defer ID --until "when PATH changes"` brings a task back once the file changes.
- Brainstorm and cost (T-0959): a brainstorm child that fails after printing keeps its answer, flagged partial; `fm ideas` writes a manifest.json; `fm cost --sessions` labels each cache bust ttl or context edit; `fm friction --minutes` estimates the minutes friction cost this week; `fm burden --by-verb` lists the fm verbs nobody calls. Reports over days now read the rotated event logs too.
- Session signals (T-0957): plan mode holds the drive like a plan-only prompt; stops that leave open work idle are counted (`fm doctor -v`); your prompts are kept in the ledger (redacted, capped, never in a sensitive project); the first session after an upgrade shows what's new, once.
- Close-out (T-0956): `fm task finish` opens its warnings with one "N warnings: kinds" line, flags merge conflict markers and debugger lines in the task's added lines, and ends with "Landed: committed SHA · pushed/not pushed" or "not committed", counting only the task's own files.
- `fm run` blocks a task that made no progress (or is still unfinished after its sessions) with the reason, so the next run takes the next task, and keeps the machine awake under systemd-inhibit where it can. `fm night --print-timer` prints a systemd user timer and service and a crontab line, with your PATH and state dir, and installs nothing (T-0955).
- Edit-time stack cards (T-0949): the first edit of a file in a task gets the matching stack card's Avoid section, once per card per task (`fm lib inject off` turns it off). A language's own card wins over a topic card that only shares its extension. `fm doctor -v` counts 7 days of enforcement: validator blocks, refused or waived lane merges, injected cards.
- MCP (T-0946): the Foreman MCP server also serves the stack library and the read-only instruments, confined to the project and never serving .env, keys or .git. `fm mcp new NAME --wrap 'CMD'` scaffolds a tested, stdlib-only, argv-only MCP server for any CLI (interpreters only with a fixed script; output capped as it streams). `fm mcp register claude [DIR]` prints the add line.
- Doctor (T-0954): an `invisible text` check warns with file:line when CLAUDE.md, your rules, or Foreman's rules, skills, agents, commands or output styles hold zero-width, bidi, tag or variation characters that could hide instructions. The docs check now also reads `fm` commands inside code fences.
- Scout (T-0950): Foreman finds its own work. When the queue drains under full autonomy, `fm scout` captures what nobody asked for yet: a newer Claude Code to adopt, dependencies with no stack card, doctor failures, a weekly self-improvement pass and a weekly brainstorm. Each finding is captured once under its own key, at most 3 a run and 5 open. `fm night` runs it first; skill sources older than a week are refreshed.
- `fm check --affected` also runs tests that historically change with the edited files (T-0952).
- A criterion whose verify command reaches a live service (aws, gcloud, kubectl, terraform apply, …) gets a lint warning (T-0943).

## 1.2.30 — 2026-10-15
- Stack library (T-0933, T-0941): 1,078 on-demand cards in 27 areas.
  - The areas are languages, Python, Node, web, JVM, .NET, mobile, Rust, Go, C/C++ and embedded, data, ML and LLM apps, infra, cloud, ops, testing, security, architecture, product domains, tools, games, hardware, docs, idioms, building Claude Code extensions, and practice.
  - Each card has a summary, Verify commands, Test first, Do, Avoid and Review. None is loaded until read.
  - `fm lib` suggests the repo's cards from its manifests, files and extensions. Ecosystem markers such as a Makefile, package.json or *.tf only suggest the cards that own them.
  - `fm lib search` ranks cards alongside installed plugins' skills, disabled ones included.
  - `fm lib show A B --section verify` reads only what's needed. `fm lib list` and `fm lib check` (the lint) round it out.
  - The session map line names the repo's cards. The map rebuilds when the card count changes.
  - Manifests are parsed without backtracking regexes: a hostile 400 KB go.mod is handled in under 2 s.
- Guard (T-0940): a `cd DIR; python3 - <<EOF` refusal names the `&&` fix. `git reset --soft/--mixed` no longer counts as a tree write. A pinned plugin yes now says an update needs its own ask. `git fetch` isn't read as a download.
- Pane:
  - Live cards hold their place. One that empties stays dimmed ("idle · last: …") for 8 s and gives rows back one a second (T-0938).
  - A Skills card shows this week's skills invoked and stack cards read, the most used, and unread suggestions. `fm ui --json` carries a skills block (T-0939).
- Claude Code 2.1.299–2.1.301 (T-0944):
  - A subagent's `turn.start` no longer resets the pane's main-turn counts.
  - Text uses `weight` instead of the deprecated `bold`.
  - A bare slash command reaching UserPromptSubmit before expansion isn't read as a work request.
- `fm check affected` is configured for this repo, so builder lanes can run only the affected tests.
- `fm lib source` (T-0945) indexes third-party skill repos read-only.
  - Each is a shallow, markdown-only checkout in Foreman's state, with hooks, LFS, filters, submodules and symlinks off. Nothing from it runs.
  - `fm lib source defaults` adds six vetted repos: wshobson/agents, anthropics/skills, trailofbits/skills, VoltAgent subagents, K-Dense scientific skills and morluto/rea. That is about 870 skills and agents, searchable as `source:name` at no context cost.
  - `update` swaps atomically and prints added and removed names.
- Enforcement, so agents don't have to remember (T-0947, T-0948).
  - Every write is validated before Claude moves on: stack cards against the lint, JSON, Python, SKILL.md and agent frontmatter, and project checks set with `fm check onwrite GLOB 'CMD {file}'`. A failure blocks with the problem, in subagents too, from a validate-only sync hook beside the async catch-all.
  - `fm lane merge` re-runs the lane task's criteria in the worktree and refuses red. `--unverified REASON` waives it, but only from the main thread and only after logging the reason.
- foreman-ui 0.8.0: the stable pane, the Skills card, and the Claude Code 2.1.299 turn and weight changes.

## 1.2.29 — 2026-10-15
- Security (T-0906): the guard reads `T=$(mktemp -d)` as a temp path only when nothing in the command moves mktemp or TMPDIR (`PATH=`, `hash -p`, `enable`, `TMPDIR=`, its own `mktemp`); a command that set PATH could make `$(mktemp -d)` return `/` while the guard read it as /tmp.
- Round 5–6 review fixes: the Stop gate's "tests pass" check leaves a green run standing after a doc edit, knows mvn, gradle, dotnet, deno, node --test, bats and `make -jN test`, and no longer counts `fm checkpoint` or `toxic` as test runs (T-0907); promise capture skips offers ("if you want, I'll also…"), idioms and narration, and keeps at most three (T-0908).
- Round 7: bare `fm` in a project prints where things stand and the next action (T-0917); a close prints its undo (`git revert --no-edit <sha>`) and "Nothing needed from you" when every criterion was proved by its own check (T-0918); `fm demo` run inside a project ends with that project's next step (T-0919); `fm doctor` warns when a project's state passes 200 MB (T-0920) and when two Foreman installs differ in version (T-0923); `fm night` runs one at a time (T-0921); `fm tidy --apply` keeps dated backups of each project's record, the newest five (T-0922); `fm complete` gives tab completion for commands, options and live task ids (`eval "$(fm complete --shell bash)"`, T-0924).
- Round-7 review: the guard's mktemp rewrite also steps aside for any mention of TMPDIR, PATH, BASH_ENV or ENV and runs on the whole command only (T-0927, security); `fm complete` never syncs; tidy backs up without holding the project lock; doctor's state size leaves backups out; the tests-pass check counts only real test runs and ignores injected reminders (T-0928). Bare `fm inbox` lists the inbox, as `fm --help` says (T-0926). `fm agents install` and the README say the Codex, Gemini CLI and opencode adapters aren't yet verified against live CLIs (T-0929).
- The Foreman pane, redesigned (T-0909): it reads top-down by urgency. What needs you and plans waiting for approval come first, then the hero: the task, a stage rail (nodes for plan → close, a comet on the stage being worked), the steps bar, the current step, and time on the task against the usual time for its type and size. Below it: Now (each running call with a moving bar), Plan (a step glows green as it gets done; each unproved criterion names its check), Pulse (swim lanes of the session's tool calls by kind, tasks closed per day for 14 days, context, spend, hook and guard gauges), the queue and inbox, then the quieter cards. Moving charts repaint at about 30 fps while work runs and stay still when idle. The band above the prompt gets a mini stage rail; the size legend is gone; a pending question reads "Waiting for your answer", not running. `fm ui --json` adds `done_days`. A Motion setting (T-0912): full (about 30 fps charts), calm (the 8 fps clock only) or off (a still pane). A task that closes takes the top of the pane for six seconds (T-0913): a green sweep, its grade and lenses, how many criteria it proved, and its time against the usual. The quieter cards (pulse, files, fleet, gates, brainstorm, spend, bench, research, activity) fold to their head line with ▾/▸, and stay folded across sessions (T-0914).

## 1.2.28 — 2026-10-15
- Claude Code integration, round 1 of a recursive improvement loop (T-0760 brainstorm: 609 ideas in 30 clusters).
  - Never idle without tmux: a plugin monitor (`foreman-mail`) runs `fm bus watch` for the whole session and prints one line per new bus message for it; `fm bus send --wake` skips tmux when that watcher's heartbeat is fresh. The watcher finds its session through the SessionStart hook's process tree, follows it when Claude Code resumes another conversation in the same process, and drops its heartbeat when ended by a signal (T-0777, T-0819, T-0820, T-0841). A resume in the same process is no longer reported as "another session" (T-0841).
  - Phone push: the Stop hook asks once for PushNotification when an ask is open or the queue drains in full autonomy (T-0778). Sessions are named after their task (sessionTitle, T-0781).
  - Agents carry effort, maxTurns and omitClaudeMd, validated by `fm doctor` (T-0768); lane briefs pass a per-tier effort (S medium, M high, T-0779); Foreman's `claude -p` children carry `--max-budget-usd` (T-0776); wiring sets `worktree.baseRef=head` (T-0803); `fm doctor` names the Claude Code features the installed version is too old for (T-0785).
  - Sensitive repos keep content home: PushNotification, Artifact, SendMessage, RemoteTrigger and other outward tools are refused there, and the gate fails closed (T-0784, T-0821).
  - `fm page` renders the queue and a brainstorm catalogue as one self-contained HTML page for a claude.ai Artifact, with home paths refused and the bare username replaced (T-0786, T-0821). `fm research list` and `fm research show NAME` (T-0802).
- Opt-in compact output: `fm compact-output on` adds a PostToolUse hook that sends Bash outputs over 6,000 characters to the model as head, every failure line and tail, with the full text (capped at 2M characters) in a file; measured on 30 days of real outputs at about 1M tokens saved. Off until turned on; `fm tidy` prunes the saved texts after 7 days (T-0783, T-0811, T-0821, T-0824).
- Correctness.
  - One-off headless `claude -p` sessions answer and stop; only `fm run`'s sessions drive the queue (T-0775). Bench replays aren't one-off (T-0820).
  - A run whose output reports failing tests counts as a failure even when a pipe hides the exit, in `fm check` and in verify commands run from Bash (T-0773, T-0820). A verify that can't run at all is refused when the criterion is added (T-0765).
  - A lane-merged task is measured from the main checkout's files before the merge, so its close checks see its whole diff (T-0804, T-0812); tier drift counts only real diff lines; `fm lane brief` refuses a task its builder couldn't focus (T-0823); a lane's edits under `.claude/worktrees/` count as the repo files they are (T-0807).
  - `fm task finish` refuses a close that leaves Foreman's self docs failing (T-0764); `fm task new --redo ID` keeps a dropped task's start point (T-0767); `fm task set` takes list fields as `a,b` or `[a, b]` (T-0810).
  - `fm` ends quietly when its reader quits (`fm … | head`), exiting 141 like a shell's SIGPIPE (T-0825, T-0839).
  - The step pointer follows the work: an edit to a file named only in a later step moves the current step there, and the 8th edit on a step with no evidence says once how to move it (T-0759, T-0839).
  - `fm lane rm` removes a lane whose only leftovers are build output (target/, build/, dist/, zig, cmake), but refuses when a key, `.env` or credentials file sits inside one (T-0833, T-0839). `fm task finish --commit` handles a file the task untracked and deleted (T-0837).
  - The stray repo-root `skill-index.json` cache (local paths) is no longer tracked (T-0836).
- Guard: a data heredoc writing an unrun source file isn't interpreter code driving Foreman (T-0766); a literal `cd DIR && python3 -` resolves relative writes in DIR only (T-0774); a data-only `python -c` may send output to /dev/null; state-direct refusals point to `fm research add`; path-only rule ids read `:path` (T-0817).
- Builders run only the affected tests (T-0806); the hook benchmark judges by CPU time on a busy machine (T-0805); `fm audit prep` asks for ReportFindings fields (T-0780).
- Round 4: a newcomer's first hour (T-0843 brainstorm: 437 ideas in 31 clusters, grounded against a clean-HOME walkthrough and a survey of rival plugins).
  - `fm --help` opens with the few commands a newcomer needs, `fm help --all` lists the rest; `fm task new -h` shows types, tiers and an example; `fm task new` reuses a same-titled capture; `fm task done`'s refusal leads with `fm task finish` (T-0854).
  - `fm doctor` prints only problems (`-v` for every check) and warns when there are no backups; gate detection picks pytest or `python3 -m unittest discover` only when nothing else runs the tests; `fm check` with no gates prints a ready `fm check add` line; the digest says "folded into another" for merged tasks (T-0855).
  - `install.sh` links `~/.local/bin/fm` (never over someone else's `fm`) and asks before setting bypassPermissions on a terminal (default no; `--bypass` opts in, no terminal leaves the mode alone); uninstall removes only what it added (T-0855, T-0871).
  - `fm demo` walks a throwaway project through a whole task in under a second, with no model calls (T-0870). INSTALL.md is written for agents, and the README says when not to use Foreman (T-0872). Help text carries no ticket ids (T-0873).
  - A bare marketplace install (`/plugin marketplace add ChaseSunstrom/foreman`, `/plugin install foreman@foreman`) works; its first session reads Foreman's rules from the plugin and offers to finish setup (T-0874).
  - Foreman's own `claude -p` children run in one private folder, and `fm tidy` sweeps the empty project folders they left behind (T-0856).
- Correctness, round 4.
  - `fm repeats` never suggests a narrowed test run as a gate (T-0857). `fm friction --brief` marks the pass in progress so it isn't called for again while its recon runs; distinct notes of one kind no longer count as repeats (T-0858, T-0860).
  - Guard: a bare `git init` (or `-q`) in a folder that already holds `.git` isn't a tree write over core; any other form, or any `GIT_*` variable in the command, still is, and the refusal says to chain the cd with `&&` or pass the path (T-0859).
  - `fm check --affected` runs a test file the task added or changed even before the map links it (T-0875). Evidence runs drop bytecode older than its source, so a same-second, same-size edit never runs stale code (T-0876). A check's track record counts `fm task prove`'s failing start-tree run (T-0880).
- Round 5 (54 ideas, every one grounded against the code; 7 built).
  - Work snapshots: before a Bash command discards uncommitted work (`git reset --hard`, `checkout -- .`, `restore`, `clean -f`, `switch -f`, an `--abort`, `worktree remove --force`, `stash drop`/`clear`…), Foreman saves it under `refs/foreman/snapshots` within 3 s and says so (or that it couldn't); `fm snapshots` lists them with the command that brings one back; `fm tidy` prunes them after 14 days (T-0884).
  - `fm night` runs the sentinel: closed tasks' checks against HEAD, a new failure bisected and captured (T-0888). `fm why` quotes the user's own request behind a line (T-0889). `fm projects --next` ranks every project's next action in one list (T-0890).
  - `fm tour` gives each changed file a review-risk verdict (review, glance, safe to skim) with its reasons and names what to review first; `fm tour --since main` does it for a whole branch or night's run, uncommitted work included (T-0885). A bare "continue" is logged as a stall with what the Stop hook did before it, and `fm friction` counts them (T-0887).
  - A reply saying the tests pass is held once when the turn's own runs don't back it: a file edited after the last test run, or that run failed (T-0886).
  - Security: commands fm stores and runs later — verify commands (`--ac "x :: CMD"`, `--verify`, intake `DONE-WHEN`), `fm check affected`'s template, `fm notify` — are checked by the guard when stored and again before fm runs them; before, `fm task finish` ran a stored `rm -rf ~` unread (T-0901).
  - Security: the guard now reads the command inside `fm quiet -- <cmd>` (it passed unread, so `fm quiet -- rm -rf ~` got through) and inside the new `fm at TARGET -- <cmd>`, which runs a command as of a task's start or any commit in a throwaway worktree (T-0898).
  - The README opens with a recording of Claude Code itself with the foreman-ui mod fixing a seeded bug (`demo/record_live.py` re-records it; `demo/live.mp4` to share); `shot.py` gains `render()` and no longer passes this session's `CLAUDE_*` variables to the Claude Code it harnesses (T-0903).
  - Showcase: the README opens with a recording of `fm demo` (animated SVG; `demo/record.py` re-records it, `--mp4` for a shareable video), a scannable why/quick start, and the long detail folded; `fm demo --pace 1` plays the demo at a watchable speed with colour (T-0902).
  - CI: the suite runs on push and pull requests on Linux (Python 3.12 and 3.14), macOS reported (T-0882).
- Round 6 (49 ideas grounded; 6 built).
  - `fm task drop` keeps an in-progress task's work as a snapshot named in its brief (T-0895). A RESEARCH task closes with a Decision, which lands in decisions.md (T-0896). `fm spec` starts from the examples the user wrote and keeps them when the model child fails (T-0897).
  - `fm at TARGET -- CMD` runs a command as of a task's start or any commit, in a throwaway worktree (T-0898).
  - A reply's own promises ("I'll also…", "I'll follow up on…") become inbox items when the turn ends (T-0899). A close lists the criteria no command of their own proved, to check by hand (T-0900).
  - Guard: `T=$(mktemp -d)` is a known temp folder, so `rm -r $T` later isn't refused (T-0892); a path-only refusal's rule id names the file kind (T-0894). The guard tests keep their temp folder outside /tmp on a fresh HOME, as on a CI runner (T-0883).

## 1.2.27 — 2026-10-09
- Learning, paid half (T-0752 milestone: T-0734, T-0743).
  - Blocking a task writes its eval case at once (local). `fm evals inbox` lists blocks, plus guard blocks later granted for the same task as false-positive candidates.
  - Bench holdout: a stable hash holds out one case in five, and `fm evolve` never trains on those. `fm bench list` and `fm bench run` take `--split train|holdout`, and `fm bench scorecard` prints each saved run's train and holdout pass rates.
  - `fm repeats --draft` writes a playbook draft from steps that 3+ finished tasks took in the same order. Adopting it is asked first.
  - Per-model lesions: when the newest model has no ablations yet and the project has bench cases, `fm night` queues `fm evolve --drop` for the next rule or skill file. Evolve events now record the model.
  - Smoke fates: with a smoke target set, `fm night` runs `fm smoke` locally, and `fm outcomes` marks tasks closed in the 7 days before a failing smoke run.
  - `fm recall --lessons --retire T-0123.1 --why "…"` keeps a lesson out of recall and tripwires. The bench gate comes later.
  - `fm task finding ID LENS confirmed|rejected "<finding>"` records each reviewer finding's verdict, and `fm usage --agents` shows per-lens precision.
- Runtime (T-0751 milestone: T-0731, T-0732).
  - `fm run --failover codex|gemini|opencode`: when a usage limit outlasts `--wait`, the task's handoff packet goes to a detached `fm session` of that agent and the run stops. It is opt-in per run, because the packet leaves for that provider.
  - `fm task revert ID` lists the task's commits, the open tasks that depend on or name it, and the `git revert` command. It never reverts anything itself.
  - `fm run --fit` takes S tasks before M before L, so more finish before a limit. It turns on by itself while usage runs ahead of pace, and a task waiting on an open one keeps waiting.
  - `fm lane new ID --spike` makes a throwaway lane that `fm lane merge` refuses.
- `fm night` pre-plans: the first queued L task without a plan review gets `fm second plan` overnight. Like other optional work, it waits while usage runs ahead of pace (T-0730).
- Test hygiene: tests always see a calm host, and the screenshot harness uses its own tmux socket, so the suite no longer fails when the machine is loaded (T-0744). Catch rates, give-up mining and plan gaps read real run results through one parser, and the check track record counts failures that `fm task finish` recorded (T-0749, T-0750).

  - Standing steers: at close, a steer worded as a rule ("never …", "from now on", "always", "by default") is listed as a rule candidate. A standing "no" becomes a veto proposal in `fm taste` right away instead of after three repeats; adopting it still needs the user's yes.
  - `fm outcomes` adds the fate "corrected" when the user's correction is logged against a task after it closed.

- Steps and execution craft (T-0695 milestone: T-0596, T-0610, T-0632, T-0633, T-0649, T-0650).
  - A step can say `(expect: TEXT)`. `fm task evidence --step N --run CMD` marks the run "expect ✓" or "expect missed" and warns on a miss.
  - `fm audit prep` adds a "Files vs steps" section: each changed file, the steps that name it, and whether it is outside the scope. The reviewer says which step each hunk serves.
  - The same failing command a third time on a step gets a nudge and is logged as tried, and `fm resume` lists what was tried.
  - First focus prints a preflight: scope paths that don't exist, uncommitted files outside the scope, and whether the gates ran green on this exact tree.
  - `fm resume` re-runs the last green check (capped at 2 minutes) and reports drift. `--no-check` skips it.
  - The close warns about debug scaffolding in added lines: `breakpoint()`, pdb, `debugger;`, `console.log` in JS/TS outside tests, and DEBUG prints.

  - `fm deps --calls`: each dependency's installed version (read locally) and its import sites with the names used, so release notes can be read against real call sites. planning.md says to probe the installed version before trusting docs.
  - Every `fm research ask` note ends with a Decision prompt (recommendation, would change if). A RESEARCH task that closes without a Decision section warns.
  - Chesterton check: closing a task that deleted tracked files warns until its Origins section says why each one existed.
  - `fm recall --repos <text>`: prior art from other projects on this machine that opted in with `fm share on` and aren't sensitive, cited file:line and redacted.
  - `fm recall --explain "<question>"`: where the identifiers a question names are defined, and the files that use them most.
  - `fm map --cold`: the most-changed files no session has read. `--capture` files one inbox item, and a free night job runs it. Research debt never sends these to web research.
- Reasoning and thought process (T-0693 milestone: T-0593, T-0609, T-0630, T-0631).
  - The stuck ladder names the stall from a step's failed runs. It is either the environment (a missing command, file, port or permission), the same error again (the fix isn't reaching the cause), or errors that change each run (progress, so keep steps small).
  - `fm second plan ID --role devil` argues for the approach the plan rejected and objects to steps that answer a different question.
  - `fm task note ID fact|question "…"` keeps a typed case file in the brief's Notes section, and `fm checkpoint` and `fm resume` print it.
  - `fm task assume ID add "<fact>" --kill "<what would show it false>"` records a kill criterion. `fm next` lists open beliefs with their kill criteria until each is verified.
- Planning and decomposition, part 2 (T-0692 milestone: T-0645, T-0666).
  - Plan from history: at close, a task keeps a "Plan gaps" section listing the steps added after work started and the steps whose first run failed. `fm focus` shows "steps similar plans added late" from related finished tasks.
  - Step contracts: a step can say `(produces: PATH, …)` and `(requires: PATH, …)`. `fm focus` flags an undone step whose product already exists, and a requirement that doesn't exist and that no earlier step produces.

## 1.2.23 — 2026-10-09
- Intent and change skills, first slice (T-0713).
  - `fm spec "<wish>" --module M [--out FILE]` turns a one-line wish into a red unittest. One tool-less child (Haiku by default) proposes assertions; only safe ones are kept (comparisons over calls into M and literals: no imports, dunders, eval or open). The file is run once to show it red. A real run on "calc.mul multiplies two numbers…" gave 7 assertions, all red.
  - `fm rewrite OLD NEW [--glob G] [--apply] [--convert]` renames in one rule: Python by tokens (strings and comments untouched), other code by whole word. It names every residual site: strings, comments, and other spellings such as OldName, oldName, OLD_NAME, old-name. `--convert` hands the residual lines to a cheap child and keeps only lines that lose every spelling of the old name. On a copy of Foreman's library, `regen_views → refresh_views` changed 38 code sites in 14 files; the 2 residuals inside the guard's regex strings were converted, leaving 0 residual sites, and every file still parses.
- Self-improvement flywheel, first slice (T-0712).
  - `fm burden [--days N]` measures Foreman's own cost on real sessions, per Foreman version (from the plugin root each session started on): the share of turns spent only on Foreman bookkeeping, guard refusals and refused fm calls per 100 turns, and the context Foreman's hooks injected per turn. This is the objective a self-change must lower. Last 7 days here: bookkeeping-only turns 2%, guard refusals 1.5 per 100 turns.
  - `fm replay --candidate REF|FILE` scores another guard (a git ref of Foreman's repo, or a `fmguard.py` file) against the working one on the corpus of real past commands. Loosened verdicts are listed first and exit 1, then tightened ones. The guard from 12 guard-commits back blocks `curl … | python3 -c` as pipe-into-shell, the false block T-0716 removed.
  - The overnight run that generates candidates waits for usage headroom.

## 1.2.22 — 2026-10-09
- Cockpit, first slice (T-0711). The Foreman pane gains a Fleet card: every live session on this machine with project, task, context and mail, plus a tile for each session on a remote the user added. Its steer box runs `fm conductor steer`, which reaches every session here and on those remotes. The task card lists the parts `fm task split` made, with their progress.
  - `fm conductor remote add NAME user@host [FM_PATH]`, `remote rm NAME` and `remote` (list). A remote's sessions are fetched over the user's own ssh (BatchMode, 5 s connect timeout) by a detached refresh every 60 s and cached, so the pane never waits on the network. Nothing is fetched from a machine that wasn't added.
- Milestone merge train, first slice (T-0710). `fm task finish ID --commit "<msg>" --stack [--stack-check CMD]` lands a task as a stack: one commit per step, or per member for an `fm batch` host. Each file goes with the step it was last edited in (the ledger's order of edits and step evidence). Each commit is checked alone in a scratch worktree with `--stack-check` or its member's own verify commands, and one that fails alone is folded into the next. Only the task's paths are reset in the index, so other staged work stays. From 30 days of history: 30 batches cost 61M input-equivalent against about 104M estimated for their members done alone (by tier medians), cheaper in 17 of 30.
- Orchestrators of orchestrators, first slice (T-0709).
  - `fm task split ID [--parts K] [--files …] [--dry-run]` partitions a task's files (its scope, or `fm graph pack`'s read-set) along the code's seams. It merges the most-linked groups over the work graph's code, co-change and test edges, and each part becomes a child task scoped to its files and told which seams to keep.
  - `fm task capsule ID` is a child's return to its parent: at most 15 lines covering status, files and lines changed, verification, decisions, lessons, follow-ups and risks.
  - A parent can't close while a child is open. Children run through lanes and builders like any task, and can split again.
  - `fm map` now links a test to a source whose name is a short prefix plus the test's (`test_bus.py` → `fmbus.py`).
  - The proof run (one L task as a tree against one session) waits for usage headroom.
- Orchestrator control plane, first slice (T-0708). All of it is local and costs no tokens until something happens.
  - `fm bus send <session|all> "<text>" [--type note|steer|stop] [--wake]`: the message reaches the session on its next tool call, once. A session about to stop is held for unread mail. `--wake` types one short line into a session's tmux pane; the statusline now records `TMUX_PANE`.
  - Leases: an edit leases the function it lands in for 20 minutes, renewed with each edit; a Write leases the file. Another session's edit inside that function is refused, naming the holder and how to message it; edits elsewhere in the file go through. `fm lease list|take|drop`.
  - `fm conductor` lists live sessions with project, task, context, unread mail and leases. `fm conductor steer "<text>" [--wake]` steers them all.

## 1.2.21 — 2026-10-09
- Debugging intuition engine, first slice (T-0706). A red run now comes with what a debugger would gather first.
  - `fm suspects` ranks files for a failure from signals already on hand: repo frames on the stack, what the task changed, the sources linked to the failing tests, and edit recency. The failure hook adds its top 3 to a failing test run.
  - `fm whyred CMD`: delta debugging (ddmin over hunks) of the task's change against its start tree, in a scratch copy, capped at 32 runs. It names the minimal hunks that turn a green command red.
  - `fm record CMD` reruns a Python command with a `sys.monitoring` flight recorder (3.12+) and shows the locals of each repo frame the failure unwound through.
  - Localisation gym: 30 seeded red episodes in a scratch copy of Foreman, each one bug plus 4 harmless edits. The root cause was in the top 3 in 29/30 (96%) and first in 27; stack-only got 15/30 and random order 60%.
- Knowledge graph, first slice (T-0707). `fm graph` keeps one local sqlite graph per project: code edges (graphify's `graphify-out/graph.json` when present, else name mentions), test links, task→file edges from `Foreman-Task` trailers and touched events, and co-change between files, with time on every edge that has one.
  - `fm graph blast PATH…`: dependents, co-change partners, linked tests and the FIX tasks that touched it.
  - `fm graph pack "<task text>"`: a ranked read-set by personalized PageRank from the words, past tasks and the code graph.
  - Both take `--as-of`. Backtest over 140 finished Foreman tasks, as of each task's creation: pack recalled 56% of the files the task changed in its top 10, against 10% for keyword retrieval on paths (better on 106 tasks, worse on 7). The blast radius of the first changed library file held 67% of the task's other changed files.
- Ambient verification, first slice (T-0705). `fm check ambient on` (off by default): after each edit, the affected tests (the `fm check --affected` selection) run in a detached process, out of the model's turns. The model hears only a flip, passing tests that now fail or failing ones that pass again, as one note on its next tool call; a passing run is the current step's evidence (dropped in T-0994). Skipped on a strained host. Replaying 10 seeded regressions in a fixture repo: 10/10 caught, reported about 0.17 s after the edit, with no test command run by the model.
- Zero-call bookkeeping, first slice (T-0704). Over 14 days, 460 tasks closed with 618 `fm task evidence` calls that re-ran a command the session had just run, and 135 refused finishes retried one gap at a time.
  - A passing command run through the same runner as one of the task's verify commands or a project gate (e.g. `python3 plugin/tests/run.py -k X`, `npm test`, `cargo test`) records itself as the current step's evidence. Piped or `;`-chained commands don't count (their exit isn't the check's), nor do steps about failing or reproducing.
  - `fm task finish` checks lenses, docs impact, the lesson and step evidence before running anything and names every gap in one refusal.
  - `fm task packet ID` carries a Reproduce section (the newest failing command fm ran); `--check` reruns it and accepts the handoff only while the failure still reproduces.
- Cache economics, first slice (T-0703). Over 30 days, cache reads were 78% of spend (every turn re-reads the whole context; this machine's sessions average 258–339k tokens per turn).
  - `fm cost --sessions`: per session, turns, context per turn, the cache read/write/fresh/output split and cache busts (turns that re-wrote over half of a 30k+ context).
  - The Stop hook's task-boundary note now also fires past 200k context tokens, not only at 60% of the window, and names the tokens.
  - foreman-ui compacts at a task close past `freshTokens` (default 200,000) as well as `freshAt` percent.

  - `fm sentinel --bisect` runs `git bisect` in a throwaway worktree for each re-check that fails now, and captures the commit that broke it, and its task, as a FIX.
  - `fm check --repeat N` reports each gate's pass rate.
  - `fm task finish` warns about changed source files that no linked test changed or ran.
  - `fm smoke set cmd "…" [--expect RE]` and `fm smoke set http URL [--status N] [--json-key K]` check products without a web UI.
  - Product journeys (Playwright steps per criterion) are deferred. A flaky-gate quarantine was skipped: a gate flaky twice stays a failure, so the race gets found.
- UI and motion (T-0681 milestone: T-0473, T-0472, T-0500).
  - A task's close-out toast carries its grade and the lenses it closed with (`✔ T-0123 done · strong · adversary, edge`), from new `title`, `grade` and `lenses` fields on `fm ui --json`'s closed tasks.
  - The band shows one quiet glyph only when something is amber or red: a failing gate, usage ahead of pace, blocked tasks or hook errors. They come from `fm ui --json`'s new `signals`.
  - A guard refusal of a long command quotes the part that matched, even when the guard names `~/x` as `/home/…/x`.
- Self-improvement and learning, first versions (T-0680 milestone: T-0499, T-0454, T-0470, T-0471).
  - The hooks log every note they put into context (hook, a short kind, its characters) and every tripwire lesson they show.
  - The friction digest turns that into a note budget per kind, the notes repeated 3+ times (candidates for a hard check instead of prose) and how often each lesson was shown.
  - `fm friction --reject TEXT --why WHY` records a dead end that later digests skip and count.
  - The bench-bound half (auto-evals on a block, a holdout split, playbook drafts, tuning per model) is captured (T-0734).
- Resilience, state and simplicity, first versions (T-0679 milestone: T-0453, T-0495, T-0468, T-0469, T-0494, T-0496).
  - The task lifecycle is one table (`fmcore.TRANSITIONS`). A chaos test runs random lifecycles, crashes them at a random write and checks that the state still loads and that every status change was a legal one. It found two bugs, now fixed: `fm task block` turned a dropped task into a blocked one, and `fm task finish` marked a dropped task done.
  - `fm usage --prune` lists the fm commands no session ran in the window, in any project, as one CLEAN capture to review. Nothing is removed.
  - `fm wiring` shows on one screen the version, hooks, project flags, standing yeses, trust, pause, budget and enabled plugins.
  - `fm doctor` warns when a project's status views don't match its briefs; five did here, left by older formats. `--repair` regenerates them.
  - `plugin/tests/mutate_guard.py` flips one operator at a time in fmguard's check functions and reports the mutants the guard tests miss.
- One-command releases (T-0678 milestone: T-0493, T-0492). `python3 release.py VERSION` bumps plugin.json and Foreman's marketplace entries, heads the Unreleased changelog entries with the version, runs `fm check` (and undoes the bump if it fails), and commits. It refuses a dirty tree or an older version. `--push URL` merges into that remote's main from a scratch clone. `--canary` first re-runs `fm sentinel` in every local project and prints what fails there now.

## 1.2.19 — 2026-10-09
- Planning, briefs and the queue, first versions (T-0677 milestone: T-0450, T-0451, T-0452, T-0488, T-0489, T-0490, T-0491).
  - `fm task defer ID WHY --until DATE`: `fm next` names it again from that day.
  - `fm next` says how long this type and tier usually takes here.
  - `fm orders add --every 1w|--on-change PATH "…"`, with `list`, `rm N` and `run`: standing orders capture a task when due, once per period or change, and `fm night` runs them.
  - `fm task done` warns when the diff is a size bigger than the planned tier.
  - `fm task assume ID add FACT --check CMD`: `fm sentinel` re-runs the check after the task is done and captures a FIX when it breaks.
  - A FEATURE L is approved only with a "Build vs reuse" section.
  - `fm doctor` names lib modules MASTER.md doesn't.
  - Deferred: `fm task revert`, budget-fit ordering, a SPIKE type.
- Orchestration, lanes and fleets, first versions (T-0675 milestone: T-0466, T-0465, T-0446, T-0447).
  - `fm task packet ID [--out PATH]` writes one redacted markdown handoff for a person or another machine: the brief, criteria, steps, evidence, the blocker, the hypotheses tried and the next probe.
  - `fm lane new` refuses, and the drive offers no builder lane, while the host is loaded past twice its CPUs or has under 512 MB available.
  - A conflicting `fm lane merge` writes `audits/ID.conflict.md` (files, conflict hunks, the tasks behind main's side) with the steps to resolve it on the branch.
  - `fm run` stops a session whose transcript hasn't moved for `--stall` minutes (default 20) and goes on; a session it can't observe is never stopped.
  - T-0485's idle-time pre-plans and the usage-limit handoff to another agent are captured (T-0730, T-0731).
- Outline-first reads (T-0724, the biggest lever T-0717 measured). The first full Read of a file over 600 lines (code, Markdown or a diff) gets its outline and a range to read instead. Read it again with no range to get all of it. This is per agent and resets at compaction. Over 30 days it would have caught 367 reads holding 4.1M tokens; with a range read after each, that saves about 3.1M tokens. It has its own PreToolUse entry, which never blocks a read on failure, and adds about 30 ms per Read.
- The diff a reviewer reads is 11% smaller across 48 real audit diffs, and up to 21% on one (T-0723, from the T-0717 measurement: reviewers read 3.2M tokens of diffs in 30 days). A file whose change repeats another's, such as the synced eval copies of the rules, becomes one line naming the first. Context lines are cut at 200 characters. Fixtures and lockfiles are listed with their +/- counts. Changed lines stay whole, and the pre-audit still reads the raw diff.
- Friction (T-0729: T-0725, T-0726). `fm lane rm` unlocks a worktree Claude Code still locks for a finished builder once its branch is in main; while the branch isn't merged, it says to merge first. `fm capture` no longer calls a request a re-ask of a dropped batch just because the batch's huge request shares a few of its words: the shared words must be a fifth of the dropped request's too.

## 1.2.18 — 2026-10-09
- `fm recall --ask` redacts passages before indexing them, caps what one question indexes at 20 M characters, and shows at most 50 answers (T-0728, from a commit security review). SQLite's snippet put `[ ]` around a matched word inside a secret, so the redactor no longer recognised it on the way out.
- Performance and cost (T-0676 milestone: T-0467, T-0486, T-0449, T-0487, T-0448).
  - `fm check` runs gates by failure odds per second of median runtime, so fail-fast stops sooner; results stay in configured order.
  - Each `hook_ms` event carries Foreman's version, and `fm doctor` warns when the p95 of the last 200 PreToolUse runs passes the hook budget, naming the version where it rose.
  - While usage runs ahead of pace, optional work waits with a note: the drive's side offers, the self-improvement pass and `fm ideas --deepen`. The queue and inbox keep flowing, and the caps still block.
  - `fm task finish` logs the session's model, type, tier and grade, and `fm cost --by-model` counts them.
  - At a step boundary of an M/L task with context nearly full, the Stop note says to checkpoint and compact there.
- Observability, replay and trust (T-0674 milestone: T-0464, T-0483, T-0484).
  - `fm explain [block|drive]` shows what's behind the last guard block or drive/Stop decision: the rule, its inputs, the grants or settings, the ledger events and the hook breaker.
  - `fm task show ID --story` prints a task's ledger as chapters (Plan, Steps, Evidence, Reviews, Close) with times, archived months included.
  - `fm recall --ask "QUESTION"` ranks passages from briefs, the ledger, decisions and research with SQLite FTS5's BM25 ranking, redacts them and cites the task ids behind each.
- `fm pause` now holds every claude launcher (T-0591, from the T-0436 review). `fm session start|send`, `fm ideas`, `fm oracle`, `fm research ask`, `fm bench` (its run commands) and `fm relate` refuse while paused, and the SessionStart relate spawn waits. Each command builder checks the pause too, so a missed path still stops. `fm pause off` is the user's to run: the guard refuses it from Claude and says to type `! fm pause off`.
- Heredocs fed to Python are no longer read as shell just because the line also sources a file (T-0714, from JARVIS). `. .venv/bin/activate` or `bash ./run.sh` after a `python3 - <<'EOF'` edit made the guard read the body as shell, so a docstring mentioning `` `at` `` was refused as scheduling a command. Only a shell or source that reads its stdin as code (`bash`, `sh -s`, `. /dev/stdin`, `bash -c 'source /dev/stdin'`) or eval counts now. Replaying 5,000 real commands shows no change.
- Close-out friction (T-0722: T-0718, T-0720). `fm audit prep` of a builder's task now reviews its branch from where it left main, not main's own later commits. `fm task finish --commit` checks for credentials before closing, so a flagged line leaves the task open to fix. A done task's refused commit can be retried with `fm task finish ID --commit MSG`.

## 1.2.17 — 2026-10-09
- Zero-token instruments (T-0701, the first slice of Frontier 01). Each answers in 15–40 lines with the root cause first.
  - `fm instruments [--json]` lists five typed tools with their input schemas and line caps.
  - `fm sym PATH:NAME` shows a definition, the names it uses and its callers.
  - `fm fail` reduces a test run to the failing test, its error and the failing source.
  - `fm logs FILE [--since-good FILE]` groups a log into templates with counts, errors first, and with `--since-good` shows what's new against a good run. On 51,406 real journal lines it printed 40 lines (7.6 MB → 4.5 KB), and the first line was the crash-looping service and its missing library.
  - `fm data FILE` gives a csv/json/jsonl/sqlite file's schema, stats and first rows.
  - `fm trace` maps a Python or JS stack trace to repo lines, each with the commit date and task that last touched it.
  - Review fixes: numeric lines fold into one template; new errors lead; output is redacted; reads are bounded; one bad sqlite byte no longer loses the file; symlinks can't escape the repo.
- Autonomy and the user (T-0671 milestone: T-0439, T-0461, T-0477).
  - `fm taste` proposes a veto once the same no comes up in three steers; the user's one yes adopts it. `fm decide --ask Q --options …` then takes its default from the taste record and never pre-answers a guard category. Synonyms and plurals count too: push, force-push, plugins, credential, delete, sudo, publish.
  - In standard autonomy, non-urgent asks wait in one digest with a deadline, and each takes its default when the deadline passes. An unreadable deadline is reset, not applied.
  - `fm capture --from-file PATH|-` attaches a log, a paste or a screenshot path. The excerpt is redacted line by line before it's cut. Credentials files are refused, and a paste is capped at 2 MB.
- The pipe-into-shell rule closes its review's gaps (T-0716). An option's value no longer passes for the script (`| bash -o posix`, `| python3 -W ignore`, `| node -r ./hook.js`); `| bash -s ARGS` reads stdin; a bare word isn't taken for a script file. A piped group or computed name (`| (sh)`, `| $SH`) counts. Inline code that runs what it reads counts too: `bash -c 'source /dev/stdin'`, `exec bash`, `eval "$(cat)"`, a `read` loop running each line, `python3 -c 'exec(sys.stdin.read())'`, `perl -ne 'system $_'`, `awk '{system($0)}'`, `xargs sh -c`. Data readers such as `json.load(sys.stdin)`, `re.compile` and `| $GREP pat` stay allowed. Replaying 5,000 real commands shows 0 new blocks.
- A shell or interpreter reading its program from a pipe is refused (T-0715, found probing the T-0698 review). `echo 'rm -rf ~' | bash` ran unread; so did `| sh -s`, `| python3`, `| node -` and `| source /dev/stdin`. None of 5,000 real commands does this, and a heredoc, which the guard reads, does the same job. Inline code (`-c`, `-e`, `-m`) and a script file are unchanged. A download piped in stays `pipe-shell`, which can be granted for an installer.
- Friction batch (T-0698, from a self-improvement pass):
