- `fm queue --preview` (T-0114, from the brainstorm's overnight dry-run idea): each queued and inbox item with the usual time for its type and size here, and what will need you (plan yeses, open asks, a core yes its scope implies), gathered at the top so they can be answered together before a long run.
- Lanes (T-0134, toward parallel work): a git worktree of the repo is a lane with its own active task, so two sessions can each work a task without pausing or touching the other's; each side's next and queue skip the other's work, the edit gate holds in a lane, and commits land on the lane's branch. `fm lane new|list|rm` manages them (rm never discards uncommitted work). Before, a worktree wasn't Foreman's project at all.
- foreman-ui split (T-0130): the transcript rows moved to `rows.tsx` and the shared constants to `state.ts` (register.tsx 1637 → 1323 lines), behaviour locked by the 57 mod tests and `claude plugin validate`. Two engine rules shape any further split: `$` can't be passed across an import, and an atom is read only in the file that declares it.
- Gates by path (T-0126): `fm check paths N GLOB…` lets a gate declare what it covers; `fm check` skips it while nothing under those paths changed since its last pass, and says so. This repo's mod tests, hook bench and e2e roundtrip now run only when their files changed.
- A step (or criterion) whose newest run failed closes when the same command passed afterwards (T-0165, self-inbox from T-0164): the intended red run of a red→green pair no longer needs a third run; a failure after the pass still refuses.
- The inbox is ranked by value for effort (T-0111): within the intake type order, items are ordered by who asked, how many items wait on them and how long they waited, per tier; dependencies come first. It was oldest-first.
- Fix (T-0164, a regression from T-0086 found closing T-0087): a Bash edit's touch is timed by the file's mtime, not by the async hook that logs it after the command, so a `scope:` reason logged in the same command covers the edit instead of reading as older and refusing the finish.
- `fm help` (T-0094): every command once, in six tiers with the everyday ones first; bare `fm` prints it instead of a usage error. `fm help` used to be an error and `fm -h` one unordered list of 51 commands.
- Hook circuit breaker (T-0087): a hook that fails 3 times in a row pauses for 10 minutes instead of failing on every event, and says so in the band (a warning row), the dashboard's hooks line, `fm doctor` and hooks.log; one success clears it, and the guard (PreToolUse) never pauses.
- Bash edits are attributed (T-0086): after each Bash call during a task, `git status` (about 2 ms, in the async PostToolUse hook) names the files whose mtime falls inside the call; they count as the task's touches and one outside its scope gets the live scope note an Edit gets (once per file). `sed -i` and generators were invisible until the task's finish.
- Rules diet (T-0085): the always-on rules drop what a gate already says when it refuses (planning needed to focus, `fm gates`/`fm task prove`, each tier's audits, how each guard category is granted, the state folder): 5881 → 5566 characters loaded in every session. The red→green refusal now names `fm task prove`.
- `FOREMAN_QUIET=1` (T-0077): a session an orchestrator drives (jarvis-code had to disable the plugin with `--settings`) gets no Foreman context, nudges or brief requirement; the guard still runs.
- Guard: a `#` right after a closing `)` is mid-word, not a comment (T-0163, automated security review of T-0161): `echo $(true)#x; rm -rf ~` and `$((1))#x` hid the rest of the line with any payload; the differential test now has those forms and the backtick and `${…}` ones.
- Work done while a task was paused isn't its own change (T-0136): re-focusing a task after another task committed a file (or the user edited one) counted those files as the task's, so its finish was refused for edits outside its scope, and audit diffs and `fm check --affected` included them. A pause now snapshots the files; a re-focus moves the start point past the work done meanwhile (the task's own changes reverse-applied in a throwaway index), or keeps it and logs why when the same lines changed.
- Guard: `rm` variable resolution also stops at a computed command name (T-0162, automated security review of T-0161): `X=read; $X D <<< ~`, a backtick command or a glob such as `rea?` ran `read` and set the variable while the guard kept its earlier literal; all three are in the differential test.
- `fm secrets` and task-tagged commits (T-0132, user request): a credential audit of the working tree, Claude Code's config (settings `env`, MCP servers in `.mcp.json` and `~/.claude.json`, file permissions, whether git tracks a project config) and, with `--history`, every branch's history; it names place and kind, never the value, and exits 1 on a finding (0.14 s on this repo; its 11 fake test credentials are now marked `pragma: allowlist secret`). `fm task finish --commit` refuses lines that look like a credential, tags the commit `Foreman-Task: T-…` and logs it in the brief.
- Guard: `rm` variable resolution fails closed, and a `#` inside a word no longer hides the rest of the line (T-0161, from the automated security review of T-0151 and an adversary review of the fix; every case below was confirmed in real bash and is in the differential test). `echo a#b; rm -rf ~` passed with any payload: shlex treated the mid-word `#` as a comment; comments are now cut only at the start of a word outside quotes (nothing is cut under `$'…'`). 22 forms changed or kept a variable differently from the guard's reading (`D[0]=~`, `D+=~`, `command export`, `builtin declare`, `! D=~`, `time D=~`, `$_`, a DEBUG trap, a space or `IFS`, `BASH_REMATCH`, a prefix assignment in POSIX mode, an alias, and quoted or `command`-prefixed words such as `"D=x"` that bash runs as commands). The resolver now reads each word as written, records only unquoted `NAME=literal` words of a command made of them, stops at any builtin outside an inert list (only builtins can change a variable) and never resolves names bash sets itself.
- Guard: `rm` resolves a variable set to a literal earlier in a straight-line command (T-0151, self-improvement pass 1): `D=<scratch path>; rm -rf "$D/x"` no longer needs a rewrite. Only simple commands joined by `;` or newlines count; a pipe, `&&`, `||`, `&`, a subshell, a group, a control keyword, or a command that can set variables otherwise (`read`, `source`, `eval`, `declare`…) leaves `$VAR` unknown and blocked, as before. The differential test checks the risky variants against real bash.
- Guard differential test (T-0160): `plugin/tests/test_guard_diff.py` runs about 50 shell forms of each dangerous payload (recursive home delete, force push, plugin install) in real bash inside bubblewrap with logging stand-ins, and fails on any dangerous call the guard didn't block. Its first run found commands run through `trap`, `find -exec`, `setsid`, `flock` and `script -c`, now all read. The self-improvement brief asks each pass for new forms.
- Guard: a shell keyword in front of a command no longer hides it (T-0159, found while tracing T-0151): `for f in a; do rm -rf ~; done`, `if …; then …`, `else …`, `{ …; }`, `! …` and `coproc …` were read as commands named `do`, `then`, `{`, so nothing after them was checked.
- Subagent calls are Foreman rows (T-0148, self-inbox from a live snapshot): `✓ ◆ fm-reviewer Review … · 7 tools · 1m 20s · 32k tokens` with the report's first line, or `◷ … · in the background`, instead of the engine's name on a highlighted block and its "Backgrounded agent" line (blanked under the row). The compact resume row also matches the prompt by its text, in case the origin doesn't say plugin.
- Guard: one quote-aware reader for command substitutions, and lines that a comment hid (T-0158, with an adversary review and two automated reviews): every `` `…` `` and `$( … )` a shell would run — nested, inside double quotes, in an unquoted heredoc, nested backticks unescaped, a `$((` that falls back to a subshell — is read as a command of its own, and each is also read to the end (a `case` pattern or a comment can close one early); single-quoted text, quoted heredocs and arithmetic aren't. A `#` comment now ends at its line: it used to hide every later line of the command. The fm-argument blanking (T-0153) applies only to Foreman's real `fm` (not any script named fm), and not with comments, PATH/alias/function changes or an unclosed quote. The old per-token regex (no nesting) is gone.
- Guard: an fm command's own arguments are data (T-0153, self-inbox): naming interpreters in a task's text beside a quoted plugin-test command blocked `fm task new` live; top-level fm commands are now blanked before the interpreter gate, except where their arguments hold a substitution or the quote scan can't follow (both read as before).
- Guard: with `$'…'` quoting in a command, every backtick span is checked (T-0157, from the automated security review): `$'\''` made the backtick scan think a live backtick was single-quoted; the scan now gives up there and reads every span (fails closed, as T-0144 does for `--run`).
- Guard backticks, corrected (T-0155, from the automated security review): T-0150's rewrite of backticks into separators hid backtick substitutions inside double quotes (`` echo "`rm -rf ~`" `` passed); now every `…` body outside single quotes is extracted by a quote-aware scan and checked as a command of its own. A backtick counts as code execution unless every interpreter in the command treats it as text (Python, Node, Deno, Bun), and versioned or alternative interpreter names (`ruby3.2`, `jruby`, `irb`, `php8.3`, `perl5.36`) are recognised.
- A self-improvement pass also waits 2 h after the last one (T-0154): the loop tuned itself after pass 2 came due 13 minutes after pass 1 with one new friction line.
- A passing criterion check verifies the step it ran in (T-0149, from self-improvement pass 1): running a criterion's exact verify command was recorded for the criterion only, so the Stop gate kept asking for step evidence (the largest friction count, 32 nudges); a failing run still verifies nothing.
- Guard backticks (T-0150, from self-improvement pass 1): a backtick counts as code execution only in ruby, perl and php, so markdown in a Python heredoc beside a quoted plugin-test command no longer blocks; a claude plugin/mcp/config call inside backticks is now caught there; and an unquoted shell backtick substitution (`` echo `claude plugin install …` ``) is split into its own command and checked (it went unread before, a gap found while checking this change).
- One drive wait is one `drive_wait` event (T-0152, from self-improvement pass 1): it was logged on every Stop while background work ran, so `fm friction` counted one long wait many times.
- Recursive self-improvement loop (T-0125): `fm friction` digests Foreman's own friction since the last pass (guard blocks with examples, failed tool calls, nudges and drive stops, slow gates, hook latency, the user's corrections and steers, lessons, and what became of the self-inbox, so a fix that didn't work shows up again); `fm friction --every N` makes `fm next` call for a pass every N closed tasks; `--brief` writes the brief for one read-only `foreman:fm-recon` subagent (≤5 grounded fixes, and whether the loop should change); findings go to the self-inbox and through the normal queue; `--mark` closes a pass.
- A turn that ends on purpose for a mod reload is no longer held by the evidence or question nudge (T-0147): it blocked twice in a row live; the resumed turn records the live evidence.
- Dashboard without duplicates (T-0146): with the pane shown the band keeps the task, what it waits on and what needs you (no today/queued/inbox row, next line or dashboard button); trust is a word beside the mode instead of a warning row; a finished subagent reads "done · N tools" (not its stale last action); a stopped brainstorm older than 2 h is one line (`fm ui --json` gives `age_h`); the queue's usual-time chip never splits; Files are project-relative with outside scratch files left out (edits record the real path), and Files and Inbox say how many more; the controls read as actions ("turn drive off", "switch to standard", "mute").
- A Foreman-styled turn (T-0143): the working line is Foreman's (animated mark, task and step, what the turn is doing, elapsed) instead of the engine's word; replies open with a Foreman mark that animates on the newest reply while the turn runs, and Foreman report lines (Changed:, ✓/✗, ⚑ Captured, ◆ Decided, Next:, ⚠ Needs you) are coloured like the rows (other text stays markdown); a finished shell row reads like the running one (`✓ ❯ Ran <description> · 42s · 2 lines`, the command under it dim, no `$`), and Claude Code's result block under it is replaced: no second copy of the output or the timeout; a command's file changes (Claude Code's own bash edit diff), a commit and an auto-background timeout are Foreman lines; diff text uses softer colours (signs keep the strong ones). `shot.py watch` snapshots this session's pane every few seconds.
- `fm task finish` closes a step whose evidence is already recorded (`fm task evidence --step`) instead of demanding `--run`, and when something truly lacks both a verify command and evidence, the error names it (`step 2 (live check)`) (T-0142).
- UI changes reach the running session during a drive (T-0145): Claude Code hot-reloads a mod only when a turn really ends, and the drive kept the turn going, so changes never showed. Now a driven turn that changed this session's `~/.claude/dev-mods/<session>/` (types Claude Code writes on load left out) ends once with a note, the Stop hook records `resume_after_reload`, and the reloaded foreman-ui says so in a toast and submits "Continue the Foreman drive" itself (once per record, only in its own session; the next prompt clears the record). A turn the drive ends to wait for background work now says what it waits on (the first live run sat idle behind a never-ending snapshot loop).
- Security (T-0144, from an automated review): the guard's interpreter claude-check left out every `--run "…"` match, heredoc and `-c` bodies included, so code could hide its own `claude plugin` call behind a fake `--run "` or hand it to the interpreter as a `--run` argument; now only a top-level `--run` argument of a real `fm` command is left out (heredoc bodies are always read), and `$'…'` quoting or a heredoc form the parser doesn't know (`<<\EOF`) leaves nothing out (both found by the review). foreman-ui rows drop every escape sequence (7- and 8-bit CSI, OSC, DCS/APC/PM/SOS strings, charset switches), C0/C1 controls, bidi overrides and zero-width or direction marks from shell output, the shown command, the quiet fm line and diff lines; a carriage return keeps what a terminal shows.
- Clean tool rows (T-0141): a finished Edit or Write is a Foreman row, `✓ ✎ Edited src/stats.py +1 −4`, then its changed lines as red/green text with the file's line numbers (no background blocks, unchanged context left out, at most 8 then `… N more changed lines`), and Claude Code's second copy of the diff under it is blanked; paths read relative to the project; a long shell command wraps under itself, not under the ✓; the band's and pane's task id never wraps when the title is long. Diff text drops control characters and bidi overrides. `shot.py self OUT.png [--back N]` snapshots the tmux pane this session runs in, and `shot.py start --model haiku` runs a cheap turn without changing the saved default model.
- Shell rows tell the truth (T-0140): the ✓/✗ mark and failures-first lines come from the exit status; a successful grep that lists `except Exception:` is ✓ with its tail, not a red ✗ (seen in a live screenshot).
- Drive survives a restart (T-0138): `claude --continue` in a project with drive on, full autonomy and open work (active, queued, or the inbox) starts its own first turn (SessionStart `initialUserMessage`: "Continue the Foreman drive: T-… — title"); a fresh start, standard autonomy, drive off or paused, `fm run`, another session active minutes ago, or only items waiting on the user starts none.
- Sizes with meaning (T-0116): `fm ui --json` adds `typical` (median minutes from first focus to done per type and size, three or more closed tasks) and the active task's `on_task_s`; the band shows `12m on it · usually ~25m`, queue rows their usual time, and the gates card what a full run costs.
- Drive trusts Claude Code's own list of in-flight background work (T-0115): when the Stop hook input carries `background_tasks`, drive waits exactly while it lists something and continues when it's empty; the event scan stays for builds that don't send it. A lost completion notice can no longer stall drive, and a command moved to the background with ctrl+b now holds it.
- A task is judged by its own changes (T-0078, T-0079): focusing a task snapshots the working files (a git tree, untracked included, no commit needed), and the security trigger, scope check, `fm audit prep` and `fm check --affected` diff from that snapshot instead of the last commit. Work in progress from before the task, or a never-committed repo, no longer makes every task need an adversary lens and scope notes for files it never touched, while its own edit to an already-dirty file still counts. When git can't produce the diff (an unreadable file, a timeout), done fails closed: the adversary lens is required, with the reason.
- Everything in the chat (T-0137): each finished shell command is a Foreman row: `✓ $ ls src  0.1s · 1 line`, then its output formatted inline (`│` gutter, failures first else the tail, errors red, warnings yellow, passes green, `└ … N more lines`); failed commands included. Claude Code's folded "Running 1 shell command…" groups unfold when they hold a command, so every command gets its own live row; reads and searches stay folded (Grep and Glob get quiet one-liners when shown). A chained or multi-line command shows up to three lines and `… +N more lines`, never reading as a plain one. The pane's Output card and the separate result summary are gone. Checked in the live render.
- Brainstorms you can watch (T-0124): `fm ideas` writes a status file; `fm ui --json` reports the newest brainstorm (answers in so far and ideas found while it runs; the count and first ideas after); the running `fm ideas` call's row in the chat shows `✦ 3/12 answers · 41 ideas so far · <newest idea>`, and the pane has a Brainstorm card. ` 0 fail` no longer reads as a failure in output summaries (seen in the live terminal).
- Shell output as a summary (T-0123): a finished Bash call's output panel becomes `✓ 32 lines · the last 6` with the lines that matter (failures first, else the tail) in their colours (errors red, warnings yellow, passes green) and `… N more · /fm → Output`; the pane's new Output card shows the last command's last 40 lines, formatted the same way. Errors keep Claude Code's full text; a backgrounded call says so in one line.
- `fm task finish … --commit "<message>"` (T-0129): commits only the files this task changed (from its focus snapshot) and only after the close succeeded, so a refused close can't be committed by a piped `finish | tail && git commit` (it happened twice in one session). A small task's `--audit` now always counts as its self audit, extra `--lens` or not. Lesson notes on edits come once per file per session and never for a task closed in the same session (T-0127). The guard's check for interpreter code running `claude plugin …` no longer reads fm's `--run "<cmd>"` arguments as interpreter code; those are checked as commands of their own, interpreter checks included (T-0128; a first, narrower version failed open on piped code and was replaced after review, T-0135).
- Faster gates (T-0133): `plugin/tests/run.py` runs the suite one test class per core: 154 s → 18 s for 537 tests (stable over four runs), and the whole `fm check` 176 s → 41 s. Every task close used to wait three minutes on it.
- foreman-ui look, from reviewing the live render (T-0122, T-0131): types are coloured words (`FEATURE medium`), not highlighted blocks; every control is a quiet `key: label` (`n: next  d: drive off  r: refresh`, `▸ start ✕ drop`, `✓ approve this plan`) instead of `[ boxed ]` buttons; beside a docked pane the band switches to single lines that cut at the end instead of wrapping letter by letter; the pane and band show the human part of what's next, not the agent's instructions; the header reads `Foreman · project`, then the mode in words (`full auto · drive on · trust on`); spinners use Claude Code's own star glyphs; the footer no longer doubles its separator, and the `full auto`/`drive off` mode pills are gone (the band says it). `mods/foreman-ui/tools/shot.py` runs Claude Code in tmux with the dev mod and draws the screen to a PNG for review.
- `/fm-trust on|off` (T-0120): a switch only you can flip that lets Claude edit the guard and Claude Code settings too (never Foreman state). The foreman-ui mod accepts it only from your own prompt or your phone (not a scheduled prompt, a skill or another plugin) and writes the trust record (`state/trust.json`) itself: Foreman state is never writable by a tool call, and fm has no `trust on`, so there is no command for Claude to forge (an earlier env-marker design was replaced after review found quoting tricks around it). `fm trust` shows it and records it in the ledger; `fm trust off` removes it. The band warns while trust is on; the session-start note and `fm ui --json` (`mode.trust`, `mode.standing`) show it.
- A standing yes for Foreman's own code (T-0119): `fm ask ID core --standing` raises one permission prompt saying it covers every later task in the project; your yes lets core edits to Foreman's code, rules, evals and spec through without a yes per task, until `fm standing off`. Claude Code's settings, Foreman state, the guard file itself and whole-tree writes that include it still ask each time; the grant and its revocation are in the ledger, and the session-start note says when one is on. Only the dialog can grant it (an agent can't).
- foreman-ui 0.5.0 (T-0109): background shells are visible — the band shows `◷ waiting on N background shells` with the command and its time, the pane lists them, and each leaves on its notification or a stop (ctrl+b-backgrounded commands included); the mascot is now Claude Code's own welcome-screen Claude in block glyphs, dancing while Claude works, blinking idle, cheering on a closed task, with a mini Claude in a random-looking (stable) color per running subagent; the band tallies what closed since your last message (`✔ since your last message: 3 done · T-0115 T-0116 T-0117`), even after the queue runs dry, and clears when you write (T-0117). A subagent that ended without telling the mod (killed, a compaction, a reload) no longer reads as running for hours: each poll asks Claude Code's own agent list, and a running subagent that has gone quiet shows for how long, with a Stop button after 10 minutes (T-0121).
- foreman-ui 0.4.0 (T-0108: T-0100 T-0102 T-0103 T-0105 T-0106 T-0107): an animated pixel Claude in the pane's top-right corner (`mascot`: blue/orange/purple/green/off; works while Claude works, blinks idle, jumps on a closed task); sizes as words everywhere (`FIX · small`, statusline too) with a legend; the band shows today's done, queued and inbox counts with a bar and what's next; a plain-words note under every `fm ask` permission prompt; finished reads and background notifications fold to one line. `fm ui --json` adds `today_done` (tasks closed today, from the ledger).
- Fresh long sessions (T-0098): when a task closes with context at or above `freshAt` (foreman-ui setting, default 40%, 0 = off), the mod compacts at that boundary with instructions that keep standing requests and live decisions and drop finished tasks' files and logs; Foreman's checkpoint and resume note restore the exact point. foreman-ui gains `userConfig` (`freshAt`, `mascot`).
- Cheaper help (T-0101): `foreman:fm-scout`, a Haiku read-only lookup agent (file:line hits only) for the many small "where is X" questions; six new on-demand playbooks (terminal/mod UI, flaky tests, release and changelog, dependency upgrades, profiling, API design) at zero always-on cost; the playbook index trimmed under its budget. Long logs stay out of context with `fm quiet`.
- Bigger, wilder brainstorms (T-0099): default lenses add `unspoken needs` (the need behind what the user keeps asking and pushing back on) and `delight`; every pack carries the user's own recent requests and corrections; lenses return 8–12 ideas with at least 3 wild ones, each tagged by category; `fm ideas --deepen K` runs a yes-and round on the K biggest categories.
- Faster close-out (T-0097, measured over 287 real tasks: median 18 bookkeeping calls a task, close-out 42 of 125 hours): `fm task finish` closes any tier in one call (`--lens '<lens>: <result>'` per audit, `--docs`, `--lesson`), the rules and `/foreman:next` point at it, and drive in full autonomy also works captured inbox requests instead of stopping on an empty queue.
- Drive no longer stalls after background work (T-0096): a completion notice that lands inside a running turn never reached the prompt hook, so finished commands looked like they were still running and drive stayed silent for up to 6 hours — the Stop hook now reads new completion notices from the transcript, a stopped task (TaskStop) counts as finished, the stale window is 2 hours, and each wait is logged as `drive_wait`.
- foreman-ui 0.3.0 (T-0095, from the round-2 brainstorm and your steers): every card animated alike (breathing borders, true-color bars, recency fades) — queue step bars, inbox age, quick-capture box, live subagents, files touched with +/− bars, last `fm check` gates, hook-latency sparkline; a context meter that checkpoints once at 85%; `/foreman:next` as ghost text when nothing waits on you; footer hint and mode labels; chimes with a Sound toggle. Less clutter: successful `fm` commands fold to one quiet line, the reply format is quiet while working (no task header, step headers or per-command lines; at most three proof lines), and replies carry no task badge any more (the statusline and band show the task; the badge went stale). `fm ui --json` adds queue progress, inbox age, a latency series and the last check run.
- A polished, animated UI (T-0092): foreman-ui 0.2.0 draws the band as a card with a true-color gradient step bar that animates while Claude works, the pane as cards (stage stepper, steps, criteria, audits meter, plans awaiting approval, queue and inbox with type chips, activity and health), every running tool call as an animated row (edits with `+N −M`, commands, reads, searches, web, subagents with their tool count and latest call; finished rows stay Claude Code's own), the working spinner with the Foreman step, and a per-turn summary on the closing line. The statusline is one clean true-color session line plus a Foreman line (a chip when the mod is on), read from a new `status.json`; the old stack is opt-in (`statusline_layout: "stack"`).
- Round I core (T-0088, from the T-0082 brainstorm): `fm ui --json`, one view-model snapshot (active task with steps, criteria, audits and blockers; next action; queue with what waits on you; inbox; pending yeses; recently closed; recent events; hook health; paths to watch) for the mod and any surface, `{"v": 1, "project": null}` outside a project; `fm task show --json` carries steps, criteria (with verify commands) and depends (T-0076); `fm task new --interpretation … --approach …` plans an M/L brief in one call (T-0083); `fm task set --section Approach` fills the template's long heading, and rewriting Acceptance criteria keeps the ticks of unchanged criteria (T-0080, T-0075); `sensitive()` ignores patterns inside string literals (T-0074); timeouts read `exit 124 (timed out after N s)` in evidence and `fm quiet` (T-0081).
- Foreman inside Claude Code (T-0084, from the T-0082 brainstorm): the `foreman-ui` mod (second plugin in the marketplace; install.sh installs it, uninstall removes it) draws a band above the prompt, a dashboard pane (`/fm`, docked on wide fullscreen terminals), toasts for finished steps and tasks, needed yeses and guard refusals, and one-press Next, Approve (plan), Drive, Autonomy, inbox Start/Drop. A pure renderer over `fm ui --json`.
- Recursion 4 probe (T-0071 round H; the super brainstorm then went dry: 3 of 24 new ideas survived grounding): `fm check` labels failures whose cause is the environment (missing module or tool, network, permissions, disk); edits of generated or vendored files get a one-time note and a pre-audit finding; `fm audit scan [--base REV]` runs the mechanical pre-audit over any branch diff (default: since the main branch), no task needed.
- Recursion 3 (T-0071 round G): a green run in which no tests ran counts as a failure; the pre-audit flags weakened tests (assertions or test files removed, skip/only/xfail added), removed public names that are still referenced, and files that usually change together (git history) with one left out; `fm map` lists the CI workflow's check commands; one-shot notes re-arm after compaction; an edit that leaves a Python or JSON file unparsable gets a note at once; git never prompts; `fm check` with no gates suggests the project's own, and `--fail-fast` stops at the first failure; `fm pr ID` prints a pull-request description from the brief; `fm cost` shows the context each session starts from.
- Recursion 2 (T-0071 round F, from a second super brainstorm, plus the D+E review): files changed through the shell or outside Claude (git since the task started) count toward Files touched and scope drift; cached gate passes expire after 12 hours; `fm check` names a gate that changes the working tree; `fm why FILE[:LINE]` traces code to its commits and Foreman tasks with their lessons; a bare "status" prompt is answered by the hook from Foreman's state at zero model tokens; manifests and lockfiles count as security-sensitive (adversary lens); a brainstorm lens that returns no ideas counts as failed and `fm ideas --seen ideas.md` carries earlier runs forward; `fm cost` says when transcripts hold no usage; audit text is marked as data like evidence; the repeated-failure note fires once per task and failure; the weekly digest check is throttled; the shared lessons file is capped; one verify-command parser.
- Unattended work (T-0071 round E): `fm run --models S=sonnet,L=opus [--save]` picks a model per tier and logs each session's tokens (T-0053); `fm notify "<cmd>"` gets the message as $1 when fm run finishes a task, blocks or stops; `fm evals add ID` turns a blocked or failed task into a plugin eval case (T-0052); `fm share on` shares this project's lessons, privacy-filtered, and recalls other projects' (T-0054); instruction-like text quoted into evidence, research, recall and tripwires is marked as data (T-0058); `fm digest` shows the week and SessionStart offers it once a week; `fm usage` reports how often the injected Next was followed (T-0051); meta.json carries a schema number that fm doctor checks.
- Faster and cheaper (T-0071 round D): `fm task finish ID --run CMD --audit HOW` closes an S task in one call (runs each open check, marks what passed, self audit, done); `fm quiet -- CMD` prints one line on success and the tail on failure (auto-evidence sees through it); `fm gates [TYPE TIER]` lists what done will require and focus prints a one-line summary; a file edited 6 times without a check, or the same failure 3 times in a task, gets one "stop and diagnose" note; `fm cost` reads the transcripts for tokens by task, session and tool (T-0064); `fm usage` lists the skills, playbooks and fm commands used and never used (T-0056); `fm ideas` reports new ideas per lens and warns about big packs; `fm doctor` caps each SKILL.md at 10,000 chars; the rules point at `fm outline`, `fm quiet`, `fm gates` and `fm task finish`.
- Memory at the right moment (T-0071 round C): recall adds "start here" (files the nearest finished task touched), the step count of similar finished tasks (T-0050), at most one matching playbook, and weighs old history less; editing a file a finished task touched and left a lesson about shows that lesson once per session (tripwires.json, rebuilt at done); `fm decide --kind costly|outward` marks decisions for the user's review (`--review`) and `--reverses` links a reversal (T-0057); checkpoints carry "ruled out:" log lines and the failures the task met into the resume note; corrections from the user are logged (`fm recall --corrections`, used by reflect); focus warns when files in scope changed since the brief was planned.
- Verification strength (T-0071 round B): `fm task prove ID --run CMD` runs a test on the start tree with only the task's test files (must fail) and on the current tree (must pass) and records both (T-0059); PERFORMANCE tasks need before/after numbers and CLEAN tasks a test run before their first edit; `fm task done` prints and stores a verification grade and records the files the task touched; `fm sentinel` re-runs the checks recent finished tasks passed (side-effect commands skipped); `fm check` names gates that got much slower and keys cached passes to the environment too; timed-out checks are killed with their whole process group and get no stdin.
- Gates from the diff (T-0071 round A): `fm task done` asks for a reason for edits outside the brief's scope (T-0055) and for the adversary lens whatever the tier when the change touches auth, crypto, secrets, exec or deserialization (T-0049); verify commands are linted when written (not on PATH, can't fail, exit status hidden by a pipe; T-0066); `fm outline PATH` lists a file's definitions with line ranges and a whole Read of a 600+ line file gets a one-time pointer to it (T-0067); `fm audit prep` puts mechanical findings (debug leftovers, conflict markers, new TODOs, secret-looking values, source without tests, big files, sensitive code) at the top of the review brief (T-0068).
- Super brainstorm (T-0071): `fm ideas --rounds N` runs rounds that each see every idea so far and add only new ones (gaps, second-order improvements, combinations), stopping when a round adds fewer than `--dry` (default 3), with a deduplicated `ideas.md` index; "fully featured"-style requests are pointed at it, and the brainstorm skill's super mode builds every grounded idea and re-brainstorms until dry. `fm task ac add` takes `criterion :: verify` too.
- Auto-evidence (R6): running a criterion's exact verify command records its real result as evidence; no second run through fm.
- Faster and fewer tokens (round 5): `fm check` reuses a pass on the identical tree and `--affected` runs only linked tests while iterating; the review brief goes to a file instead of the conversation; the per-turn state note is sent only when it changed.
- Project map (T-0044): `fm map` (gates, layout, entry points, hot files, test links; rebuilt when HEAD moves), `fm impact PATH`, likely tests for the scope at focus; intake grounds from map, impact and recall first.
- Verification (T-0045..T-0047): FIX tasks need red→green proof (or a stated reason); `fm check` times gates, reruns a failure once (flaky) and labels failures that predate the task; a recurring failure points to the finished task that met it, with its lesson.
- Recall and lessons (T-0043, T-0048): `fm focus` records and prints the project's related past work (similar briefs with outcome and lesson, decisions, research) with a tier hint; `fm recall`; M/L tasks close with `fm task done --lesson`.
- Leaner and cheaper: `fm audit prep` prints one combined brief, so an L review is one reviewer pass instead of five subagents (past-finding seeding reads combined reviews by section); `fm ideas` runs four lenses by default; the always-on rules shrank from 7.6k to 4.9k characters (doctor caps them at 6,000) and ask for economical reads and short replies (T-0060..T-0062).
- `fm task new --ac "<done when> :: <verify cmd>"` attaches the verify command, so an M/L task made in one command can be focused (T-0042); `git fetch` no longer counts as a tree write unless it may move HEAD (`--update-head-ok`) (T-0040).
- `setup-plugins.sh` refuses a real run inside a Claude Code session (`CLAUDECODE=1`): its installs would skip the user's yes; `--dry-run` still works, and a terminal run is unchanged (T-0039).
- Plugin approvals are pinned (T-0036): `fm ask ID plugin --pin <plugin id>` binds the yes to that plugin and a hash of its content; `fm plugins install|enable` and `claude plugin install|enable` spend it only for that plugin, unchanged, within 24 h, and otherwise say which `fm ask` to run again. The dialog names the plugin; pins never travel through `fm sync`.
- The Stop evidence gate reads a completion word under a finished step's `▸ Step N/M` header as about that step, however far below the header it is (T-0038).
- Round 10 (final audit): a whole skill, agent or command folder copied, moved, linked or extracted into place needs the user's `plugin` yes like a single new file; `fm sync` never reads a FIFO or device node from the mirror; `fm doctor`'s core integrity check works in a git worktree and budgets the always-on rules by characters too (≤ 9000, now 7.5k), not only lines; a question bounced back by the Stop hook in a session without AskUserQuestion (`claude -p`) is decided and recorded, and the final message keeps the plan.
- Round 9: `fm audit prep` seeds each lens brief with this project's past findings for that lens (up to six CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM headlines from reviews saved as `<task>-<lens>`, newest first, raw transcripts included), so a reviewer starts from the weak spots earlier reviews found.
- Round 8: `fm run` waits out a usage limit instead of stopping: Claude Code's limit message triggers a logged wait (5 min doubling to 1 h) and a retry of the same task, up to `--wait` hours in total (default 6; `0` stops at once as before).
- Round 7: `fm doctor` warns when protected core differs from the last commit (a half-applied edit or an unreviewed change) and budgets skill/agent/command descriptions (≤ 6000 chars, now 4.2k); the two large follow-ups (headless audits, eval capture) are deferred with revisit triggers.
- Round 7: no self-lockout from a broken library (T-0037): the hook script falls back to the committed library when the working copy can't be imported, and reads the active task with the committed `fmcore` when the working one fails; `fmguard` is self-contained again.
- Round 6: hooks p50 ≈ 29 ms (was ≈ 41): `traceback` loads only on errors and `fmcore.record` replaces `@dataclass` on the hook path; sync hashing measured at 3 ms for 300 briefs, so its stat cache was dropped.
- Round 5: `fm research add --from-agent FILE` keeps a subagent's final report; `fm audit prep --note`; `fm task drop ID --done-in HOST` for never-started requests another task did; the progress line names the current step; `fm doctor` names the latest hook error. Round-4 audit fixes: checkouts, extractions and recursive copies over a folder that contains protected paths need that category (a checkout at Foreman's root can't swap the guard); `core.hooksPath` in every spelling, `direnv allow` and more startup paths are `system`; titles are control-free wherever they're read; pulled briefs are sanitized; the guard fallback only trusts Foreman's own repo and ignores `GIT_*` variables.
- Round 4: the guard falls back to the committed `fmguard.py` when the working copy fails (no more self-lockout); persistence (shell startup files, autostart, git hooks and `core.hooksPath`, `crontab`, `at`) is `system`; titles and dashboard text carry no terminal control sequences; `fm sync` redacts secrets on export and skips imports over 1 MB.
- `fm sync`: opt-in mirror of briefs, decisions and research in the repo (`.foreman/`), imported by other clones at session start; grants never travel, exports don't stale audits, hand edits are `state-direct`.
- `fm repeats`: commands and procedures a project keeps repeating, from its briefs, with what to turn each into (a gate, a script, a project skill or agent); `fm tidy` counts them; `fm repeats dismiss` retires handled ones; the end-of-task procedure turns them into project tools, and creating a new skill, agent or command file needs the user's yes (guard category `plugin`: always-on context).
- `fm task evidence --run "<cmd>"` records the command's real exit code and output (failed runs block marking the step done); `fm check` runs the project's gates together and fails on any failure; `fm audit prep ID` freezes the task's diff and prints the lens briefs; a `plugin` yes covers one change; `fm next`/statusline audit state matches the done gate after evidence-only updates; the Stop evidence gate ignores claims about other steps or tasks; `fm docs` skips ignored markdown.
- Plugins: `fm plugins find <need>` (local marketplace search with an always-on estimate), `fm plugins check` (Stop hooks, duplicate MCP servers, skills duplicating Foreman; in `fm doctor`), and `fm plugins install|enable|disable` after the user's yes (new user-only guard category `plugin`, which also covers `claude plugin …`, `claude mcp add/remove` and `claude config set`); fresh installs are removed by uninstall-user. `fm plugins add-marketplace` (same yes; removed by uninstall-user) and `fm plugins forget`; `check` reports the curated conflicts shared with `setup-plugins.sh`, reads block-scalar descriptions and `plugin.json` hooks/MCP, and honors project-scope `enabledPlugins`. The guard also catches `claude` plugin commands behind global options, `/plugin install`-style prompts (argv, stdin, heredoc), `--settings`/`--mcp-config`/`--plugin-dir` sessions, the same commands run from interpreter code (Python or Node argv forms), `getattr`/`__import__` calls into Foreman's modules, and writes under `~/.claude/plugins` (including `git pull`/`checkout` there, via `-C`, `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` or `GIT_WORK_TREE`); `npx @anthropic-ai/claude-code` counts as `claude`. Only synchronous Stop hooks count as conflicts (`async`/`asyncRewake` ones can't hold a turn open). uninstall-user keeps the manifest entries it couldn't remove, so a later run retries them.
- Drive waits while this session's background agents or commands run (their completion notification wakes the session) instead of pushing busywork; background work older than 6 h is assumed lost. Audits recorded against the current worktree id stay fresh when only evidence is added afterwards.
- Approvals and questions are Claude Code prompts, not chat: `fm ask` raises the native permission prompt (what it grants is in the prompt); a `PermissionRequest` hook confirms the dialog was shown and PostToolUse of that same call grants, so chat messages no longer cancel pending requests. Questions use AskUserQuestion: a question left in reply text is sent back once to become a prompt. The dialog text can't be disguised (control/bidi characters stripped), unknown options or categories are refused, and under `fm run` fm ask is refused (the task is blocked instead). Chat "yes" remains the fallback where a dialog can't be tied to the call; if a dialog was approved before `/reload-plugins` loaded the new hook, fm ask says so. The task id, why and title are shown without control, format or separator characters, and `fm ask -p …` is refused. `fm doctor` checks that hooks.json registers every handled event. After updating Foreman, run `/reload-plugins`.
- Conversation view: `foreman:Foreman` output style (selected by install-user when you have none), badge with stage (`T-0015 FEATURE · executing 3/5`), a second statusline line with a progress bar, audits, queue, autonomy and exactly what a yes grants, and `fm watch` with a stage timeline, a Recent feed (evidence, audits, captures, decisions, approvals) and "Waiting on you".
- Headless: `fm serve` runs Claude Code Remote Control in a repo as a systemd user unit (restart with a crash-loop breaker, survives logout and reboot, output discarded) with the project in full autonomy + drive; `fm serve status|stop [--all]`, uninstall stops every unit. Starting it needs the user's yes (new user-only guard category `remote`); it turns on linger, finds `claude` on the saved PATH at each start, and refuses untrusted repos or a running `fm run`; `fm doctor` warns on a dead unit. `~/.claude.json` is now protected core and user unit files count as `system`. `fm run` works the queue in fresh `claude -p` sessions, one task each (drive scoped by `FOREMAN_DRIVE_TASK`), skipping tasks waiting on you and stopping on no progress, a failed session or the timeout.

## 1.1.0 — 2026-09-26
Claude works autonomously and checks its own work.
- You never run commands: consent is a yes/no question. `fm ask ID CAT…` records a one-shot request that only your next reply can grant (starts with yes) or cancel; pasted text, other sessions and requests older than 24 h never grant. `core` can no longer be granted from the command line at all.
- Audits gate `fm task done`: S self-check, M `intent` + one more lens, L all five (`intent`, `adversary`, `edge`, `operator`, `maintainer`), each with its own prompt and context slice (`skills/intake/references/audit.md`), recorded after the last edit (`fm task audit`). FINAL VERIFY adds a session-wide operator + adversary audit.
- Full autonomy (`fm autonomy full`, or `FULL AUTO`): no questions mid-run; decisions recorded with `fm decide`; what only you can grant is collected for one summary at the end.
- `/foreman:brainstorm` for open-ended requests (detected by the prompt hook): context pack, `fm ideas` runs one tool-less `claude -p` per lens in parallel (a subagent can't be tool-less), then grounding, scoring and a queued slate.
- Harness-enforced procedure: stages derived from briefs, `fm next`, the next action injected every turn (a hint; the gates below are enforced), `fm focus` plan gate, no edits without an active task, one-command S tasks (`fm task new … --ac … --step … --focus`).
- Context rot and doc drift: M/L tasks record their Docs impact before done ("documenting" stage); `fm docs` reports markdown that drifted from the repo (tidy includes it); `fm doctor` checks Foreman's own docs against its code; auto-compaction at 70% (`CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE`, reversible) and a context note at task boundaries.
- Status, doctor, tidy and improve can be started by Claude; plain words map to Foreman actions.
- Protected core widened to all Foreman code (`plugin/lib`, `bin`, `hooks`); interpreter-code writes to protected paths are caught.
- Approval hardening from the audits: `fm ask` is bound to the session the PreToolUse hook saw running it (not the agent's environment), requests without a trusted session are refused, a negation right after the yes cancels it ("ok, don't…"), and a task's tier can't be lowered once it has evidence.
- Audit freshness also compares a content id of the working tree (edits made any way, Bash included, make audits stale).
- State falls back to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/foreman` (or a per-user temp dir) when `~/.claude` is read-only, as in `claude plugin eval`; `fm doctor` warns and `uninstall.sh` purges whichever is in use. Eval prompts no longer break on a `---` in the rules.
- Final audit wave: a fallback-state marker can't redirect Foreman's state any more (the guard protects every fallback location and now checks archive extraction, clones, `-t` target dirs and `wget -P` by where they write; markers in dirs others can write are ignored; session start names a fallback in use), `fm doctor --restore-state` moves state back, doctor checks the env values; docs named in Docs impact must exist and show no drift before done, and drift elsewhere is reported at done; background-agent notifications no longer act as your words (approvals, override words, plan-only holds); plan-only detection ignores constraints such as "don't change the API"; a `?` in a code block doesn't stop drive; worktree ids reuse git's stat cache (fast on big repos); one Foreman-module rule in the guard; named lock waits and a shared `fm ask` TTL.
- Fixes found by the audits: `fm` rejects abbreviated options (`--allo core`); session ids come from `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` (the env-file export is stale after a resume); `ledger_tail` honours n beyond 64 KB; a busy lock no longer drops your yes silently; uninstall on a machine without `settings.json` leaves nothing behind.

## 1.0.0 — 2026-09-26
First full build from `BUILD_PROMPT.md` (branch `foreman/build`).
- `fm` state CLI (Python stdlib): projects, briefs with evidence-gated steps/criteria/done, intake parser, canonical queue with dependency ordering, ledger, checkpoints, decisions, research notes, sensitive repos, drive mode, tidy, doctor, watch, install/uninstall wiring.
- Hooks: one dispatcher for 13 events; fail-closed guard (8 categories + self-authorize), Stop evidence gate + drive, factual SessionStart/UserPromptSubmit injection, async timeline, reply badge, terminal notifications.
- Skills, rules (51 lines at 1.0.0), read-only agents; ECC and superpowers procedures ported with MIT attribution.
- Visibility: statusline wrapper (original + claude-hud + Foreman line), subagent rows, `fm watch`, theme, OTel/Grafana snippet.
- `install.sh`: installs plugin-dev only with `--build`; `--build` starts Claude Code only when a terminal can actually be opened.
- Verification: 170+ unit/behaviour tests, hook bench (p95 ≤ 38 ms), live `claude -p` scenarios, install round trip, `claude plugin eval` suite.

## 0.1.0
Bootstrap: `BUILD_PROMPT.md`, `install.sh`, `setup-plugins.sh`, `configure-repo.sh`, `reset-claude.sh`, `/foreman:build`.
